Part I — Lecture 8 (pehla hissa)

Boolean Classification — Loss Functions

Ye Part OHT-II ka Q-4 hai, aur poore paper ka sab se zyada aane wala hissa. Lecture-7 ne kaha tha "better loss functions later" — ye wohi "later" hai. Yahan 6 mukhtalif loss functions aate hain. Darne ki zaroorat nahi: inhein alag alag rata lagane ke bajaye, ek hi framework (I1) samajh lein, aur baaqi sab usi se nikal aayenge. I9 ka comparison table poore Part ka khulasa hai — kam waqt ho to seedha wahan jayein.

I1Penalty function p(·) ka framework

Setup wahi hai jo H15 mein tha:

Do classes ko embed karein: y = ±1 RERM se classifier fit karein (mukhtalif losses aur regularizers ke saath) Test data par NEYMAN-PEARSON metric se validate karein: (κ fn + fp) / n jahan κ = positive example ko ghalat batane (false negative) ki nisbati saza (relative penalty) κ = 1 → NP metric bilkul overall ERROR RATE ban jaata hai

Ab asal nuktah. Kyunki v sirf −1 ya +1 ho sakti hai, is liye loss function define karne ke liye hamein sirf do functions chahiye:

ℓ(y-hat, -1) = jab asli label -1 tha, to y-hat kehna kitna bura hai ℓ(y-hat, +1) = jab asli label +1 tha, to y-hat kehna kitna bura hai

Aur yahan lecture ki sab se achi chaal aati hai: ye do functions alag alag nahi banaye jaate. Ek hi penalty function p se dono nikal aate hain:

Master formula — poora Lecture-8 isi par khada hai

ℓ(y-hat, -1) = p( y-hat ) ℓ(y-hat, +1) = κ · p( -y-hat ) jahan p : R → R PENALTY FUNCTION hai

Yaani: aap ko sirf p yaad rakhna hai. Negative class ke liye p(y-hat), aur positive class ke liye wohi p magar palat kar (−y-hat) aur κ se multiply kar ke. Chhe losses = chhe p. Bas.

Ye palatne (−y-hat) ka kya matlab hai? Poori baat symmetry ki hai:

  • Negative class (v = −1) ke liye: hum chahte hain y-hat negative ho. To saza y-hat barhne par barhni chahiye — yaani p(y-hat).
  • Positive class (v = +1) ke liye: hum chahte hain y-hat positive ho. Ye bilkul ulti cheez hai. To agar y-hat ko palat dein (−y-hat), to masla wapas pehli surat jaisa ban jaata hai — aur wohi p kaam kar jaata hai.

p kaisa hona chahiye? Slide ki shart (negative class ke nuqta-e-nazar se):

p(y-hat) CHHOTA hona chahiye jab y-hat NEGATIVE ho (kyunki negative class ke liye negative prediction theek hai) p(y-hat) BADA hona chahiye jab y-hat POSITIVE ho (kyunki negative class ke liye positive prediction GHALAT hai)

Aur κ ka kaam: slide kehti hai κ "controls the relative cost (or importance) of misclassifying the positive class", aur "larger values of κ impose a higher penalty on false negatives".

Ek hi cheez — teen zubaanon mein

Ye teeno jumle ek hi baat keh rahe hain, aur paper mein kisi bhi shakal mein aa sakte hain:

  • "κ false negatives par zyada saza lagata hai"
  • "κ positive class ke misclassification ki cost barhata hai"
  • "κ positive representative se hatne par zyada weight deta hai"

I2Acha loss function kaisa hona chahiye

Chhe losses ko muqabla karne se pehle, ye tay kar lein ke hum kis cheez par muqabla kar rahe hain. Char khoobiyan matloob hain, aur koi bhi loss chaaron poori nahi karta — yehi poore lecture ka asli nuktah hai:

KhoobiMatlabKyun chahiye
1. Sahi shakal
(monotone)		Margin barhne par loss kabhi barhe na — yaani jitna zyada theek, utna kam lossWarna model ko "zyada theek" hone par saza milegi, jo bilkul ulta hai
2. ConvexPyaali (bowl) ki shakal — koi local minimum ka jaal nahiTo RERM ko efficiently hal kiya ja sake — jawab milne ki zamanat
3. DifferentiableHar jagah derivative mojood ho — koi moad (kink) nahiGradient-based optimization smooth chalti hai
4. NP loss ke qareebJo cheez hum waqai naapte hain (error rate), loss us jaisa hoWarna hum ek cheez minimize kar rahe hain aur naap doosri rahe hain

Ab slide ka buniyadi jumla, jo bilkul ulta lagta hai magar poore lecture ki jaan hai:

Lecture-8 ka asal paigham

"Surprisingly, we get better performance using different loss functions, that are also easier to minimize."

Yaani: wo loss jo hamari asli metric (error rate) ke bilkul barabar hai (Neyman-Pearson loss), use karna bura hai. Aur jo loss us se mukhtalif hai (hinge, logistic), wo behtar nateeja deta hai — aur minimize karna bhi aasaan hai. Dono faide. Agar paper mein poocha jaaye "why not just minimize the error rate directly?", to poora jawab yahan hai, aur tafseel I4 mein.

Wajah do lafzon mein: NP loss flat (chapta) hai — uska derivative taqreeban har jagah zero hai, to optimizer ko koi rasta nahi milta ("kis taraf jaoon?"). Proxy losses ek dhalaan (slope) dete hain jo optimizer ko sahi taraf le jaati hai — aur raaste mein model ko ye bhi sikhaati hai ke "zyada yaqeen se theek hona" behtar hai, sirf "theek hona" nahi. Yehi baat generalization behtar kar deti hai.

I3Square loss aur uski kharabi

Ye seedha OHT-II ka Q-4 part 3 hai. Formula:

p(u) = (1 + u)² ℓ(y-hat, -1) = (1 + y-hat)² ℓ(y-hat, +1) = κ · (1 - y-hat)² [ kyunki p(-y-hat) = (1 - y-hat)² ]

Pehli nazar mein theek lagta hai: jab y-hat = −1 ho (negative class ke liye bilkul sahi), to loss (1−1)² = 0. Perfect.

Magar masla tab hota hai jab prediction "bohat zyada" sahi ho. Slide ki apni misaal:

ℓ(-3.0, -1) = (1 + (-3.0))² = (-2.0)² = 4.0

Ye bilkul bemani hai. Dekhein kyun:

  • Asli class −1 hai (negative).
  • Model ne kaha −3.0 — yaani wo bohat poore yaqeen se keh raha hai "ye negative hai".
  • Prediction bilkul theek hai, aur bohat aitmaad ke saath theek hai — yaani sab se behtareen surat.
  • Phir bhi loss 4.0 hai — yaani model ko theek hone par saza di ja rahi hai!
Muqabla karein: y-hat = -1.0 → loss = 0.0 (theek) y-hat = -3.0 → loss = 4.0 (zyada theek, magar ZYADA SAZA!) y-hat = +1.0 → loss = 4.0 (BILKUL GHALAT — magar WOHI saza!) y-hat = -3.0 (bohat yaqeen se SAHI) aur y-hat = +1.0 (BILKUL GHALAT) ko square loss BARABAR bura samajhta hai.

Q-4 part 3 ka mukammal jawab — isay yaad kar lein

"Square loss is not monotonically decreasing in the margin. It penalises any deviation from the representative in both directions, so a confident correct prediction y-hat = −3.0 for v = −1 incurs loss (1−3)² = 4.0 — the same as the clearly wrong prediction y-hat = +1. During ERM this pushes correct, confident predictions back toward the boundary, wasting model capacity and distorting the decision boundary. A good classification loss must be non-increasing in the margin v · y-hat — flat (or zero) once the point is correctly classified with enough margin, as in hinge or logistic loss."

Phir square loss kyun padhte hain? Kyunki iska ek bada faida hai, aur slide usay saaf likhti hai: "ERM with this loss reduces to a least-squares optimization problem, making it computationally easy to solve." Yaani ye tez aur aasaan hai (closed-form hal mil jaata hai) — magar classification ke liye ghalat hai. Ye trade-off hi asal jawab hai.

Asaan misaal — square loss ka maslak

Ek ustad socha jaaye jo kehta hai "sahi jawab 50 marks hai". Ek bachche ne 50 likha — poore marks. Doosre ne 90 likha (yaani bilkul sahi raste par, aur bohat aage) — magar ustad usay bhi ghalat keh deta hai, kyunki 50 nahi likha. Square loss wohi ustad hai — usay "bilkul theek nishane" se chahiye, "theek taraf" se kaam nahi chalta. Classification mein hamein sirf taraf chahiye, nishana nahi — is liye ye ustad galat jagah par hai.

I4Neyman-Pearson (0-1) loss

Agar error rate ko sidha minimize karna hai, to loss ko bilkul error rate jaisa bana dein. Yehi NP loss hai:

┌ 1, jab u ≥ 0 p(u) = │ └ 0, jab u < 0 ℓ(y-hat, -1) = p(y-hat) → 1 agar y-hat ≥ 0, warna 0 ℓ(y-hat, +1) = κ · p(-y-hat) → κ agar y-hat ≤ 0, warna 0

Yaani: ghalti par 1 (ya κ), theek par 0. Isi liye isay "0-1 loss" bhi kehte hain. Ye bilkul wohi cheez hai jo hum naapte hain.

NP loss — teen nuqte (slide se lafz ba lafz)

  • Faida: "it's the same as our performance metric, which would seem good" — yaani jo naapna hai, wohi minimize ho raha hai. Koi mismatch nahi.
  • Nuqsan: "but it's very hard to minimize L(θ), since it's discontinuous, has zero derivative almost everywhere" — yehi aakhri jumla poore masle ki jaan hai, aur exam mein yehi likhna hai.
  • Hal: "we get better performance using different loss functions, that are also easier to minimize. If they're convex, and the regularizer is convex, we can solve the RERM problem efficiently."

Ek lafz seekh lein: PROXY LOSS. Jo loss hum asal mein istemaal karte hain (hinge, logistic) usay proxy ya surrogate loss kehte hain — yaani "asli cheez ka naib". Wo NP loss ki naqal karte hain, magar optimize-able shakal mein.

Asaan misaal — NP loss kyun kaam nahi karta

Socha jaaye aap ek bilkul andhere kamre mein hain aur darwaza dhoondna hai. Agar farsh bilkul chapta ho, to aap ko koi ishaara nahi milta — har qadam par wohi ehsaas. Aap ko andhaadhund (random) chalna padega. Magar agar farsh darwaze ki taraf dhalwaan ho, to aap mehsoos kar ke seedha wahan pohonch jaayenge. NP loss chapta farsh hai; hinge aur logistic dhalwaan farsh hain.

I5Sigmoid loss

Pehli koshish: NP loss ki chhalaang ko smooth kar do, taake derivative mojood ho jaaye.

1 p(u) = ---------- 1 + e-u ℓ(y-hat, -1) = 1 / (1 + e-y-hat) ℓ(y-hat, +1) = κ · 1 / (1 + ey-hat)

Slide ka faisla do lafzon mein: "differentiable approximation of Neyman-Pearson loss but not convex".

KhoobiSigmoid loss
Sahi shakal (monotone)?Haan — margin barhne par loss girta hai
Differentiable?Haan — har jagah smooth, NP loss ka masla hal ho gaya
NP loss ke qareeb?Bohat qareeb — shakal taqreeban wohi, bas smooth
Convex?NAHI — aur yehi iski maut hai

Non-convex hone ka nuqsan kya hai? Ye seedha sawal ban sakta hai:

  • Curve mein do mod hain (pehle neeche ki taraf mudta hai, phir oopar ki taraf) — yaani ye "S" ki shakal hai, pyaali ki nahi.
  • Is wajah se empirical risk L(θ) mein kai local minima ho sakte hain — chhote chhote garhe.
  • Optimizer kisi chhote garhe mein phans sakta hai aur samajhega ke kaam ho gaya, jabke asli (global) minimum kahin aur tha.
  • Yaani: jawab milne ki koi zamanat nahi, aur jawab shuruati guess (initialization) par depend karega.

Convex vs non-convex — ek tasveer mein

Convex = ek saaf pyaali. Kanchi (marble) kahin se bhi chhodein, wo hamesha ek hi sab se neeche wale nuqte par pohonchegi. Non-convex = oonchi neechi zameen, jis mein kai garhe hain. Kanchi us garhe mein ruk jaayegi jo uske sab se qareeb tha — zaroori nahi ke wo sab se gehra ho. Isi liye convexity ki itni ahmiyat hai: wo zamanat deti hai ke jo jawab mila, wohi best hai.

To sigmoid loss se kya seekha? Ke sirf "differentiable" aur "NP ke qareeb" hona kaafi nahi — convexity sab se zyada zaroori hai. Isi liye agli do losses (logistic aur hinge) NP loss se zyada door hain, magar convex hain — aur wohi practice mein istemaal hoti hain.

I6Logistic loss

p(u) = log( 1 + eu ) ℓ(y-hat, -1) = log( 1 + ey-hat ) ℓ(y-hat, +1) = κ · log( 1 + e-y-hat )

Slide ka faisla: "differentiable and convex approximation of Neyman-Pearson loss" — yaani dono khoobiyan ek saath. Yehi is loss ki bari baat hai.

KhoobiLogistic loss
Convex?HAAN — global minimum ki zamanat
Differentiable?HAAN — har jagah smooth
Monotone decreasing in margin?HAAN — jitna zyada theek, utna kam loss
Kabhi bilkul zero hota hai?Nahi — hamesha thoda sa positive rehta hai

Wo "kabhi zero nahi hota" wali baat samajh lein — is par sawal banta hai. Hisaab karein:

ℓ(-5, -1) = log(1 + e-5) = log(1.0067) = 0.0067 ← chhota, magar ZERO NAHI ℓ(-10, -1) = log(1 + e-10) = log(1.00005) = 0.00005 ← aur chhota, phir bhi zero nahi Chahe y-hat kitna bhi bada (sahi taraf) ho jaaye, loss zero ko sirf CHHOOTA hai, barabar nahi hota.

Iska amli asar: logistic loss hamesha thora sa dabaav daalta rehta hai — wo kehta hai "theek hai, magar aur yaqeen se theek ho jao". Is liye sahi classify hue points bhi training ko thora thora asar dete rehte hain. Hinge loss is mamle mein mukhtalif hai (dekhein I7): wo ek had ke baad bilkul zero ho jaata hai, aur phir us point ko bhool jaata hai.

Naam: Jab logistic loss ko kisi bhi regularizer ke saath istemaal karein, to us classifier ka naam logistic regression hai. (Naam mein "regression" hai magar ye classification ka tareeqa hai — ye confuse karne wali baat hai, aur exam mein poochi jaa sakti hai.)

I7Hinge loss

Ye OHT-II ka Q-4 part 1 hai — aur sab se zyada aane wala loss.

p(u) = (1 + u)+ = max(0, 1 + u) ℓ(y-hat, -1) = (1 + y-hat)+ = max(0, 1 + y-hat) ℓ(y-hat, +1) = κ · (1 - y-hat)+ = κ · max(0, 1 - y-hat)

Slide ka faisla: "another convex approximation of Neyman-Pearson loss".

"Hinge" (kabza/chitkani) naam kyun? Kyunki curve ki shakal darwaze ke kabze jaisi hai: ek hissa bilkul chapta (zero), phir achanak ek moad, aur us ke baad seedhi dhalaan. Wo moad hi "hinge" hai.

Hinge ki sab se khaas baat — margin ka tasawwur

Dhyan dein loss y-hat = 0 par zero nahi hota, balki y-hat = −1 par. Yaani:

y-hat = -0.5 → loss = max(0, 1 - 0.5) = 0.5 (THEEK classify hua, magar phir bhi SAZA mili!) y-hat = -1.0 → loss = max(0, 1 - 1.0) = 0.0 (ab maafi mili) y-hat = -2.0 → loss = max(0, 1 - 2.0) = 0.0 (yahan bhi 0 — extra inaam nahi)

Yaani hinge loss sirf "theek" se khush nahi hota — wo margin ke saath theek maangta hai. Boundary ke bilkul paas ka sahi jawab bhi usay qubool nahi. Yehi "margin" ka tasawwur hai, aur yehi SVM ki buniyad hai (I11).

Hinge vs logistic — sab se ahem farq (ye muqabla poocha jaata hai):

 HingeLogistic
Convex?HaanHaan
Differentiable?Nahi — y-hat = ∓1 par moadHaan — har jagah smooth
Bilkul zero hota hai?Haan — margin ≥ 1 par poori maafiNahi — hamesha thora positive
AsarDoor ke sahi points ka training par koi asar nahi → boundary sirf qareebi points se bani — "support vectors"Saare points thora thora asar dete rehte hain
Classifier ka naamSVM (square regularizer ke saath)Logistic regression

Ab Q-4 part 1 ka hal. Diya hua: κ = 4.0, y-hat = −0.40, asli label v = +1:

ℓ(y-hat, +1) = κ · (1 - y-hat)+ = 4.0 × max( 0, 1 - (-0.40) ) = 4.0 × max( 0, 1 + 0.40 ) = 4.0 × 1.40 = 5.60

Dhyan dein: asli class positive thi magar prediction negative aayi (−0.40) — yaani ye ek false negative hai. Aur kyunki κ = 4.0 hai, is ghalti ki saza chaar guna ho gayi. Exam mein ye ek line zaroor likhein — yehi "technical justification" hai.

I8Hubristic loss (Huber + logistic)

Ye sab se kam mashhoor loss hai, magar slides par poora diya gaya hai — aur yehi wajah hai ke is par sawal aa sakta hai (jo hissa kam padha jaata hai, examiner usi se poochta hai).

Naam "hubristic" = Huber + logistic hai. Teen tukdon wala (piecewise) function:

┌ 0, jab u < -1 │ p(u) = │ (u + 1)², jab -1 ≤ u ≤ 0 │ └ 1 + 2u, jab u > 0 ℓ(y-hat, -1) = p(y-hat) ℓ(y-hat, +1) = κ · p(-y-hat)

Pehle check karein ke ye waqai jurti (continuous) hai — ye derivation-type sawal hai:

u = -1 par: pehla tukda : 0 doosra tukda : (-1 + 1)² = 0 ✓ barabar u = 0 par: doosra tukda : (0 + 1)² = 1 teesra tukda : 1 + 2(0) = 1 ✓ barabar Derivatives bhi milte hain (yaani SMOOTH hai, sirf jurti nahi): u = 0 par: doosra ka derivative : 2(u+1) = 2(0+1) = 2 teesra ka derivative : 2 ✓ barabar

Teen tukdon ka maqsad — har tukda ek khaas kaam karta hai:

HissaShakalKaam
u < −1Zero (chapta)Door ke sahi points ko poori maafi — jaise hinge mein
−1 ≤ u ≤ 0Quadratic (pyaali)Hinge ka moad hata diya — is hisse mein smooth ho gaya (differentiable)
u > 0Linear (seedhi line)Bari ghaltiyon par saza sirf seedhi barhti hai, square ki tarah phatti nahi → outliers se mehfooz (robust)

Hubristic kyun banaya gaya — ek line mein

Ye hinge ki do kharabiyan ek saath theek karta hai: (1) hinge ka moad hata kar usay differentiable bana deta hai (beech mein quadratic daal kar), aur (2) phir bhi bari ghaltiyon par linear rehta hai, is liye square loss ki tarah outliers se tabah nahi hota. Yaani: hinge ki maafi + logistic ki smoothness + Huber ki robustness. Aur ye convex bhi hai. (Ye bilkul wohi tarkeeb hai jo OHT-1 ke Huber loss mein thi — beech mein square, kinaron par linear.)

I9Saare losses ka comparison — poore Part I ka khulasa

Agar sirf ek cheez yaad rakhni ho, ye table yaad rakhein. Isi se Q-4 jaise saare sawal nikal aate hain.

Lossp(u)ConvexDiff'ableSahi shakalKhaas baat / masla
Square(1+u)²HaanHaanNAHISahi, yaqeeni predictions ko bhi saza deta hai. Faida: least-squares se exactly hal ho jaata hai
Neyman-Pearson
(0-1)		1 if u≥0, else 0NAHINAHIHaanBilkul asli metric — magar discontinuous, derivative taqreeban har jagah zero. Minimize karna bohat mushkil
Sigmoid1/(1+e−u)NAHIHaanHaanNP ka smooth naqal — magar non-convex, local minima mein phans sakta hai
Logisticlog(1+eu)HaanHaanHaanDono khoobiyan. Kabhi bilkul zero nahi hota → logistic regression
Hinge(1+u)+HaanNAHIHaanMargin ≥ 1 par bilkul zero → support vectors → SVM
Hubristic0 / (u+1)² / 1+2uHaanHaanHaanHinge ka moad hata diya, magar linear tail rakha → smooth aur robust

Is table ki kahani — agar sawal mein "compare these loss functions" aaye, ye tarteeb se likhein:

  1. NP loss wo cheez hai jo hum waqai chahte hain — magar optimize nahi ho sakti (flat + discontinuous).
  2. Square loss optimize aasaan hai — magar shakal hi ghalat hai (sahi predictions ko saza).
  3. Sigmoid shakal theek kar deta hai — magar convexity qurbaan kar deta hai.
  4. Logistic aur hinge dono cheezein de dete hain: sahi shakal aur convexity. Is liye yehi practice mein istemaal hote hain.
  5. Hubristic hinge ko smooth bana deta hai, robustness qaim rakh kar.

Numerical sawal ke liye — sab se tez tareeqa

Loss nikalne ke liye hamesha teen qadam:

QADAM 1 Label dekho: v = -1 ya v = +1 ? QADAM 2 Sahi formula uthao: v = -1 → p( y-hat) (κ NAHI lagta!) v = +1 → κ · p(-y-hat) (κ LAGTA hai) QADAM 3 Number daal kar hisaab karo.

Sab se aam ghalti: κ ko negative class par bhi laga dena. Dhyan rahe — κ sirf positive class (v = +1) par lagta hai, kyunki wohi false negative ki saza hai. Doosri aam ghalti: −y-hat mein nishaan palatna bhool jaana.

I10Naam waale classifiers — LS, logistic regression, SVM

Slides par ek chhota magar bohat zyada poocha jaane wala slide hai: "Boolean classifiers with names". Teen classifiers, aur har aik sirf do cheezon se pehchana jaata hai — kaunsa loss aur kaunsa regularizer:

NaamLossRegularizerSlide ka lafz
Least squares classifierSquare lossSquare"uses square loss, square regularizer"
Logistic regressionLogistic lossKoi bhi"uses logistic loss, any regularizer, as in, logistic regression with … regularizer"
Support vector machine (SVM)Hinge lossSquare"uses hinge loss, square regularizer"

Ye teen jore rata lagane layaq hain

SQUARE loss + square reg = LEAST SQUARES CLASSIFIER LOGISTIC loss + koi bhi reg = LOGISTIC REGRESSION HINGE loss + square reg = SUPPORT VECTOR MACHINE (SVM)

Sawal dono taraf se aa sakta hai: "which loss function does an SVM use?" (jawab: hinge), ya "a classifier uses hinge loss with square regularization — what is it called?" (jawab: SVM). Dono taraf se tayyar rahein.

Logistic regression ke saath "any regularizer" kyun likha hai? Kyunki logistic loss ko kisi bhi regularizer ke saath istemaal karne par naam wohi rehta hai — bas regularizer ka naam saath lag jaata hai: "logistic regression with ℓ1 regularizer" (Lasso waala), ya "with square regularizer" (Ridge waala). Magar SVM ke saath aisa nahi — wahan square regularizer definition ka hissa hai.

I11SVM — decision boundary aur margin lines

Slides par SVM ka ek graph hai, aur us ke saath teen baatein likhi hain. Ye teeno yaad karein:

SVM ki teen lines — slide se

(1) decision boundary : y-hat = 0 (2) black lines : y-hat = ±1 ← MARGIN ki lines (3) "what is the training risk here?" ← slide ka sawal

Teen lines ka matlab:

  • y-hat = 0 — asli faisle ki line (decision boundary). Yahan se class badal jaati hai. (Ye wohi sign rule hai — H15.)
  • y-hat = +1 aur y-hat = −1 — margin ki lines. Hinge loss in ke bahar waale sahi points ko bilkul maaf kar deta hai (loss = 0). In ke andar jo bhi aaye, us par saza lagti hai — chahe wo sahi taraf ho.
  • Yaani donon margin lines ke beech ka ilaaqa "no man's land" hai — SVM chahta hai ke ye ilaaqa khaali rahe.

Slide ke sawal "what is the training risk here?" ka jawab — ye socha hua sawal hai:

Training risk kaise nikaalein

Risk = saare points ka average hinge loss. Aur kyunki margin ke bahar waale sahi points ka loss 0 hai, is liye:

training risk = 1/n × ( sirf un points ka hinge loss jo margin ke ANDAR ya GHALAT taraf hain )

Yaani sirf support vectors ginne hain. Agar tasveer mein saare points margin ke bahar sahi taraf hon, to training risk bilkul zero hoga — aur ye hinge loss ke saath mumkin hai, jo square loss ke saath kabhi mumkin nahi (kyunki wahan har point kuch na kuch loss deta hai).

I12Australian weather — poori case study

Slides par ek asli, mukammal case study hai. Ye ahem hai kyunki is mein poora pipeline ek saath nazar aata hai — aur numbers par seedha sawal ban sakta hai.

Data:

CheezTafseel
KaamAaj ki measurements se batana ke kal baarish hogi ya nahi
Jagah / arsaAustralia ke kai mausami stations, 10 saal (2007–2017)
Kul records142,193
Missing data hatane ke baad112,925 (yaani ~29,000 records gir gaye)
SourceKaggle — weather-dataset-rattle-package

Fields (features):

NUMERIC (12 fields): MinTemp, MaxTemp, Rainfall, WindGustSpeed, WindSpeed9am, WindSpeed3pm, Humidity9am, Humidity3pm, Pressure9am, Pressure3pm, Temp9am, Temp3pm CATEGORICAL: Location (44 possible locations) WindGustDir, WindDir9am, WindDir3pm (16 compass points har aik) RainToday (YES / NO) EXTRA: date

Embedding ke faisle — ye hissa sab se zyada seekhne wala hai, kyunki yahan har faisle ki ek wajah hai:

FieldKya kiyaKyun
12 numeric fieldsIdentity map (jaise hain, waise rakh liye)Ye pehle se numbers hain
3 wind directionsOne-hot (16 compass points har aik) → 48 columnsDirection mein koi tarteeb nahi — North "South se bada" nahi hota
RainToday{−1, +1} mein embedBoolean hai — ±1 aasaan rehta hai
date, locationISTEMAAL NAHI KIYESlide: "did not improve validation performance"
Sab kuchStandardize kiyaScale bohat mukhtalif thay (pressure ~1000, humidity ~50)
Aakhir meinConstant feature add kiyaOffset/bias ke liye
Nateeja: x ∈ R6212 numeric + 48 wind + 1 RainToday + 1 constant = 62

Do faisle jo exam mein poochey jaa sakte hain:

  1. Location ko kyun nahi liya? Kyunki 44 locations ka one-hot 44 naye columns banata — yaani d 62 se 106 ho jaata — aur validation performance behtar nahi hui. Yaani extra complexity ka koi faida nahi mila. Ye validation se feature chunne ki misaal hai.
  2. Date ko kyun nahi liya? Wohi wajah. (Aur aik aur baat — date ko theek se istemaal karne ke liye usay mausam/seasonality mein badalna padta, jo ek alag feature engineering ka kaam hai.)

Model:

Output embed: RainTomorrow → {-1, +1} Loss: LOGISTIC ℓ(y-hat,-1) = log(1 + ey-hat) ℓ(y-hat,+1) = log(1 + e-y-hat) Predictor: LINEAR y-hat = θTx Regularizer: SQUARE r(θ) = ‖ θ2:d ‖₂² (dhyan: θ₁ yaani OFFSET regularize NAHI kiya — wohi qaida jo OHT-1 ke F8 mein tha)

Nataij — ye numbers yaad rakhein, in par seedha sawal banta hai:

CheezValueMatlab
Train/test split80 / 20Randomly
Test vs train resultsTaqreeban barabarOverfitting nahi hui — regularization kaam kar gaya
Minimum probability of error16%Model ki best performance
Rain frequency22%Baarish 22% dinon mein hoti hai
Baseline error22%Jo model hamesha "no rain" kahe, wo 22% error dega
6 features se dobara train16.5%62 features ki jagah sirf 6 — aur sirf 0.5% kharab

Sab se ahem nuktah — 16% vs 22%

Slide jaan boojh kar dono numbers saath likhti hai: model ka error 16%, aur "hamesha no rain" kehne wale bewaqoof model ka error 22%. Yaani asli faida sirf 6 percentage points ka hai. Sabaq: accuracy ka number akela bemani hai — usay hamesha baseline ke saath muqabla karna chahiye. Agar baarish 50% dinon mein hoti, to baseline 50% hota, aur 16% error zabardast hota. Magar yahan data imbalanced hai (22% vs 78%), is liye 84% accuracy itni khaas nahi. Ye wohi masla hai jo H3 ke cancer example mein tha.

Sab se important feature — aur is ki khoobsurat wajah:

Sab se important feature: Pressure9am - Pressure3pm Wajah (slide par likhi hai): "RAPIDLY FALLING PRESSURE indicates a STORM IS COMING"

Ye bohat umda misaal hai: model ne khud ba khud wo cheez pakad li jo mausamiyat (meteorology) ka buniyadi usool hai — pressure tezi se girna toofan ki nishani hai. Aur dhyan dein ke ye ek farq (difference) hai, do alag numbers nahi. Model ne Pressure9am ko positive weight aur Pressure3pm ko negative weight de kar khud ye farq bana liya.

Aakhri experiment — aur iska sabaq:

Sirf 6 features se dobara train kiya: MinTemp, MaxTemp, WindGustSpeed, Humidity3pm, Pressure9am, Pressure3pm Error: 16.5% (62 features ke saath 16% tha) Yaani: features 62 se 6 kar diye (90% kam), aur performance sirf 0.5 percentage point kharab hui.

Ye sparsity aur feature selection ka asli faida dikhata hai (wohi jo OHT-1 ke F9 mein tha): chhota model taqreeban utna hi acha hai, magar samajhne, chalane aur maintain karne mein kai guna behtar. Agar sawal mein "would you recommend the 6-feature or 62-feature model?" aaye, to jawab: 6-feature — kyunki 0.5% ka nuqsan interpretability, speed aur robustness ke faide ke muqable mein kuch bhi nahi.

Part J — Lecture 8 (doosra hissa)

Multi-class Classification

Ab K > 2. Khushi ki baat ye hai ke koi nayi cheez nahi aati — wohi embed/predict/un-embed, wohi RERM, wohi hinge aur logistic. Sirf ek nayi cheez seekhni hai: signed distance (J3), kyunki Boolean mein "kitna theek hai" ka jawab y-hat khud tha, magar multi-class mein wo nikaalna padta hai. Baaqi sab usi par khada hai.

J1Multi-class ka poora setup

V = {1, …, K} 1. Classes ko EMBED karo: y₁, …, yK ∈ Rm 2. NEAREST-NEIGHBOUR un-embedding: v-hat = arg min ‖ y-hat - yk ‖₂ k 3. RERM se predictor fit karo 4. TEST data par NEYMAN-PEARSON metric se validate karo: K NP = Σ κk rk / n k=1 rk = class k ko kisi doosri class samajhne ki ginti κk = class k ko ghalat samajhne ki hamein kitni fikar κ₁ = κ₂ = … = κK = 1 → NP = error rate

Chaaron qadam Boolean waale hi hain. Boolean asal mein multi-class ka ek khaas case hai jahan K = 2 — aur aage J5/J6 mein hum ye saabit kar ke dikhayenge.

J2Kaunsa representative zyada qareeb hai — derivation

Buniyadi sawal: x kab a ke zyada qareeb hai, b ke muqable mein? Jawab nikalna aasaan hai, aur ye derivation seedha sawal ban sakti hai.

Shuru karein: ‖x - a‖₂ < ‖x - b‖₂ Dono taraf SQUARE karein (dono positive hain, is liye jaiz hai): ‖x - a‖₂² < ‖x - b‖₂² Dono ko khol dein ( ‖x-a‖² = ‖x‖² - 2aTx + ‖a‖² ): ‖x‖² - 2aTx + ‖a‖² < ‖x‖² - 2bTx + ‖b‖² ‖x‖² DONO taraf hai → kaat dein (yehi asal chaal hai!): -2aTx + ‖a‖² < -2bTx + ‖b‖² Sab ek taraf karein: 2(b - a)Tx - ‖b‖₂² + ‖a‖₂² < 0

Sab se ahem nateeja — aur kyun

‖x‖² kaat jaata hai, aur jo bacha wo x mein LINEAR hai. Is liye:

Decision boundary: 2(b-a)Tx - ‖b‖₂² + ‖a‖₂² = 0 Ye Rm mein ek HYPERPLANE hai, jiska: NORMAL VECTOR = b - a (yaani a se b ki taraf ka teer) aur jo guzarta hai MIDPOINT (a + b)/2 se

Yehi wajah hai ke Voronoi regions ke kinaare seedhe hote hain (H18). Agar ‖x‖² na kaatta, to boundary quadratic (tedhi) hoti. Aur isi liye ye derivation itni ahem hai — ye batati hai ke "nearest neighbour" jaisa tedha sawal asal mein ek seedhi line ka sawal hai.

Midpoint se guzarne ki tasdeeq (verification) — agar "show that it passes through the midpoint" poocha jaaye:

x = (a+b)/2 rakh kar dekhein: 2(b-a)T (a+b)/2 = (b-a)T(a+b) = bTa + bTb - aTa - aTb = ‖b‖² - ‖a‖² ( kyunki aTb = bTa, kaat gaye ) Ab poora expression: (‖b‖² - ‖a‖²) - ‖b‖² + ‖a‖² = 0 ✓ Yaani midpoint hamesha boundary PAR hota hai — bilkul theek, kyunki wo dono se BARABAR faasle par hai.

J3Signed distance to the decision boundary

Pichhle topic mein hum ne jaana ke x kis taraf hai. Ab ek number chahiye jo batayein: kis taraf hai AUR kitna door hai. Yehi signed distance hai — aur poore Part J ki jaan.

x ki a ke muqable b ki taraf SIGNED DISTANCE: 2(b - a)Tx - ‖b‖₂² + ‖a‖₂² d(x) = ------------------------------------- 2 ‖b - a‖₂ (oopar wala hissa J2 se aaya hai; neeche 2‖b-a‖ se taqseem kar diya)

Char khoobiyan — slides par chaaron likhi hain, aur chaaron poochi jaa sakti hain:

KhoobiMatlab
d(x) < 0x a ke zyada qareeb hai, b ke muqable — yaani sahi taraf (agar asli class a hai)
|d(x)|x ka boundary se asli faasla (ye sirf ishaara nahi, waqai distance hai)
d(x) = 0Decision boundary — bilkul darmiyan
d is an affine function of xYaani d(x) = cTx + e ki shakal — is liye hisaab aur optimization aasaan rehti hai

Wo 2‖b−a‖ se taqseem kyun ki? Ye poocha jaa sakta hai. Wajah: normalization. Is ke bagair number sirf "kis taraf" batata (nishaan), magar uski size ka koi matlab na hota — wo a aur b kitne door hain, us par depend karti. Taqseem karne ke baad number asli faasla (true geometric distance) ban jaata hai, jo har jode ke liye ek jaise paimane par hota hai. Isi liye aage hinge loss mein "margin of at least one" ka matlab banta hai.

Boolean se rishta — ye zaroor samajh lein

Agar K = 2 ho aur embedding y1 = −1, y2 = +1 ho, to signed distance kya banta hai? Formula mein daal kar dekhein (a = −1, b = +1, m = 1):

2(1 - (-1)) y-hat - (1)² + (-1)² 4 y-hat - 1 + 1 d(y-hat) = ------------------------------------ = --------------- = y-hat 2 |1 - (-1)| 4

d = y-hat — bilkul wohi! Yaani Boolean case mein signed distance aur y-hat ek hi cheez hain. Isi liye Boolean mein hamein signed distance ki zaroorat nahi padi thi — y-hat khud hi wo kaam kar raha tha. Multi-class mein K representatives hote hain, is liye usay alag se nikaalna padta hai. Ye ek number ka hisaab 6 marks dila sakta hai agar sawal mein "show that the multi-class loss reduces to the Boolean one" aaye.

J4Multi-class loss ki shart

Pehle notation. Jab asli class k ho, to hamein K functions chahiye (har class ke liye ek):

ℓk(y-hat) = "jab asli class k thi, to y-hat kehna kitna bura tha"

Ab shart. Ye signed distance ki zubaan mein likhi jaati hai, aur bohat saaf hai:

ℓk(y-hat) CHHOTA hona chahiye jab: max dj|k(y-hat) < 0 j≠k ℓk(y-hat) BADA hona chahiye jab: max dj|k(y-hat) ≥ 0 j≠k jahan dj|k(y-hat) = y-hat ki, yk ke muqable yj ki taraf, signed distance

Is max ka matlab samajh lein — yahan asli chaalaki hai:

  • Har dj|k < 0 ka matlab: y-hat apni class yk ke zyada qareeb hai, yj ke muqable — yaani class j se muqabla jeeta.
  • Sahi un-embedding ke liye ye saari doosri classes ke saath jeetna zaroori hai — ek bhi se haar gaye to jawab ghalat.
  • Aur "saari d negative hain" likhne ka sab se chhota tareeqa ye hai: "sab se bari d bhi negative hai", yaani maxj≠k dj|k < 0.

Asaan misaal — max kyun

Socha jaaye ek race hai jis mein aap ko sab ko haraana hai, pehli position leni hai. Ab ye kehne ke liye "main jeet gaya", kaafi hai ke jo sab se tez banda tha, us se bhi main aage tha. Baaqi sab ka hisaab karne ki zaroorat nahi — jo sab se bada khatra tha, usay dekh lein, kaam ho gaya. max wohi "sab se bada khatra" dhoondta hai. Isi liye multi-class hinge loss mein bhi sirf sab se khatarnaak competitor gina jaata hai.

Multi-class Neyman-Pearson loss — wohi 0-1 loss, multi-class shakal mein:

┌ 0, jab max dj|k(y-hat) < 0 (theek un-embed hua) │ j≠k ℓk(y-hat) = │ └ κk, warna (ghalat hua) Yaani: theek par ZERO, ghalat par κk.

Aur wohi purana masla, slides ke lafzon mein: "it's hard to minimize". Is liye phir proxy loss chahiye, jo:

  • NP loss ki naqal kare, ya kam az kam uska maza (flavour) pakde, aur
  • aasaani se optimize ho — yaani convex ho, ya differentiable, ya dono.

Agle do topics bilkul yehi do proxy losses hain — aur dono wohi hain jo Boolean mein thay: hinge aur logistic.

J5Multi-class hinge loss (multi-class SVM)

ℓk(y-hat) = κk · max ( 1 + dj|k(y-hat) )+ j≠k

Teen tukdon mein padhein:

  1. dj|k — "class j se muqable mein main kitna theek hoon" (negative = theek).
  2. (1 + d)+ — "margin of at least one" ki shart. Sirf d < 0 kaafi nahi; d ≤ −1 chahiye, warna saza lagti hai. (Bilkul Boolean hinge jaisa — I7.)
  3. maxj≠k — "sab se khatarnaak competitor dekho" (J4 ki race wali misaal).

Slides ki teen baatein:

BaatTafseel
Kab zero hota hai"zero when y-hat is correctly un-embedded, with a margin of at least one"
Khoobi / kharabiConvex — magar differentiable nahi (wohi moad ka masla)
NaamQuadratic regularization ke saath → multi-class SVM

Boolean par reduce karna — ye derivation poochi jaa sakti hai

K = 2, embedding y1 = −1, y2 = +1. J3 se hum jaante hain d = y-hat (class 1 ke liye) aur d = −y-hat (class 2 ke liye). Daal dein:

class 1 (v = -1) : sirf ek competitor (j=2), d2|1 = y-hat ℓ₁(y-hat) = κ₁ ( 1 + y-hat )+ class 2 (v = +1) : sirf ek competitor (j=1), d1|2 = -y-hat ℓ₂(y-hat) = κ₂ ( 1 - y-hat )+

Ye bilkul Boolean hinge loss hai (I7)! Aur agar κ1 = κ2 = 1 ho, to ye "usual hinge loss" ban jaata hai. Yaani multi-class hinge Boolean hinge ka aam (general) roop hai — koi nayi cheez nahi, bas zyada classes ke liye phailaya gaya. maxj≠k ghayab ho gaya kyunki K=2 mein competitor sirf ek hota hai, aur ek cheez ka max wohi cheez hoti hai.

J6Multi-class logistic loss

K ℓk(y-hat) = κk · log Σ exp ( dj|k(y-hat) ) j=1 jahan hum dk|k = 0 lete hain (yaani apni class ke saath apna muqabla zero hai — theek bhi hai)

Dhyan dein summation j = 1 se K tak hai, yaani apni class bhi shaamil hai (j = k bhi). Aur us ka hissa exp(0) = 1 hai. Ye 1 hi wo cheez hai jo loss ko hamesha positive rakhti hai — bilkul jaise Boolean logistic mein log(1 + eu) mein 1 tha.

Slides ki do baatein:

  • Convex aur differentiable — dono khoobiyan, hinge ke bar-khilaf.
  • Naam: multi-class logistic regression.

Boolean par reduce karna

Wohi K = 2 waala hisaab (d = y-hat aur d = −y-hat, aur dk|k = 0):

class 1 (v = -1): ℓ₁ = κ₁ log( e0 + ey-hat ) = κ₁ log( 1 + ey-hat ) class 2 (v = +1): ℓ₂ = κ₂ log( e-y-hat + e0 ) = κ₂ log( 1 + e-y-hat )

Bilkul Boolean logistic loss (I6). Aur κ1 = κ2 = 1 par "usual logistic loss". Ye "wo 1 kahan se aaya" ka jawab bhi de deta hai — wo exp(dk|k) = exp(0) = 1 hai, yaani apni class ka hissa.

Hinge vs logistic — multi-class mein bhi wohi farq:

 Multi-class hingeMulti-class logistic
Formula ki roohmaxj≠k — sirf sab se bada khatra dekhta haiΣj exp(·) — saare competitors ka hissa leta hai
Convex?HaanHaan
Differentiable?Nahi (max aur (·)+ dono moad banate hain)Haan (log aur exp smooth hain)
Zero ho sakta hai?Haan — margin poora ho jaaye to bilkul zeroNahi — hamesha thora positive
NaamMulti-class SVMMulti-class logistic regression

Ek mufeed baat: log Σ exp(·) ko "log-sum-exp" kehte hain, aur ye asal mein max ka smooth naqal hai (logΣexp taqreeban max ke barabar hota hai, magar smooth). Yaani logistic loss hinge loss ka smooth version hai — dono ek hi cheez keh rahe hain, ek sakhti se aur ek narmi se. Ye baat samajh lein to dono formulas yaad rakhna aasaan ho jaata hai. (Aur agar aap ne "softmax" suna hai, to wo yehi cheez hai.)

J7Iris example — confusion matrix padhna

Slides ka aakhri example — aur confusion matrix interpret karne ka behtareen sawal.

CheezTafseel
DatasetFisher ka mashhoor Iris dataset, 1936
Records150 plants — har species se 50
Classes (K = 3)iris setosa, iris versicolor, iris virginica
Features (4)sepal length, sepal width, petal length, petal width
SetupOne-hot embedding, multi-class logistic loss, κk = 1 for all k, saare data par train

Tajurba 1 — sirf do features (sepal_length aur sepal_width):

┌ ┐ │ 50 0 0 │ C = │ 0 38 13 │ │ 0 12 37 │ └ ┘ trace = 50 + 38 + 37 = 125 errors = 150 - 125 = 25 error rate = 25/150 = 16.67%

Tajurba 2 — saare chaar features:

┌ ┐ │ 50 0 0 │ C = │ 0 49 1 │ │ 0 1 49 │ └ ┘ trace = 50 + 49 + 49 = 148 errors = 150 - 148 = 2 error rate = 2/150 = 1.33%

Dono matrices ka mukammal tajziya — 4 nuqte

  1. Setosa bilkul alag hai. Dono matrices mein pehla row/column saaf hai: 50 0 0. Yaani setosa linearly separable hai baaqi dono se — ek bhi ghalti nahi, do features par bhi nahi.
  2. Saari ghaltiyan versicolor aur virginica ke darmiyan hain. Pehli matrix mein 13 + 12 = 25, doosri mein 1 + 1 = 2. Setosa kabhi shaamil nahi — ye bohat saaf pattern hai.
  3. Confusion symmetric hai. 13 vs 12 (aur 1 vs 1) — taqreeban barabar. Yaani model kisi ek class ki taraf jhuka nahi hua (biased nahi) — ye do classes waqai aapas mein ghul mili hui hain. Agar ye 25 aur 0 hota, to model mein bias hota aur baat alag hoti.
  4. Petal features hi asli kaam karte hain. Error 16.67% se 1.33% par aa gaya — yaani 12.5 guna behtar — sirf petal length aur petal width milaane se. Nateeja: sepal ki measurements in do species mein farq nahi karti, magar petal ki karti hai.

Ye poora khulasa ek jumle mein (agar "interpret" poocha jaaye): "Setosa is perfectly separable from both other species with just the two sepal features. All errors occur between versicolor and virginica and are nearly symmetric (13/12), indicating genuine overlap rather than model bias. Adding the two petal features reduces the error rate from 16.67% to 1.33%, showing that petal dimensions — not sepal dimensions — carry the information needed to distinguish these two species."

Dhyan — ye numbers TRAINING error hain

Slide likhti hai "trained on all data". Yaani koi train/test split nahi hua — ye 1.33% training error hai, test error nahi. Agar sawal mein poocha jaaye "can we conclude this model will achieve 1.33% error on new flowers?", to jawab NAHI hai — aur wajah wohi hai jo OHT-1 ke C1 mein thi: jis data se model bana, usi par naapna jhoota (optimistic) andaza deta hai. Sahi tareeqa: data ko train/test mein baantein, jaise Australian weather example mein 80/20 kiya gaya tha.

Teen lectures ka poora khulasa — ek safe par

LECTURE 6 — NEURAL NETWORKS layer : gi(zi-1) = h( (Θi)T(1, zi-1) ) parameters : (di-1 + 1) × di per layer, phir JAMA karo h ke bagair: poora network ek LINEAR predictor ban jaata hai perceptron : margin = y(wTx); agar < 0 to w ← w + ηyx LECTURE 7 — CLASSIFIERS poora amal : EMBED → PREDICT → UN-EMBED un-embed : nearest neighbour; one-hot ke saath = ARGMAX naapna : confusion matrix; error rate = 1 - trace/n NP metric : (κ fn + fp)/n κ = 1 → error rate LECTURE 8 — LOSS FUNCTIONS framework : ℓ(y-hat,-1) = p(y-hat) , ℓ(y-hat,+1) = κ p(-y-hat) square : (1+u)² convex, magar SHAKAL GHALAT NP (0-1) : step shakal theek, magar OPTIMIZE NAHI HOTA sigmoid : 1/(1+e-u) smooth, magar NON-CONVEX logistic : log(1+eu) convex + differentiable → logistic regression hinge : (1+u)+ convex, zero ho jaata hai → SVM multi-class: wohi sab, magar dj|k (signed distance) ke saath

Asli Paper — OHT-II, November 2026

Q1–Q9, poore hal ke saath

Asli Q16 marks3-layer forward pass + biases

Hidden activations z1, z2 aur output ŷ

A 3-layer fully connected feedforward neural network (two hidden layers with ReLU activations max(0,a) and a linear output layer) computes the composition ŷ = g3(g2(g1(x))) with explicit biases bi:
z1 = max(0, W1x + b1),   z2 = max(0, W2z1 + b2),   ŷ = W3z2 + b3
Given input x = [1.0, −2.0]T and layer parameters:
W1 = [[2.0, 1.0], [−1.0, −1.5]], b1 = [−1.0, 0.5]
W2 = [[1.2, −0.4], [−0.5, 0.8]], b2 = [1.5, −2.5]
W3 = [3.0, −2.0], b3 = [0.25]
Compute the hidden layer activations z1 and z2, and the final scalar network output ŷ.

Hint — teen cheezein jo yahan farq daalti hain

  • Ab biases hain (sample paper ke Q-1 mein nahi thay). Har layer par Wx ke baad + b lagana hai — ReLU lagane se pehle. Tarteeb: Wx → +b → ReLU.
  • Aakhri layer par ReLU NAHI hai — sawal mein saaf likha hai "linear output layer", aur formula bhi ŷ = W3z2 + b3 hai, bina max(0,·) ke. Yahan ReLU laga dena sab se bari ghalti hogi.
  • Teen cheezein maangi gayi hain — z1, z2, ŷ. Teeno alag likhein.

English — paper par likhein

Layer 1 — z1 = max(0, W1x + b1):

W₁x : 2.0(1.0) + 1.0(-2.0) = 2.0 - 2.0 = 0.0 -1.0(1.0) + (-1.5)(-2.0) = -1.0 + 3.0 = 2.0 + b₁ : 0.0 + (-1.0) = -1.0 2.0 + 0.5 = 2.5 ReLU : z¹ = ( max(0,-1.0), max(0,2.5) ) = ( 0, 2.5 )

Layer 2 — z2 = max(0, W2z1 + b2):

W₂z¹: 1.2(0) + (-0.4)(2.5) = 0 - 1.0 = -1.0 -0.5(0) + 0.8 (2.5) = 0 + 2.0 = 2.0 + b₂ : -1.0 + 1.5 = 0.5 2.0 + (-2.5) = -0.5 ReLU : z² = ( max(0,0.5), max(0,-0.5) ) = ( 0.5, 0 )

Layer 3 — linear output, ŷ = W3z2 + b3 (no ReLU):

ŷ = 3.0(0.5) + (-2.0)(0) + 0.25 = 1.5 + 0 + 0.25 = 1.75

Answer: z1 = (0, 2.5),  z2 = (0.5, 0),  ŷ = 1.75.

Justification to add: ReLU switches off one unit in each hidden layer — the first unit of z1 and the second of z2. Consequently W3's second weight (−2.0) contributes nothing, and the output is produced by a single active path through the network. The output layer is deliberately linear, so ŷ is unbounded and may be negative — which is required, since it is a regression-style score, not a non-negative activation.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: z1 = (0, 2.5), z2 = (0.5, 0), ŷ = 1.75

Har layer par teen qadam, isi tarteeb se:

(1) W × (pichhla output) ← matrix multiply (2) + b ← bias jama karo (3) ReLU ← negative ko zero

Layer 1:

pehli row : 2.0(1.0) + 1.0(-2.0) = 0.0 doosri row : -1.0(1.0) + (-1.5)(-2.0) = -1.0 + 3.0 = 2.0 ╵╵╵╵╵╵╵╵╵ minus × minus = PLUS 3.0 bias jama : (0.0 - 1.0, 2.0 + 0.5) = (-1.0, 2.5) ReLU : (0, 2.5) ← -1.0 zero ho gaya

Layer 2: ab input (0, 2.5) hai — dhyan rahe z1, x nahi!

pehli row : 1.2(0) + (-0.4)(2.5) = -1.0 doosri row : -0.5(0) + 0.8(2.5) = 2.0 bias jama : (-1.0 + 1.5, 2.0 - 2.5) = (0.5, -0.5) ReLU : (0.5, 0) ← -0.5 zero ho gaya

Layer 3 — yahan ReLU NAHI lagana

Sawal kehta hai "a linear output layer", aur formula bhi ŷ = W3z2 + b3 hai — is mein max(0,·) hai hi nahi. Aadat se ReLU laga dena sab se aam ghalti hai.

ŷ = 3.0(0.5) + (-2.0)(0) + 0.25 = 1.5 + 0 + 0.25 = 1.75

Wajah likhein: ReLU ne har hidden layer mein ek ek neuron band kar diya — pehli mein pehla, doosri mein doosra. Is liye W3 ka doosra weight (−2.0) bilkul bekaar gaya, aur output sirf ek chalte hue raste se bana. Aur output layer jaan boojh kar linear rakhi gayi hai, taake ŷ negative bhi ho sake — kyunki ye ek score hai, koi activation nahi.

Asli Q26 marksactivation functions

Ek neuron, teen activation functions

A single artificial neuron receives input vector x = [2.0, −3.0, 1.0]T with synaptic weights w = [0.5, 1.0, −0.5]T and scalar bias b = 1.5, forming the cell body pre-activation sum a = wTx + b. Evaluate the neuron's output firing response under three standard activation functions:
1. Leaky ReLU: h(a) = max(0.1a, a)
2. ELU (with α = 1.0): h(a) = a if a ≥ 0, else α(ea − 1)
3. — teesra function tasveer mein nazar nahi aata (neeche note)

Hint — ek hisaab, teen jawab

  • a sirf EK BAAR nikaalein. Teeno activation functions usi ek a par lagte hain. Log teen baar poora hisaab karte hain aur waqt zaya karte hain.
  • Bias ko bhool na jayein — a = wTx + b. Yahan b = 1.5 hai, aur wo jawab ko −2.5 se −1.0 kar deta hai.
  • a negative aata hai (−1.0) — aur yehi is sawal ka asal maqsad hai. Har activation function negative input par alag rawayya dikhata hai; agar a positive hota to teeno ka jawab ek jaisa (a hi) aata aur sawal bemani ho jaata.

English — paper par likhein

Step 1 — pre-activation (computed once):

a = wTx + b = (0.5)(2.0) + (1.0)(-3.0) + (-0.5)(1.0) + 1.5 = 1.0 + (-3.0) + (-0.5) + 1.5 = -1.0

Since a = −1.0 < 0, every activation is evaluated on its negative branch.

Step 2 — Leaky ReLU:

h(a) = max(0.1a, a) = max( 0.1(-1.0), -1.0 ) = max( -0.1, -1.0 ) = -0.1

Step 3 — ELU (α = 1.0):

a < 0 → h(a) = α(ea - 1) = 1.0(e-1 - 1) = 0.3679 - 1 = -0.6321

Answer: a = −1.0; Leaky ReLU = −0.1; ELU = −0.632.

Justification to add: both activations stay negative but shrunken, unlike plain ReLU which would output exactly 0 and kill the gradient. Leaky ReLU scales by a constant 0.1, keeping a small constant slope; ELU saturates smoothly toward −α = −1. Both therefore avoid the dead-ReLU problem, and ELU additionally has a smooth derivative at the origin.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: a = −1.0; Leaky ReLU −0.1; ELU −0.632

Qadam 1 — a nikaalein (sirf ek baar!):

0.5 × 2.0 = 1.0 1.0 × -3.0 = -3.0 -0.5 × 1.0 = -0.5 ------ jama = -2.5 + bias 1.5 ------ a = -1.0

a negative hai — aur yehi sawal ki jaan hai. Ab har activation ka negative waala hissa lagega.

Qadam 2 — Leaky ReLU. "Leaky" ka matlab "thora sa risne wala" — wo negative ko poora zero nahi karta, bas 0.1 se guna kar ke chhota kar deta hai:

max( 0.1 × (-1.0) , -1.0 ) = max( -0.1 , -1.0 ) = -0.1 ← -0.1 bada hai -1.0 se (number line par dayein)

Dhyan: −0.1 aur −1.0 mein bada −0.1 hai, kyunki wo zero ke zyada qareeb hai. Yahan log ulta chun lete hain.

Qadam 3 — ELU. a < 0 hai, to doosra formula:

1.0 × (e-1 - 1) = 1.0 × (0.3679 - 1) = -0.6321

Wajah likhein (yahan marks hain): dono jawab negative hain magar chhote kar diye gaye. Agar aam ReLU hota to jawab bilkul 0 hota aur gradient mar jaata (dead ReLU — dekhein sawal 8). Leaky ReLU ek sthir chhoti dhalaan rakhta hai; ELU narmi se −1 ki taraf jaa kar ruk jaata hai. Dono dead-ReLU ka ilaaj hain — aur ELU origin par smooth bhi hai.

Teesre activation function ke baare mein — imaandari se

Sawal kehta hai "three standard activation functions", magar 9 mein se kisi tasveer mein teesra function nazar nahi aata — har tasveer mein item 2 (ELU) ke foran baad Q-3 shuru ho jaata hai. Main usay bana kar nahi likh raha.

Magar aap ko fikar karne ki zaroorat nahi — mehnat ka hissa (a = −1.0) ho chuka. Teesra jo bhi ho, jawab is table se foran nikal aayega:

Agar teesra ye hoFormulaa = −1.0 par jawab
Sigmoid (sab se ghalib)1/(1 + e−a)0.2689
tanh(ea−e−a)/(ea+e−a)−0.7616
Standard ReLUmax(0, a)0 (neuron band)

Sigmoid sab se ghalib imkaan hai — wo Lecture-6 ka doosra bunyadi activation hai, aur isi paper ke Q-7 mein bhi sigmoid ka zikr aata hai. Agar Q-2 ki saaf tasveer mil jaaye to main usay poora kar doonga.

Asli Q36 marksperceptron, augmented vector

Do sequential updates — w(1) aur w(2)

A binary perceptron uses threshold t = 0.5 and feature weights [w1, w2]T = [0.2, −0.4]T. Encoding the threshold as weight w0 = t paired with a permanent input x0 = −1, the decision rule becomes wTx > 0, with initial augmented weight vector w(0) = [w0, w1, w2]T = [0.5, 0.2, −0.4]T.
Using learning rate η = 0.5 and the Perceptron Learning Rule (Δw = ηyx if margin y(wTx) ≤ 0), perform two sequential updates on the following training samples:
(a) [x1, x2]T = [1.0, 2.0]T, label y1 = +1
(b) [x1, x2]T = [2.0, −1.0]T, label y2 = −1
What is the intermediate weight vector w(1) and final weight vector w(2)?

Hint — do cheezein jo yahan sab se zyada bigadti hain

  • x0 = −1 hai, +1 nahi! Har feature vector ke aage −1 chipkana hai: x1 ka augmented roop [−1, 1.0, 2.0] hai. Ye G15 wala tareeqa hai jahan threshold ko weight bana diya jaata hai. +1 lagane par poora jawab ghalat.
  • "Sequential" — doosra update w(1) par lagega, w(0) par nahi.
  • Pehle margin check karein, phir update. Rule saaf likha hai: update tab hi jab y(wTx) ≤ 0. (Yahan dono baar shart poori hoti hai — neeche dekhein.)

English — paper par likhein

Augmented inputs (prepend x0 = −1):

x₁ = [-1, 1.0, 2.0]T, y₁ = +1 x₂ = [-1, 2.0, -1.0]T, y₂ = -1 w(0) = [0.5, 0.2, -0.4]T

Update 1:

w(0)Tx₁ = 0.5(-1) + 0.2(1.0) + (-0.4)(2.0) = -0.5 + 0.2 - 0.8 = -1.1 margin = y₁(-1.1) = (+1)(-1.1) = -1.1 ≤ 0 → UPDATE Δw = ηy₁x₁ = 0.5(+1)[-1, 1.0, 2.0] = [-0.5, 0.5, 1.0] w(1) = [0.5, 0.2, -0.4] + [-0.5, 0.5, 1.0] = [ 0.0, 0.7, 0.6 ]

Update 2:

w(1)Tx₂ = 0.0(-1) + 0.7(2.0) + 0.6(-1.0) = 0 + 1.4 - 0.6 = +0.8 margin = y₂(+0.8) = (-1)(+0.8) = -0.8 ≤ 0 → UPDATE Δw = ηy₂x₂ = 0.5(-1)[-1, 2.0, -1.0] = [0.5, -1.0, 0.5] w(2) = [0.0, 0.7, 0.6] + [0.5, -1.0, 0.5] = [ 0.5, -0.3, 1.1 ]

Answer: w(1) = [0.0, 0.7, 0.6]T,  w(2) = [0.5, −0.3, 1.1]T.

Verification — both samples are now correctly classified:

w(2)Tx₁ = 0.5(-1) + (-0.3)(1.0) + 1.1(2.0) = -0.5 - 0.3 + 2.2 = +1.4 margin = (+1)(+1.4) = +1.4 > 0 ✓ w(2)Tx₂ = 0.5(-1) + (-0.3)(2.0) + 1.1(-1.0) = -0.5 - 0.6 - 1.1 = -2.2 margin = (-1)(-2.2) = +2.2 > 0 ✓

Justification to add: both updates were triggered because each margin was negative, i.e. the sample was misclassified. After two updates both margins are positive, so the algorithm has converged on this two-point set. Note that w0 moved from 0.5 to 0.0 and back to 0.5 — the threshold is being learned alongside the weights, which is precisely the benefit of the augmented formulation.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: w(1) = [0.0, 0.7, 0.6],  w(2) = [0.5, −0.3, 1.1]

Sab se pehle — −1 chipkayein

Sawal kehta hai x0 = −1 (permanent input). To har sample teen numbers ka ban jaata hai:

x₁ = [-1, 1.0, 2.0] x₂ = [-1, 2.0, -1.0]

+1 lagana sab se bari ghalti hogi — paper ne saaf −1 kaha hai.

Update 1 — pehle score, phir margin:

score = 0.5(-1) + 0.2(1.0) + (-0.4)(2.0) = -0.5 + 0.2 - 0.8 = -1.1 margin = (+1) × (-1.1) = -1.1 → NEGATIVE, to update karo Δw = 0.5 × (+1) × [-1, 1.0, 2.0] = [-0.5, 0.5, 1.0] w(1) = [0.5, 0.2, -0.4] + [-0.5, 0.5, 1.0] = [0.0, 0.7, 0.6]

Update 2 — ab w(1) se shuru:

score = 0.0(-1) + 0.7(2.0) + 0.6(-1.0) = 1.4 - 0.6 = +0.8 margin = (-1) × (+0.8) = -0.8 → NEGATIVE, update karo Δw = 0.5 × (-1) × [-1, 2.0, -1.0] = [+0.5, -1.0, +0.5] ╵╵╵╵ ╵╵╵╵ -1 ka ulta -1 ka ulta w(2) = [0.0, 0.7, 0.6] + [0.5, -1.0, 0.5] = [0.5, -0.3, 1.1]

Dhyan: y = −1 ke saath har entry ka nishaan palat jaata hai — to −1 waali entry +0.5 ban gayi.

Verification — zaroor likhein:

x₁ par: -0.5 - 0.3 + 2.2 = +1.4, margin +1.4 ✓ x₂ par: -0.5 - 0.6 - 1.1 = -2.2, margin +2.2 ✓

Dono theek ho gaye — yaani algorithm ruk jaayega.

Ek umda baat (extra marks): dekhein w0 kaise badla — 0.5 → 0.0 → 0.5. Yaani threshold bhi seekha jaa raha hai, baaqi weights ke saath saath. Yehi augmented tareeqe ka poora maqsad tha (G15): threshold ko alag se sambhalne ki zaroorat nahi rehti.

Asli Q46 marksbiological neuron model

Neuron fire karta hai ya nahi?

A biological neuron model calculates its cell body activation sum as a = Σi=13 xiwi and fires (output = 1) if activation exceeds threshold t (a > t), otherwise remaining inactive (output = 0). The current input signals are x = [1.5, 0.8, −2.0]T and synaptic connection weights are w = [0.6, 0.5, −0.4]T, with threshold t = 1.80.

Hint — ek chhoti si chaal

  • Teesra term dhyan se. x3 = −2.0 aur w3 = −0.4 — dono negative, to zarb POSITIVE (+0.80). Yahan minus laga dein to a = 0.50 aata hai aur jawab poora ulta ho jaata hai.
  • Yahan bias NAHI hai — formula sirf Σxiwi hai (Q-2 ke bar-khilaf, jahan +b tha). Sawal ghaur se padhein.

English — paper par likhein

Step 1 — activation level:

a = Σ xiwi = (1.5)(0.6) + (0.8)(0.5) + (-2.0)(-0.4) = 0.90 + 0.40 + 0.80 = 2.10

Step 2 — compare with the threshold:

a = 2.10 > t = 1.80 → the neuron FIRES OUTPUT = 1

Justification to add: the third term is positive (+0.80) because a negative input meets a negative weight — an inhibitory signal through an inhibitory synapse excites the neuron. It is precisely this term that lifts a from 1.30 to 2.10 and across the threshold; without it the neuron would not fire.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: a = 2.10, aur 2.10 > 1.80, to neuron FIRE karta hai — output = 1.

1.5 × 0.6 = 0.90 0.8 × 0.5 = 0.40 -2.0 × -0.4 = +0.80 ← DHYAN: minus × minus = PLUS ------ a = 2.10

Teesra term sab se ahem hai. Dekhein agar wahan ghalti ho jaaye:

Sahi : 0.90 + 0.40 + 0.80 = 2.10 > 1.80 → FIRES (output 1) Ghalat : 0.90 + 0.40 - 0.80 = 0.50 < 1.80 → nahi (output 0)

Yaani ek nishaan ki ghalti poora jawab ulta kar deti hai.

Wajah likhein: negative input (−2.0) aur negative weight (−0.4) mil kar neuron ko uksaate hain, dabate nahi. Aur haqeeqat mein yehi term usay threshold ke paar le gaya — is ke bagair a = 1.30 hota aur neuron fire na karta.

Asli Q56 marksMCQ — distance + classification

2D perceptron — origin se faasla aur do points

A 2D perceptron classifier employs the decision rule: output = 1 if (w1x1 + w2x2) > t, else 0. Model weights are w = [3.0, −4.0]T and the threshold is t = 10.0. Evaluate:
1. The shortest perpendicular Euclidean distance from the origin (0,0) to the decision boundary 3x1 − 4x2 − 10 = 0.
2. The model classification for query points P = (2.0, −2.0) and Q = (1.0, 1.0).
A) Distance = 2.0; Point P fires (output 1), Point Q does not fire (output 0)
B) Distance = 2.5; Both Point P and Point Q fire (output 1)
C) Distance = 1.0; Point P does not fire (output 0), Point Q fires (output 1)

Hint — MCQ ko chaalaki se hal karein

  • Ye poore paper ka wahid MCQ hai. Teeno options mein distance alag hai — to sirf distance nikaal lein, jawab foran mil jaayega. Classification check karna sirf tasdeeq ke liye hai.
  • Examiner ka tohfa: ‖w‖ = √(3² + 4²) = √25 = 5 — poora number (3-4-5 triangle). Agar jawab saaf na aaye to kahin ghalti hai.
  • MCQ hone ke bawajood poora hisaab likhein — paper ki instruction hai "show calculations". Sirf "A" likhne par poore marks shayad na milein.

English — paper par likhein

Part 1 — perpendicular distance from the origin:

Boundary: 3x₁ - 4x₂ - 10 = 0 Distance from (p,q) to Ax+By+C = 0 is |Ap+Bq+C| / √(A²+B²) With (0,0), A = 3, B = -4, C = -10 : | 3(0) - 4(0) - 10 | 10 10 d = -------------------- = ------ = --- = 2.0 √( 3² + (-4)² ) √25 5

Equivalently d = |t| / ‖w‖2 = 10/5 = 2.0.

Part 2 — classify the two points:

P = (2.0, -2.0): 3(2.0) - 4(-2.0) = 6 + 8 = 14 14 > 10 → FIRES, output = 1 Q = (1.0, 1.0): 3(1.0) - 4(1.0) = 3 - 4 = -1 -1 ≯ 10 → does NOT fire, output = 0

Answer: option A — Distance = 2.0; P fires (output 1), Q does not fire (output 0).

Justification to add — signed distances. Dividing each margin by ‖w‖ = 5 gives the point's distance from the boundary: P: (14−10)/5 = +0.8, Q: (−1−10)/5 = −2.2. So Q is rejected decisively (2.2 away on the negative side) while P is accepted only narrowly (0.8) — P is the less reliable of the two classifications.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: Option A — distance 2.0, P fire karta hai, Q nahi.

Hissa 1 — faasla. Seedha formula d = |t| / ‖w‖:

‖w‖ = √(3² + (-4)²) = √(9 + 16) = √25 = 5 d = 10 / 5 = 2.0

Dhyan: (−4)² = +16 hai, −16 nahi.

Hissa 2 — dono points check karein:

P = (2.0, -2.0): 3(2) - 4(-2) = 6 + 8 = 14 ╵╵╵ -4 × -2 = +8 (minus × minus) 14 > 10 → FIRE (output 1) Q = (1.0, 1.0): 3(1) - 4(1) = 3 - 4 = -1 -1 < 10 → fire NAHI (output 0)

MCQ ki chaalaki

Teeno options mein distance alag hai (2.0, 2.5, 1.0). To sirf 10/5 = 2.0 nikaalein — jawab A foran mil gaya, classification check kiye bagair. Magar hisaab phir bhi poora likhein, kyunki paper kehta hai "show calculations".

Ek umda baat (extra marks): har point ka boundary se apna faasla bhi nikal sakta hai — score mein se 10 ghata kar 5 se taqseem:

P: (14 - 10)/5 = +0.8 → haan, magar BILKUL kinaare par Q: ( -1 - 10)/5 = -2.2 → nahi, aur BOHAT SAAF taur par nahi

Yaani Q ka faisla P se kahin zyada pakka hai. P boundary ke paas hai, to zara se shor (noise) par uska jawab palat sakta hai — wohi margin ki size wali baat.

Asli Q66 marksReLU vs linear activation

Two-layer network — ReLU ke saath aur bagair

Consider a 2-layer neural network f(x) = W2φ(W1x) mapping R2 → R with parameters W1 = [[1.0, −2.0], [−2.0, 3.0]], W2 = [2.0, 1.0], x = [4.0, 1.0]T. Evaluate the scalar network output f(x) under two structural regimes:
1. Non-linear ReLU activation function φ(z) = max(0, z).
2. Identity (linear activation), collapsing the architecture into flinear(x) = W2W1x.

Hint — sawal khud jawab ka raasta bata raha hai

  • z = W1x dono case mein wohi hai — ek baar nikaalein, dono jagah istemaal karein.
  • Sawal ne khud likh diya hai flinear(x) = W2W1x — yaani examiner chahta hai ke aap G7 ka "collapse" waala nuqta dikhayein. To W2W1 alag se nikaal kar bhi dikhayein — yahan asli marks hain.
  • Bias koi nahi hai — seedha matrix multiply.

English — paper par likhein

Step 1 — pre-activation z = W1x (common to both):

z₁ = 1.0(4.0) + (-2.0)(1.0) = 4.0 - 2.0 = 2.0 z₂ = -2.0(4.0) + 3.0 (1.0) = -8.0 + 3.0 = -5.0 z = ( 2.0, -5.0 )

Regime 1 — with ReLU:

φ(z) = ( max(0,2.0), max(0,-5.0) ) = ( 2.0, 0 ) f(x) = W₂φ(z) = 2.0(2.0) + 1.0(0) = 4.0 + 0 = 4.0

Regime 2 — identity activation:

flinear(x) = W₂W₁x W₂W₁ = [2.0, 1.0] · [[1.0,-2.0],[-2.0,3.0]] = [ 2.0(1.0)+1.0(-2.0) , 2.0(-2.0)+1.0(3.0) ] = [ 0 , -1.0 ] flinear(x) = [0, -1.0] · [4.0, 1.0] = 0(4.0) + (-1.0)(1.0) = -1.0 Check directly: W₂z = 2.0(2.0) + 1.0(-5.0) = 4.0 - 5.0 = -1.0 ✓

Answer: with ReLU f(x) = 4.0; with linear activation f(x) = −1.0.

Two points worth stating. (i) Without the nonlinearity the entire two-layer network is exactly the single linear predictor with weight vector [0, −1] — which ignores x1 completely, so the depth has bought nothing. (ii) The two outputs differ in sign (+4 vs −1), so under ±1 un-embedding the two models assign this input to opposite classes. The activation function is not a detail — it changes the decision.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: ReLU ke saath f(x) = 4.0; linear ke saath f(x) = −1.0.

Qadam 1 — z nikaalein (dono ke liye wohi):

pehli row [1.0, -2.0] × [4.0, 1.0]: 4.0 - 2.0 = 2.0 doosri row [-2.0, 3.0] × [4.0, 1.0]: -8.0 + 3.0 = -5.0 z = (2.0, -5.0)

Qadam 2A — ReLU: 2.0 bach gaya, −5.0 zero ho gaya.

φ(z) = (2.0, 0) f = 2.0(2.0) + 1.0(0) = 4.0

Qadam 2B — linear: z waisa hi aage.

f = 2.0(2.0) + 1.0(-5.0) = 4.0 - 5.0 = -1.0

Asli marks yahan hain — collapse dikhayein

Sawal ne khud flinear(x) = W2W1x likha hai, yaani wo chahta hai ke aap do matrices ko ek bana kar dikhayein:

W₂W₁ = [2.0, 1.0] · [[1.0,-2.0],[-2.0,3.0]] = [0, -1.0] f(x) = [0, -1.0] · [4.0, 1.0] = -1.0 ✓ wohi jawab

Yaani poora 2-layer network, ReLU ke bagair, bas ek linear predictor w = [0, −1] hai. Aur dekhein — x1 ka weight zero hai, yaani wo pehle feature ko bilkul nazarandaz kar deta hai!

Sab se ahem nuktah: dono jawabon ka nishaan mukhtalif hai — +4 aur −1. ±1 embedding mein un-embedding sirf sign dekhti hai (H15), to ReLU wala model class +1 kehta hai aur linear wala class −1. Activation function faisla hi palat deta hai.

Asli Q76 markshubristic loss, kappa, convexity

Cost-sensitive hubristic loss — aur sigmoid se behtar kyun

In cost-sensitive Boolean classification (labels y ∈ {−1, +1}) with false-negative penalty κ = 3.0, the Hubristic loss (Huber + logistic) is defined for negative examples as:
ℓ(ŷ, −1) = 0 if ŷ < −1;  (ŷ+1)² if −1 ≤ ŷ ≤ 0;  1 + 2ŷ if ŷ > 0
and for positive examples as ℓ(ŷ, +1) = κℓ(−ŷ, −1). A linear predictor produces score ŷ = −0.40. Evaluate:
1. The Hubristic loss ℓ(ŷ, −1) if the true label is negative (y = −1).
2. The cost-sensitive Hubristic loss ℓ(ŷ, +1) if the true label is positive (y = +1).
3. Why Hubristic loss is preferred over Sigmoid loss ℓsig(ŷ, −1) = 1/(1+e−ŷ) during RERM training.

Hint — sab se bari phisalan

  • Part 2 mein −ŷ daalna hai, ŷ nahi! ℓ(ŷ,+1) = κℓ(−ŷ,−1) — yaani ŷ = −0.40 ke liye piecewise function ko +0.40 par dekhna hai. Is se teesra tukda lagta hai, beech wala nahi. Yahan taqreeban sab ghalti karte hain.
  • Part 3 sab se zyada marks ka hai, aur jawab ek lafz hai: convexity. Us par poora paragraph likhein.
  • ŷ ki value tasveer mein chhup gayi hai — yahan docx waali value −0.40 li gayi hai. Agar paper mein koi aur number ho, to tareeqa bilkul wohi rahega, bas numbers badal jaayenge.

English — paper par likhein

Part 1 — true label negative, ŷ = −0.40:

-0.40 lies in [-1, 0] → middle piece (ŷ+1)² ℓ(-0.40, -1) = (-0.40 + 1)² = (0.60)² = 0.36

Part 2 — true label positive, ŷ = −0.40:

ℓ(ŷ, +1) = κ · ℓ(-ŷ, -1) = 3.0 · ℓ(+0.40, -1) +0.40 > 0 → third piece 1 + 2ŷ ℓ(+0.40, -1) = 1 + 2(0.40) = 1.80 ℓ(-0.40, +1) = 3.0 × 1.80 = 5.40

Answer: ℓ(ŷ,−1) = 0.36,  ℓ(ŷ,+1) = 5.40 — 15× larger, because −0.40 is a false negative and κ = 3.0 triples its penalty.

Part 3 — why Hubristic is preferred over Sigmoid in RERM. The decisive reason is convexity:

  • Hubristic is convex — its slope is non-decreasing (0, then 2(ŷ+1) ∈ [0,2], then constant 2). Sigmoid loss is not convex; it is S-shaped.
  • With a convex loss and convex regularizer the RERM objective is convex, so it has a single global minimum reachable efficiently and reliably. A non-convex sigmoid loss admits many local minima: the optimizer can stall in one, the result depends on initialisation, and there is no optimality guarantee.
  • Hubristic is differentiable everywhere — at ŷ = 0 both pieces give value 1 and slope 2 — so gradients are well defined, unlike hinge loss which has a kink.
  • It keeps both properties that matter: exactly zero for confidently correct predictions (ŷ < −1), and only linear growth for large errors, making it robust to outliers (square loss grows quadratically).

One-line summary: sigmoid loss is the closer approximation to the Neyman-Pearson loss, but Hubristic is convex, differentiable and robust — and in RERM, solvability beats closeness of approximation.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: ℓ(ŷ,−1) = 0.36 aur ℓ(ŷ,+1) = 5.40.

Part 1. −0.40 kahan girta hai? [−1, 0] ke darmiyan → beech wala tukda:

(-0.40 + 1)² = (0.60)² = 0.36

Part 2 — yahan sab se bari ghalti hoti hai

Formula hai ℓ(ŷ,+1) = κ · ℓ(−ŷ, −1) — andar −ŷ hai!

ŷ = −0.40, to −ŷ = +0.40. Aur +0.40 > 0 hai, to teesra tukda (1 + 2ŷ) lagega — beech wala nahi!

ℓ(+0.40, -1) = 1 + 2(0.40) = 1.80 ℓ(-0.40, +1) = 3.0 × 1.80 = 5.40

Muqabla karein: 0.36 vs 5.40 — 15 guna farq. Do wajuhat: (i) prediction negative hai magar asli class positive thi → false negative; (ii) κ = 3.0 saza teen guna kar deta hai.

Part 3 — ek lafz mein jawab: CONVEXITY.

  • Hubristic convex hai — uski dhalaan barhti jaati hai (0 → 0..2 → sthir 2), kabhi kam nahi hoti. Sigmoid loss convex NAHI — wo "S" ki shakal hai.
  • Convex loss + convex regularizer = poora RERM convex → ek hi sab se neeche wala nuqta, aur wo yaqeeni mil jaata hai. Sigmoid ke saath kai local minima — optimizer chhote garhe mein phans sakta hai, aur jawab shuruati guess par depend karega.
  • Hubristic har jagah differentiable bhi hai — ŷ = 0 par dono tukde value 1 aur slope 2 dete hain, to moad nahi. (Hinge mein moad hota hai.)
  • Aur do khoobiyan qaim: yaqeeni sahi predictions par bilkul zero, aur bari ghaltiyon par sirf linear barhna → outliers se mehfooz.

Khulasa: sigmoid asli NP loss ki behtar naqal hai — magar hubristic convex, differentiable aur robust hai. RERM mein "hal ho jaana" "naqal achi hona" se zyada ahem hai (I2).

Asli Q86 markshyperplane + signed distance

Decision hyperplane, signed distance, aur un-embedded class

In multi-class nearest-neighbor classification, two prototype embedding vectors are given: a = [4.0, 2.0]T (Class 1) and b = [0.0, −2.0]T (Class 2). Evaluate:
1. The analytical equation of the decision hyperplane H separating Class 1 and Class 2, defined by ‖ŷ − a‖2 = ‖ŷ − b‖2.
2. The signed distance D(ŷ, a, b) for query prediction ŷ = [1.0, 3.0]T, and determine the un-embedded class.

Hint — formula aur ek muft check

  • Part 2 mein sirf class nahi, signed distance bhi maanga gaya hai — yaani J3 ka poora formula chahiye, sirf do faasle compare karna kaafi nahi.
  • Muft verification: hyperplane ko hamesha midpoint (a+b)/2 = [2, 0] se guzarna chahiye. 20 second ka check, poore marks bachata hai.
  • Nishaan ka matlab yaad rakhein: D < 0 ka matlab a ke qareeb (Class 1), D > 0 ka matlab b ke qareeb (Class 2).

English — paper par likhein

Part 1 — the decision hyperplane. From ‖ŷ−a‖² = ‖ŷ−b‖² the ‖ŷ‖² terms cancel, leaving

2(b-a)Tŷ - ‖b‖₂² + ‖a‖₂² = 0 b - a = [0-4, -2-2] = [-4, -4] ‖a‖₂² = 4² + 2² = 20 ‖b‖₂² = 0² + (-2)² = 4 2(-4)ŷ₁ + 2(-4)ŷ₂ - 4 + 20 = 0 -8ŷ₁ - 8ŷ₂ + 16 = 0 Divide by -8 : H : ŷ₁ + ŷ₂ - 2 = 0 i.e. ŷ₁ + ŷ₂ = 2

Checks: midpoint (a+b)/2 = [2,0] satisfies it (2+0 = 2 ✓); normal direction a−b = [4,4] ∝ [1,1] matches the coefficients ✓.

Part 2 — signed distance of ŷ = [1.0, 3.0]T:

2(b-a)Tŷ - ‖b‖² + ‖a‖² D(ŷ,a,b) = ---------------------------- 2 ‖b - a‖₂ numerator = 2(-4)(1.0) + 2(-4)(3.0) - 4 + 20 = -8 - 24 + 16 = -16 ‖b-a‖₂ = √(16+16) = √32 = 4√2 denominator = 2(4√2) = 8√2 ≈ 11.3137 D = -16 / 11.3137 = -1.4142 = -√2

D < 0 ⇒ ŷ is closer to a ⇒ un-embedded class is Class 1.

Cross-check by direct distances: ‖ŷ−a‖² = (1−4)²+(3−2)² = 10 and ‖ŷ−b‖² = (1−0)²+(3+2)² = 26. Since 10 < 26, the query is closer to a — Class 1, agreeing with the sign of D. The magnitude √2 ≈ 1.41 is the query's true perpendicular distance from H.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: H: ŷ1 + ŷ2 = 2;  D = −√2 ≈ −1.414;  class = Class 1.

Part 1 — hyperplane. Teen cheezein nikaalein, formula mein daal dein:

b - a = [-4, -4] ‖a‖² = 16 + 4 = 20 ‖b‖² = 0 + 4 = 4 -8ŷ₁ - 8ŷ₂ - 4 + 20 = 0 -8ŷ₁ - 8ŷ₂ + 16 = 0 -8 se taqseem: ŷ₁ + ŷ₂ = 2

Ye check zaroor karein — 20 second ka kaam

Boundary ko midpoint se guzarna chahiye: (a+b)/2 = [2, 0]. Daal kar dekhein: 2 + 0 = 2 ✓ theek. Aur normal vector a−b = [4,4], jo [1,1] ke mutanasib hai — hamare coefficients bhi 1, 1 hain ✓

Part 2 — signed distance. Ab J3 ka poora formula lagayein (sirf do faasle compare karna kaafi nahi, kyunki number maanga gaya hai):

OOPAR : 2(-4)(1.0) + 2(-4)(3.0) - 4 + 20 = -8 - 24 + 16 = -16 NEECHE : 2 × ‖b-a‖ = 2 × √(16+16) = 2 × √32 = 2 × 5.657 = 11.314 D = -16 / 11.314 = -1.414 (yaani -√2)

Nishaan padhein: D negative hai → ŷ a ke zyada qareeb hai → Class 1.

Doosre tareeqe se check karein (likhein to acha hai):

a se faasla²: (1-4)² + (3-2)² = 9 + 1 = 10 b se faasla²: (1-0)² + (3+2)² = 1 + 25 = 26 ╵╵╵╵ 3 - (-2) = 5, phir 25 10 < 26 → a ke qareeb → Class 1 ✓ wohi jawab

Dono tareeqon ka jawab hamesha milega, kyunki hyperplane wohi nearest-neighbour boundary hai (H18). Aur D ki size (1.414) batati hai ke query boundary se kitni door hai — yaani faisla kitna pakka hai.

Asli Q96 marks3-class confusion matrix

Accuracy, precision aur recall — N = 200

A 3-class classification model is evaluated on a test set of N = 200 samples. The resulting confusion matrix C (where row i is the true class and column j is the predicted class) is:
C = [[70, 6, 4], [5, 50, 5], [2, 8, 50]]
Calculate accuracy, precision and recall.

Hint — sab se ahem baat sawal ki pehli line mein hai

  • "Row i is the TRUE class, column j is the PREDICTED class" — ye lecture ke convention ka ULTA (transpose) hai. Sawal jo kehta hai wohi maanein, warna precision aur recall aapas mein badal jaayenge. (Lecture khud warning deti hai: "some people use the transpose of C".)
  • Is convention ke saath: recall → row ka total se taqseem; precision → column ka total se taqseem.
  • Teen classes hain, to precision aur recall bhi TEEN hain — ek number likh dena adhoora jawab hai.
  • Pehla check: saari entries jama karein — 200 aana chahiye, jaisa sawal kehta hai. Agar na aaye to kahin number ghalat padha hai.

English — paper par likhein

Step 1 — totals (rows = true, columns = predicted):

pred1 pred2 pred3 | ROW SUM (true totals) true 1 [ 70 6 4 ] | 80 true 2 [ 5 50 5 ] | 60 true 3 [ 2 8 50 ] | 60 | COL SUM 77 64 59 | N = 200 ✓ (77+64+59 = 200)

Step 2 — accuracy:

trace = 70 + 50 + 50 = 170 accuracy = 170 / 200 = 0.85 = 85% (error rate = 30/200 = 15%)

Step 3 — recall per class (correct / row sum):

Class 1: 70/80 = 0.8750 = 87.50% Class 2: 50/60 = 0.8333 = 83.33% Class 3: 50/60 = 0.8333 = 83.33%

Step 4 — precision per class (correct / column sum):

Class 1: 70/77 = 0.9091 = 90.91% Class 2: 50/64 = 0.7813 = 78.13% Class 3: 50/59 = 0.8475 = 84.75%

Macro-averages: recall = 84.72%, precision = 84.59%.

Interpretation to add. Class 1 is the strongest (recall 87.50%, precision 90.91%) — it is both found reliably and predicted reliably. Class 2 has the weakest precision (78.13%): when we predict class 2 we are wrong most often, because 6 class-1 and 8 class-3 samples leak into it — C32 = 8 is the largest off-diagonal entry. Classes 2 and 3 have identical recall (83.33%), so the dominant problem is over-prediction of class 2 rather than class 3 being hard to find.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: accuracy 85%; recall 87.50 / 83.33 / 83.33%; precision 90.91 / 78.13 / 84.75%.

Pehle ye tay karein — warna poora jawab ulta

Sawal kehta hai rows = TRUE, columns = PREDICTED — lecture ka ulta. To:

RECALL → ROW ke total se taqseem (kitne ASAL mein thay) PRECISION → COLUMN ke total se taqseem (kitne HUM NE kahe)

Qadam 1 — totals (ye qadam chhodne par sab ghalat):

Row sums (asli ginti) : 80, 60, 60 → jama = 200 ✓ Col sums (hamari ginti): 77, 64, 59 → jama = 200 ✓

Dono ka total 200 aaya — bilkul wohi jo sawal ne kaha. Ye pehla aur sab se zaroori check hai.

Qadam 2 — accuracy (diagonal jama, N se taqseem):

(70 + 50 + 50) / 200 = 170/200 = 85%

Qadam 3 — recall ("jo thay, un mein se kitne pakde"):

Class 1: 70/80 = 87.50% Class 2: 50/60 = 83.33% Class 3: 50/60 = 83.33%

Qadam 4 — precision ("jo kaha, un mein se kitne sach thay"):

Class 1: 70/77 = 90.91% Class 2: 50/64 = 78.13% ← sab se kamzor Class 3: 50/59 = 84.75%

Dhyan dein denominator kaise badalta hai: Class 1 ke liye recall mein 80 (row) aur precision mein 77 (column) — oopar wala number (70) wohi rehta hai, sirf neeche wala badalta hai. Yehi dono ka poora farq hai (H6).

Tajziya likhein (yahan asli marks hain):
Class 1 sab se mazboot hai — recall 87.50% aur precision 90.91%, yaani wo acchi tarah pakdi bhi jaati hai aur jab kahi jaaye to sach bhi hoti hai.
Class 2 ki precision sab se kam (78.13%) — jab hum "class 2" kehte hain to sab se zyada ghalat hote hain, kyunki class 1 ke 6 aur class 3 ke 8 samples us mein ghus aate hain. C32 = 8 poore matrix ka sab se bada off-diagonal number hai.
Class 2 aur 3 ka recall bilkul barabar hai (83.33%) — to asal masla "class 3 dhoondna mushkil hai" nahi, balki "class 2 zaroorat se zyada predict ho rahi hai" hai. Ye farq likhne par poore marks milte hain.

Q10

Ek sawal jo abhi tak nahi mila

Tasveeron se Q1 se Q9 tak sab mil gaye. Sirf Q-10 kisi tasveer mein nahi aaya — aur docx mein bhi wo "illegible" likha tha. Is ka hal main bana kar nahi likh raha; magar ye batana mufeed hai ke kaunsa topic bach gaya hai.

Q-10 kis topic se ho sakta hai — jo bacha hai

Q1–Q9 ne jo cover kar liya wo nikaal dein, to Lecture 6/7/8 mein se ye bachta hai (tarteeb imkaan ke hisaab se):

TopicKyun ghalib imkaan haiTayyari yahan se
Parameter counting
(di-1+1)×di		Sab se mazboot imkaan. Ye sample paper ka Q-2 tha, Lecture-6 ka markazi formula hai, aur asli paper ke nau sawalon mein bilkul nahi aaya — halanke paper ka do tihai Lecture-6 se hai.G8 · sample Q-2 · sawal 2, sawal 3, sawal 9
Neyman-Pearson metric
(κ·fn + fp)/n		Q-7 mein κ ka sirf loss waala roop aaya — metric waala nahi. Boolean confusion matrix de kar NP error poochna aasaan 6-marks sawal hai.H7 · H10 · sawal 53, sawal 78
One-hot embedding
aur argmax un-embedding		Q-8 ne nearest-neighbour ka geometry wala hissa liya, magar one-hot aur argmax nahi aaya. ŷ vector de kar class poochna sab se saada numerical hai.H12 · H16 · sawal 81, sawal 82
Hinge / logistic loss ka hisaabQ-7 mein hubristic aaya. Hinge aur logistic (yaani SVM aur logistic regression ke losses) bach gaye — aur wo sample paper ke Q-4 mein thay.I6 · I7 · I9 · sawal 36, sawal 37

Agar Q-10 ki saaf tasveer mil jaaye (ya Q-2 ka teesra activation function), to main usay bhi isi tarah poora hal kar ke yahan laga doonga.

Is paper se 6 sabaq

  1. Paper poora numerical hai. Nau ke nau sawal hisaab ke hain — sirf Q-7 ke teesre hisse mein "why" hai. Rata lagane se kaam nahi chalega.
  2. Lecture-6 ka wazan sab se zyada hai — Q1 se Q6 tak, yaani do tihai paper. Forward pass, activation functions aur perceptron update sab se zyada practice maangte hain.
  3. Minus ke nishaan sab se bade dushman hain. Q1 (−1.5 × −2.0 = +3.0), Q2 (max(−0.1, −1.0) = −0.1), Q3 (x0 = −1), Q4 (−2.0 × −0.4 = +0.80), Q5 (−4 × −2 = +8), Q8 (3 − (−2) = 5) — chhe sawalon mein nishaan ki phisalan hai. Hamesha bracket laga kar likhein.
  4. Sawal ki pehli line convention batati hai. Q9 mein "rows are true classes", Q3 mein "x0 = −1", Q1 mein "linear output layer" — teeno jagah ek lafz nazarandaz karne par poora jawab ghalat ho jaata hai.
  5. Verification likhein. Q3 mein dono points dobara check karna, Q9 mein row/column totals ka 200 aana, Q8 mein midpoint check — ye chhote checks ghalti pakad lete hain aur marks bhi dete hain.
  6. Har jawab ke baad ek line wajah. Paper ki apni instruction hai: "provide brief technical justifications". Q6 mein W2W1 = [0,−1] dikhana, Q1 mein "ReLU ne ek ek neuron band kiya" likhna, Q9 mein "class 2 zyada predict ho rahi hai" batana — yahan asli marks hain.

Numerical sawal mein poore marks lene ka tareeqa — 5 qadam

QADAM 1 FORMULA pehle likho — number daalne se pehle. Agar hisaab mein ghalti ho jaaye, formula ke marks bach jaate hain. QADAM 2 HAR qadam alag line par — z, phir h, phir f(x). Sirf aakhri number likhne par aadhe marks katte hain. QADAM 3 NUMBERS DAAL KAR dikhao — "1.5(2.0) + 1.0(-1.0)" likho, seedha "2.0" nahi. QADAM 4 JAWAB ko ghera do — aur units/shakal batao (vector hai ya scalar). QADAM 5 EK LINE ki WAJAH likho — "ReLU ne doosra neuron band kar diya", "margin negative tha is liye update hua". Rubric mein "technical justification" yehi hai.

Waqt: 10 sawal, 60 minute — yaani 6 minute per sawal. Numerical sawal 4–5 minute mein ho jaate hain, to conceptual ke liye waqt bach jaata hai. Kisi ek sawal par 8 minute se zyada na lagayein.
