OHT-II Sample Paper — November 2026

Chaaron sawal, poore hal ke saath

Max marks: 10 × 6 = 60. Waqt: 18:30–19:30, second shift. Rubrics: problem analysis, design capability, technical justification.

OHT-II Q-16 marksforward pass, ReLU

2-layer network ka forward pass — z, h, aur f(x)

A 2-layer fully connected feedforward network (one hidden layer) without biases is defined by f(x) = W2 max(0, W1x). The input vector and weight matrices are given as:
x = (2.0, −1.0),   W1 = [[1.5, 1.0], [−2.0, 0.5]],   W2 = [0.8, −1.2]
What is the intermediate pre-activation vector z = W1x, the hidden activation vector h = ReLU(z), and the final scalar output f(x)?

Hint — sawal mein chhupe ishaare

  • "without biases" — ye sab se ahem lafz hai. Iska matlab: (1, z) wala 1 chipkana nahi hai. Seedha W1x karein, b + W1x nahi.
  • "2-layer … (one hidden layer)" — examiner ne bracket mein khud kholl diya. Do matrices hain (W1, W2), teen nahi. (G9)
  • Teen cheezein maangi gayi hain — z, h, f(x). Teeno alag alag likhni hain, warna marks katenge.
  • W2 ek row vector hai, is liye output scalar hoga — sawal mein khud likha hai "final scalar output".

English — paper par likhein

Step 1 — Pre-activation z = W1x:

z₁ = 1.5(2.0) + 1.0(-1.0) = 3.0 - 1.0 = 2.0 z₂ = -2.0(2.0) + 0.5(-1.0) = -4.0 - 0.5 = -4.5 z = ( 2.0, -4.5 )

Step 2 — Hidden activation h = ReLU(z), applied elementwise:

h₁ = max(0, 2.0) = 2.0 h₂ = max(0, -4.5) = 0.0 h = ( 2.0, 0.0 )

Step 3 — Output f(x) = W2h:

f(x) = 0.8(2.0) + (-1.2)(0.0) = 1.60 + 0 = 1.60

Answer: z = (2.0, −4.5),  h = (2.0, 0.0),  f(x) = 1.60.

Justification to add: the second hidden unit has a negative pre-activation (−4.5), so ReLU switches it off (h2 = 0). Its output weight −1.2 therefore contributes nothing, and the output is determined entirely by the first hidden unit. This is the nonlinearity doing its work — a linear network could not switch a unit off.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: z = (2.0, −4.5),  h = (2.0, 0.0),  f(x) = 1.60

Qadam 1 — z nikaalein. Ye bas matrix ko vector se multiply karna hai. Qaida: matrix ki ek row utha kar, vector ke saath jod kar jama kar do.

pehli row [1.5, 1.0] ke saath x = [2.0, -1.0]: 1.5 × 2.0 = 3.0 1.0 × -1.0 = -1.0 jama = 2.0 ← z₁ doosri row [-2.0, 0.5] ke saath: -2.0 × 2.0 = -4.0 0.5 × -1.0 = -0.5 jama = -4.5 ← z₂

Qadam 2 — ReLU lagayein. ReLU chowkidar hai: negative ko zero, positive ko jaane do. Aur ye har number par alag alag lagta hai:

2.0 positive tha → waisa hi raha → 2.0 -4.5 negative tha → ZERO ho gaya → 0.0

Qadam 3 — output. Phir wahi multiply-aur-jama:

0.8 × 2.0 = 1.60 -1.2 × 0.0 = 0.00 jama = 1.60

Yaad rakhein: dekhein ke doosre neuron ka weight −1.2 tha — aur wo bilkul bekaar gaya, kyunki ReLU ne us neuron ko band kar diya tha (h2 = 0, aur 0 se multiply = 0). Exam mein ye ek line zaroor likhein — yehi "technical justification" hai aur isi ke marks hain. Aur ye nonlinearity ka asli kaam bhi dikhata hai: linear network kisi neuron ko band nahi kar sakta.

OHT-II Q-26 marksparameter counting

MLP mein kitne trainable parameters hain?

A multi-layer perceptron for multi-class classification has an input dimension of d = 12, a first hidden layer with 32 neurons, a second hidden layer with 16 neurons, and an output layer with 4 classes. If every neuron across all layers includes a learnable scalar bias parameter, what is the total number of trainable parameters in this network?

Hint — sawal mein chhupe ishaare

  • "includes a learnable scalar bias parameter" — yaani +1 lagana hai. (Q-1 mein ulta tha: "without biases".) Ye lafz hi poora jawab badal deta hai — bias ke saath 1,012, bagair 960.
  • Chaar numbers diye hain (12, 32, 16, 4) — yaani teen parameter matrices (har do numbers ke beech ek). Input layer khud koi parameter nahi rakhti.
  • "total" — har layer ka alag nikaal kar jama karna hai. Aur layer-wise breakdown dikhana zaroori hai, warna "design capability" ke marks nahi milte.

English — paper par likhein

Formula. For each layer i, the parameter matrix includes weights and biases, with dimension (di−1 + 1) × di:

Layer 1 (12 → 32): (12+1) × 32 = 13 × 32 = 416 [ 384 weights + 32 biases ] Layer 2 (32 → 16): (32+1) × 16 = 33 × 16 = 528 [ 512 weights + 16 biases ] Layer 3 (16 → 4): (16+1) × 4 = 17 × 4 = 68 [ 64 weights + 4 biases ] ------- Total trainable parameters = 1,012

Answer: 1,012 trainable parameters.

Justification to add: the +1 in each row count accounts for the bias of every neuron in that layer, equivalently the constant input 1 appended to each layer's input in gi(zi−1) = h((Θi)T(1, zi−1)). The activation function contributes no trainable parameters, and the input layer holds none.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: 1,012 parameters.

Formula: har layer ke liye (pichhle neurons + 1) × (is layer ke neurons). Phir sab jama kar do.

Wo +1 kya hai: bias. Dekhein — is layer ke har neuron ka apna ek bias hota hai, aur bias ko aise ginna sab se aasaan hai ke "ek extra input jo hamesha 1 hai" maan lein. Is liye rows mein +1 aa jaata hai.

Layer 1: (12 + 1) × 32 = 416 Layer 2: (32 + 1) × 16 = 528 Layer 3: (16 + 1) × 4 = 68 ----- JAMA 1,012

Alag alag kar ke dekhein (agar sawal mein weights aur biases alag poochey jaayein):

WEIGHTS: 12×32 + 32×16 + 16×4 = 384 + 512 + 64 = 960 BIASES : 32 + 16 + 4 = 52 JAMA : 960 + 52 = 1,012

Chaar ghaltiyan jo log karte hain:
(1) +1 bhool jaate hain → 960 aata hai (ghalat).
(2) Input ko ek layer gin kar chauthi matrix bana dete hain — input layer mein koi parameter nahi hota, wo sirf numbers hain.
(3) ReLU/sigmoid ke parameter gin lete hain — activation function mein koi parameter nahi.
(4) Output layer chhod dete hain — wo bhi poora layer hai, uske bhi weights aur bias hain.

OHT-II Q-36 marksperceptron margin & update

Perceptron — margin aur updated weight vector

A binary perceptron has current augmented weight vector wold = [w0, w1, w2] = [0.4, −0.6, 0.2], where w0 corresponds to a constant bias input x0 = 1. A training sample arrives with feature vector x = [x0, x1, x2] = [1.0, 2.0, 3.0] and true label y = +1. If the learning rate is η = 0.2, what is the classification margin, and what is the updated weight vector wnew after applying the Perceptron Learning Algorithm?

Hint — sawal mein chhupe ishaare

  • "augmented" — bias pehle se vector mein shaamil hai (w0 aur x0 = 1). Yaani aap ko kuch chipkana nahi, sab tayyar hai. (G15)
  • x0 = 1 (not −1) — sawal ne khud bata diya. Jo diya ho wohi use karein.
  • Do cheezein maangi gayi hain — margin aur wnew. Dono likhni hain.
  • Margin pehle nikaalein, phir faisla karein — kyunki update sirf tab hota hai jab margin negative ho. Seedha update kar dena (bina margin check kiye) ghalat tareeqa hai, chahe jawab theek aa jaaye.

English — paper par likhein

Step 1 — Raw prediction field:

woldTx = (0.4)(1.0) + (-0.6)(2.0) + (0.2)(3.0) = 0.4 - 1.2 + 0.6 = -0.20

Step 2 — Classification margin:

margin = y (woldTx) = (+1)(-0.20) = -0.20 < 0

Since the margin is negative, the sample is misclassified and triggers a weight update.

Step 3 — Weight adjustment Δw = η y x:

Δw = 0.2 × (+1) × [1.0, 2.0, 3.0] = [ 0.20, 0.40, 0.60 ]

Step 4 — Update:

wnew = wold + Δw = [ 0.4+0.20, -0.6+0.40, 0.2+0.60 ] = [ 0.60, -0.20, 0.80 ]

Verification:

wnewTx = 0.60(1) - 0.20(2) + 0.80(3) = 0.60 - 0.40 + 2.40 = +2.60 > 0 (now correctly classified)

Answer: margin = −0.20 (misclassified); wnew = [0.60, −0.20, 0.80].

Justification to add: the update moves w in the direction +ηx because y = +1, which provably increases the margin: the new margin equals the old margin + η‖x‖², since y² = 1. Here it rises from −0.20 to +2.60.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: margin = −0.20 (yaani ghalat classify hua), aur wnew = [0.60, −0.20, 0.80].

Qadam 1 — score nikaalein. Dot product: har jodi multiply, phir jama.

0.4 × 1.0 = 0.4 -0.6 × 2.0 = -1.2 0.2 × 3.0 = 0.6 jama = -0.20

Qadam 2 — margin. Formula margin = y × score:

margin = (+1) × (-0.20) = -0.20

Ye negative hai — iska matlab kya? Asli label +1 tha (yaani positive), magar model ka score −0.20 aaya (yaani negative ki taraf). Dono alag taraf hain → model ne ghalti ki → weights badalne padenge.

Qadam 3 — kitna badalna hai. Δw = η y x — yaani learning rate, label, aur feature vector, teeno ka multiply:

0.2 × (+1) × [1.0, 2.0, 3.0] = [0.20, 0.40, 0.60]

Qadam 4 — jama kar dein.

[0.4, -0.6, 0.2] + [0.20, 0.40, 0.60] = [0.60, -0.20, 0.80]

Qadam 5 — VERIFY karein (ye line chhodna nahi hai!):

naya score = 0.60(1) - 0.20(2) + 0.80(3) = 0.60 - 0.40 + 2.40 = +2.60 → POSITIVE

Ab score positive hai, aur label bhi +1 tha — yaani ab point theek classify ho gaya. Margin −0.20 se +2.60 par pohonch gaya.

Yaad rakhein: ye jaadu nahi, riyazi hai. Update ke baad margin hamesha barhta hai, aur wajah ye hai:
naya margin = purana margin + η‖x‖²
Aur η‖x‖² hamesha positive hota hai (kyunki η > 0 aur square hamesha positive). Is liye margin ko barhna hi hai. (Ye derivation G16 mein hai — agar "prove the update increases the margin" poocha jaaye to wahan se likhein.)
Aur verification ki line hamesha likhein — official solution mein bhi likhi hui hai, aur rubric ke "technical justification" ke marks isi mein hain.

OHT-II Q-46 markshinge loss, square loss, kappa

Cost-sensitive hinge loss, square loss, aur square loss ki kharabi

In cost-sensitive Boolean classification (y ∈ {−1, +1}), false negatives are penalized with a relative weight of κ = 4.0 compared to false positives. A linear predictor produces a continuous prediction score y-hat = −0.40.
(1) What is the cost-sensitive Hinge loss if the true label is positive (y = +1)?
(2) What is the Square loss if the true label is negative (y = −1)?
(3) Why does Square loss exhibit undesirable behavior for a confident correct prediction like y-hat = −3.0 when y = −1?

Hint — sawal mein chhupe ishaare

  • Teen hisse hain, aur teesra conceptual hai — pehle do mein sirf hisaab, teesre mein wajah. Teesre ke marks sab se zyada hain, aur log usay jaldi mein chhota kar dete hain. Us par poora paragraph likhein.
  • Part (1) mein y = +1 → formula κ · p(−y-hat) hai, yaani κ lagega.
  • Part (2) mein y = −1 → formula p(y-hat) hai, yaani κ NAHI lagega. Ye sab se aam ghalti hai — log dono mein 4.0 multiply kar dete hain.
  • "confident correct prediction" — yehi part (3) ka poora jawab hai: prediction sahi hai aur poore yaqeen se hai, phir bhi saza mil rahi hai.

English — paper par likhein

(1) Cost-sensitive Hinge loss, y = +1:

ℓ(y-hat, +1) = κ (1 - y-hat)+ = 4.0 × | 1 - (-0.40) |+ = 4.0 × (1.40) = 5.60

(2) Square loss, y = −1:

ℓ(y-hat, -1) = (1 + y-hat)² = (1 + (-0.40))² = (0.60)² = 0.36

(3) Why square loss is undesirable. For a confident correct negative prediction y-hat = −3.0 with y = −1:

ℓ(-3.0, -1) = (1 + (-3.0))² = (-2.0)² = 4.0

Square loss is not monotonically non-increasing in the margin y · y-hat. It penalises any deviation from the representative in both directions, so it treats "too correct" exactly like "wrong":

y-hat = -1.0 (exactly at representative) → loss = 0.00 y-hat = -3.0 (confidently CORRECT) → loss = 4.00 y-hat = +1.0 (clearly WRONG) → loss = 4.00

The confidently correct prediction incurs the same loss (4.0) as the clearly wrong one. During ERM this actively pushes correct, confident predictions back toward the decision boundary, wasting model capacity and distorting the boundary.

What a good classification loss must do: be non-increasing in the margin — flat or zero once the point is correctly classified with sufficient margin. Hinge loss ((1−y·y-hat)+, exactly zero beyond margin 1) and logistic loss (log(1+e−y·y-hat), decaying to zero) both satisfy this; square loss does not.

Add this balancing line: square loss is still used because ERM with it reduces to a least-squares problem solvable exactly in closed form — it is computationally the easiest, but geometrically wrong for classification.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: (1) 5.60  ·  (2) 0.36  ·  (3) kyunki sahi aur yaqeeni prediction par bhi saza lagti hai.

Part (1). Label +1 hai, is liye formula κ · (1 − y-hat)+:

1 - (-0.40) = 1 + 0.40 = 1.40 (minus minus = plus) positive hai, to (·)+ kuch nahi badalta 4.0 × 1.40 = 5.60

Ye itna bada (5.60) kyun hai? Do wajuhat: (i) asli class positive thi magar score negative aaya — yaani ye ek false negative hai; (ii) aur κ = 4.0 is ghalti ki saza chaar guna kar deta hai. Ye line exam mein likhein.

Part (2). Ab label −1 hai, is liye formula (1 + y-hat)² — aur κ nahi lagta:

1 + (-0.40) = 0.60 (0.60)² = 0.36

Sab se bari ghalti — is se bachein

κ sirf positive class par lagta hai (y = +1), negative par nahi. Wajah: κ ka maqsad false negative ki saza barhana hai, aur false negative sirf tab hota hai jab asli class positive ho. Is liye part (2) mein 4.0 se multiply nahi karna.

Part (3) — asal sawal. Pehle hisaab:

ℓ(-3.0, -1) = (1 + (-3.0))² = (-2.0)² = 4.0

Ab socha jaaye ke yahan ho kya raha hai:

  • Asli class negative thi.
  • Model ne −3.0 kaha — yaani bilkul theek taraf, aur bohat poore yaqeen ke saath.
  • Ye to sab se behtareen surat hai!
  • Magar loss 4.0 hai — yaani model ko theek hone par saza di ja rahi hai.

Aur is se bhi bura: muqabla karein —

y-hat = -3.0 → loss 4.0 (yaqeen se SAHI) y-hat = +1.0 → loss 4.0 (bilkul GHALAT) DONO KO SQUARE LOSS BARABAR BURA SAMAJHTA HAI!

Wajah ek line mein: square loss ki shakal pyaali (parabola) hai — wo sirf −1 par zero hoti hai aur dono taraf oopar uthti hai. Dayein taraf uthna theek hai (wo ghalat predictions hain), magar bayein taraf uthna bilkul ghalat hai — wahan prediction aur behtar ho rahi hai.

Amli nuqsan: training (ERM) mein model is saza se bachne ki koshish karega, to wo apni sahi aur yaqeeni predictions ko boundary ki taraf wapas kheench lega — sirf is liye ke loss kam ho. Yaani model ki taqat ghalat jagah kharch hoti hai aur boundary bigad jaati hai.

Acha loss kaisa hona chahiye: margin barhne par loss kabhi barhna nahi chahiye — ek had ke baad usay chapta (ya zero) ho jaana chahiye. Hinge margin 1 ke baad bilkul zero ho jaata hai; logistic zero ki taraf girta rehta hai. Dono theek hain — square loss nahi.

Insaaf ki ek line bhi likhein: square loss phir bhi istemaal hota hai kyunki is ke saath ERM least-squares ban jaata hai, jo formula se seedha (exactly) hal ho jaata hai — sab se aasaan aur tez. Yaani ye hisaab mein behtareen magar classification ke liye geometrically ghalat hai. Ye trade-off likhne par poore marks milte hain.

Chaaron sawalon se kya seekha — baaqi 6 sawalon ki tayyari

Sample paper ka style bilkul saaf hai, aur is se andaza lagaya ja sakta hai ke baaqi sawal kaise honge:

  1. Numbers diye jaate hain, hisaab maanga jaata hai — chaar mein se teen poore numerical hain. Rata lagane se kaam nahi chalega, tareeqa aana chahiye.
  2. Ek ek lafz maayne rakhta hai — "without biases" (Q-1) vs "includes a learnable bias" (Q-2). Ek lafz badla, poora jawab badal gaya. Sawal do baar padhein.
  3. Aakhir mein "why" aata hai — Q-4 ka teesra hissa. Sirf hisaab kaafi nahi, wajah bhi chahiye.
  4. Verification maangi jaati hai — Q-3 ke official solution mein verification ki line likhi hui hai. Numerical ke baad hamesha check kar ke dikhayein.

Lecture 7 par khaas dhyan dein: chaaron sample sawal Lecture 6 (Q-1,2,3) aur Lecture 8 (Q-4) se hain. Lecture 7 se ek bhi nahi (oopar chapter filter mein "LEC 7 (0)" isi liye khaali hai) — yaani confusion matrix, error rate, precision/recall, Neyman-Pearson metric, aur embedding/un-embedding se sawal aane ka imkaan zyada hai, kam nahi. Practice Questions mein in sab par sawal diye gaye hain.

Ye tab kis liye hai. Aap ke paas sirf 2 din hain. 108 sawal karna is waqt mein mumkin bhi nahi aur zaroori bhi nahi. Is liye yahan sirf 37 sawal rakhe gaye hain — wo jo har us qism ko cover karte hain jo paper mein aa sakti hai, bina kisi takraar (repetition) ke.

Ye 37 kaise chune gaye: saara material (Lecture 6, 7, 8 + asli paper + sample paper + un ke solutions) dekh kar har alag qism ka ek sawal rakha gaya. Jahan do sawal ek hi cheez poochte thay, wahan sirf behtar wala rakha. Koi "out of the box" sawal nahi — har sawal seedha lectures ya paper se hai. (Poore 108 sawal abhi bhi Extra Practice tab mein mojood hain, agar waqt bach jaaye.)

In 37 sawalon ki taqseem — paper ke pattern ke mutabiq

SectionPaper meinYahanKaunsi qismein cover hoti hain
A — Neural Network6 sawal
(36 marks)		1–20Forward pass (bias ke saath aur bagair), activation functions, parameter counting, perceptron margin & update, decision boundary ka faasla, dead ReLU, threshold↔bias
B — Boolean Classification3 sawal
(18 marks)		21–31Hubristic, hinge, logistic, square, sigmoid loss ka hisaab; κ; Neyman-Pearson; signed distance aur hyperplane
C — Classifier1 sawal
(6 marks)		32–37Confusion matrix se metrics, precision vs recall, Type I/II, embed→predict→un-embed, argmax

Har sawal par full bank #N likha hai — is ka matlab hai ke wohi sawal Extra Practice tab mein number N par hai, agar us qism ki aur mashq chahiye.

Chapter filter
Section A — sawal 1–20

Neural Network — numericals

Paper mein yahan se 6 sawal aayenge (36 marks). Ye bees sawal NN ki har qism cover karte hain. Agar waqt bilkul kam ho, to sirf ye bees karein — baaqi sab chhod dein.

16 marksfull bank #27asli Q1 jaisa

A 3-layer network with ReLU hidden layers and a linear output layer has x = [2.0, 1.0]T, W1 = [[1.0, −1.0], [0.5, 2.0]], b1 = [0.5, −3.0], W2 = [[2.0, 0.5], [−1.0, 1.0]], b2 = [−1.0, 0.5], W3 = [1.0, −3.0], b3 = 0.5. Compute z1, z2 and ŷ.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

3-layer network hai — do hidden layers par ReLU, aur output layer linear (us par ReLU nahi). Saare matrices aur biases diye hain. Teen cheezein nikaalein: z1, z2, aur aakhri output ŷ.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

W₁x = ( 1.0(2)+(-1.0)(1), 0.5(2)+2.0(1) ) = ( 1.0, 3.0 ) + b₁ = ( 1.0+0.5, 3.0-3.0 ) = ( 1.5, 0.0 ) z¹ = ReLU = ( 1.5, 0 ) W₂z¹ = ( 2.0(1.5)+0.5(0), -1.0(1.5)+1.0(0) ) = ( 3.0, -1.5 ) + b₂ = ( 3.0-1.0, -1.5+0.5 ) = ( 2.0, -1.0 ) z² = ReLU = ( 2.0, 0 ) ŷ = W₃z² + b₃ = 1.0(2.0) + (-3.0)(0) + 0.5 = 2.5

Note: ReLU kills one unit in each hidden layer, so W3's weight −3.0 contributes nothing. No ReLU on the output layer.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: z1 = (1.5, 0), z2 = (2.0, 0), ŷ = 2.5

Har layer par tarteeb: Wx → +b → ReLU. Aakhri layer par ReLU nahi.

Yaad rakhein: dono hidden layers mein ek ek neuron mar gaya (ReLU ne zero kar diya) — is liye W3 ka doosra weight −3.0 bekaar gaya. Ye line likhne par justification ke marks milte hain.

26 marksfull bank #33asli Q6 jaisa

For f(x) = W2φ(W1x) with W1 = [[2, 1], [1, −3]], W2 = [1, 2], x = [1, 2]T: evaluate f(x) with ReLU, and with identity activation. Show the collapsed weight vector.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

2-layer network hai. f(x) do tarah nikaalein — ReLU ke saath, aur identity (linear) activation ke saath. Aur linear case mein collapsed weight vector bhi dikhayein.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

z = W₁x = ( 2(1)+1(2), 1(1)+(-3)(2) ) = ( 4, -5 ) ReLU : φ(z) = (4, 0) → f = 1(4) + 2(0) = 4 Linear : φ(z) = (4, -5) → f = 1(4) + 2(-5) = -6 Collapsed: W₂W₁ = [1(2)+2(1), 1(1)+2(-3)] = [4, -5] f = [4,-5]·[1,2] = 4 - 10 = -6 ✓

Note: the outputs have opposite signs (+4 vs −6), so under ±1 un-embedding the two models predict different classes for the same input.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: ReLU = 4; linear = −6; collapsed W2W1 = [4, −5].

Do baatein likhein: (1) ReLU ke bagair poora network sirf ek linear predictor [4, −5] hai — do layers ka faida zero. (2) Dono jawabon ka nishaan ulta hai, to dono models is input ko alag alag class denge.

36 marksfull bank #28asli Q2 jaisa

A neuron has x = [1.0, 2.0, −1.0]T, w = [2.0, −1.0, 1.0]T, bias b = −1.5, with a = wTx + b. Evaluate the output under Leaky ReLU max(0.1a, a), ELU (α=1), and sigmoid.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Ek neuron hai, bias ke saath. Pehle a nikaalein, phir teen activation functions lagayein: Leaky ReLU, ELU, aur sigmoid.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

a = 2.0(1.0) + (-1.0)(2.0) + 1.0(-1.0) + (-1.5) = 2.0 - 2.0 - 1.0 - 1.5 = -2.5 Leaky ReLU : max(0.1(-2.5), -2.5) = max(-0.25, -2.5) = -0.25 ELU : e^(-2.5) - 1 = 0.0821 - 1 = -0.918 Sigmoid : 1/(1+e^(2.5)) = 1/13.182 = 0.076

Note: all three sit on their negative branch. Leaky ReLU and ELU stay negative; sigmoid maps it into (0,1) and so is always positive.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: a = −2.5; Leaky ReLU −0.25; ELU −0.918; sigmoid 0.076.

a sirf ek baar nikaalein — teeno usi par lagte hain. Bias ko bhoolna nahi.

Dhyan: max(−0.25, −2.5) = −0.25 — bada wo hai jo zero ke qareeb hai. Aur sigmoid hamesha positive jawab deta hai (0 aur 1 ke beech), chahe a kitna bhi negative ho — ye baaqi do se buniyadi farq hai.

46 marksfull bank #29

A neuron has x = [1.0, 1.0]T, w = [2.0, 1.0]T, b = 0.5. Evaluate ReLU, Leaky ReLU, ELU and sigmoid. What do you notice, and why?

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Ek neuron hai. Chaaron activation functions lagayein: ReLU, Leaky ReLU, ELU, sigmoid. Phir batayein ke aap ne kya ghaur kiya aur uski wajah kya hai.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

a = 2.0(1.0) + 1.0(1.0) + 0.5 = 3.5 (POSITIVE) ReLU : max(0, 3.5) = 3.5 Leaky ReLU : max(0.35, 3.5) = 3.5 ELU : a ≥ 0, so h(a) = a = 3.5 Sigmoid : 1/(1+e^(-3.5)) = 0.971

Observation: the three ReLU-family activations give the identical answer 3.5.

Why: ReLU, Leaky ReLU and ELU are all defined to be exactly a on the positive branch — they differ only in how they treat negative inputs. Sigmoid differs because it squashes every input into (0,1); at a = 3.5 it is already near its ceiling of 1, i.e. saturated, so its gradient there is nearly zero.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: a = 3.5; ReLU, Leaky ReLU aur ELU teeno ka jawab 3.5; sigmoid 0.971.

Kya ghaur kiya: ReLU-family ke teeno ka jawab bilkul ek jaisa hai.

Kyun: kyunki teeno ki tareef positive taraf h(a) = a hai — wo sirf negative taraf mein ek doosre se mukhtalif hain. Is liye jab a > 0 ho, teeno bilkul barabar.

Sabaq (ye likhein): Leaky ReLU aur ELU ka poora faida sirf negative taraf hai (dead ReLU se bachna). Aur sigmoid 3.5 par saturate ho chuka hai (0.971, yaani 1 ke bilkul paas) — wahan derivative taqreeban zero hai, jo training sust kar deta hai. Isi liye A4.2 (jahan a < 0 tha) asal mein zyada maaloomati sawal hai.

56 marksfull bank #30asli Q3 jaisa

A perceptron has augmented weights w(0) = [1.0, −0.5, 0.5]T with permanent input x0 = −1, and η = 0.2. Process two samples in sequence: (a) [2.0, 1.0]T with y = −1; (b) [1.0, 3.0]T with y = −1. Give the weight vector after each sample.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Perceptron ke augmented weights diye hain (x0 = −1 ke saath), learning rate 0.2. Do samples ek ke baad ek process karein, aur har sample ke baad weight vector batayein.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

Augmented: x₁ = [-1, 2.0, 1.0], x₂ = [-1, 1.0, 3.0] SAMPLE (a): w(0)Tx₁ = 1.0(-1) + (-0.5)(2.0) + 0.5(1.0) = -1 - 1 + 0.5 = -1.5 margin = (-1)(-1.5) = +1.5 > 0 → CORRECT, NO UPDATE w(1) = w(0) = [1.0, -0.5, 0.5] SAMPLE (b): w(1)Tx₂ = 1.0(-1) + (-0.5)(1.0) + 0.5(3.0) = -1 - 0.5 + 1.5 = 0 margin = (-1)(0) = 0 ≤ 0 → UPDATE Δw = 0.2(-1)[-1, 1.0, 3.0] = [0.2, -0.2, -0.6] w(2) = [1.0, -0.5, 0.5] + [0.2, -0.2, -0.6] = [1.2, -0.7, -0.1]

Answer: w(1) = [1.0, −0.5, 0.5] (unchanged), w(2) = [1.2, −0.7, −0.1].

Verification: w(2)Tx1 = −2.7 (margin +2.7) and w(2)Tx2 = −2.2 (margin +2.2) — both correct.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: w(1) = [1.0, −0.5, 0.5] — koi tabdeeli nahi; w(2) = [1.2, −0.7, −0.1].

Ye sawal aap ko phansaane ke liye bana hai

Pehle sample ka margin +1.5 hai — yaani positive, to point theek hai, to UPDATE NAHI HOGA. Jo log seedha Δw nikaal kar laga dete hain (bina margin check kiye), un ka poora jawab ghalat ho jaata hai. Hamesha pehle margin check karein.

Doosra sample: score bilkul 0 aaya, to margin bhi 0 — aur rule kehta hai margin ≤ 0 par update. 0 bhi shaamil hai (point boundary par hai, "theek classify" nahi maana jaata), to update hoga.

Verification likhein: update ke baad dono samples ka margin positive ho jaata hai (+2.7 aur +2.2) — yaani ab dono theek hain.

66 marksfull bank #12

A perceptron has w = [0, 1.0, −1.0]. Sample x = [1.0, 1.0, 2.0] with y = +1 and η = 0.5. Compute the margin, the updated weights, and verify the result.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Weights [0, 1.0, −1.0]. Sample x = [1.0, 1.0, 2.0], label y = +1, learning rate 0.5. Margin, naye weights, aur verification — teeno nikaalein.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

wTx = 0(1.0) + 1.0(1.0) + (-1.0)(2.0) = 0 + 1 - 2 = -1.00 margin = (+1)(-1.00) = -1.00 < 0 → misclassified, update Δw = ηyx = 0.5(+1)[1.0, 1.0, 2.0] = [0.5, 0.5, 1.0] wnew = [0+0.5, 1.0+0.5, -1.0+1.0] = [0.5, 1.5, 0.0] Verify: wnewTx = 0.5(1) + 1.5(1) + 0.0(2) = 2.00 > 0 ✓

Check against theory: new margin = old margin + η‖x‖² = −1.00 + 0.5(1+1+4) = −1.00 + 3.00 = 2.00 — matching the direct computation.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: margin = −1.00 (ghalat), wnew = [0.5, 1.5, 0.0], naya score = +2.00 (ab theek).

Qadam: score −1.00 → margin −1.00 (negative, yaani ghalti) → Δw = 0.5 × [1,1,2] = [0.5, 0.5, 1.0] → jama kar dein.

Khoobsurat check: theory kehti hai naya margin = purana + η‖x‖². Dekhein: ‖x‖² = 1+1+4 = 6, to −1.00 + 0.5(6) = +2.00 — bilkul wohi jawab. Agar ye do tareeqe na milein, to kahin hisaab ghalat hua hai. Exam mein ye check 1 minute mein ho jaata hai aur ghalti pakad leta hai.

76 marksfull bank #11

A perceptron has w = [0.5, 0.3, −0.4] (with x0 = 1 the bias input). A sample x = [1.0, −1.0, 2.0] arrives with y = −1 and η = 0.1. Compute the margin and state whether an update occurs.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Perceptron ke weights [0.5, 0.3, −0.4] hain. Ek sample aaya: x = [1.0, −1.0, 2.0], asli label y = −1, learning rate 0.1. Margin nikaalein aur batayein ke update hoga ya nahi.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

wTx = 0.5(1.0) + 0.3(-1.0) + (-0.4)(2.0) = 0.5 - 0.3 - 0.8 = -0.60 margin = y(wTx) = (-1)(-0.60) = +0.60 > 0

Margin is positive, so the sample is correctly classified and NO update occurs — wnew = wold = [0.5, 0.3, −0.4].

Note: the score −0.60 and the label −1 have the same sign, so their product is positive. A negative score is correct here, since the true class is negative.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: margin = +0.60, aur update NAHI hoga — weights waise hi rahenge.

Kyun: score −0.60 aaya (negative), aur asli label bhi −1 tha (negative). Dono ek hi taraf hain, yaani prediction theek hai. Do negative multiply hone par jawab positive aata hai — isi liye margin positive hai.

Yaad rakhein: negative score ka matlab "ghalti" nahi! Agar asli class negative ho, to negative score sahi hai. Hamesha margin dekhein (y × score), sirf score nahi — margin ka poora maqsad yehi hai ke wo label ko hisaab mein le aata hai.

86 marksfull bank #16

A neuron has x = [2.0, 1.0, 0.5], w = [0.3, 0.4, 1.0] and threshold t = 1.5. (a) Does it fire? (b) What happens if w3 is set to zero?

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Ek neuron hai: x = [2.0, 1.0, 0.5], w = [0.3, 0.4, 1.0], threshold t = 1.5. (a) Kya ye fire karta hai? (b) Agar teesra weight w3 zero kar dein to kya hoga?

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

(a) a = 0.3(2.0) + 0.4(1.0) + 1.0(0.5) = 0.60 + 0.40 + 0.50 = 1.50 a = 1.50 ≥ t = 1.50 → it FIRES (output = 1), exactly at threshold (b) a = 0.60 + 0.40 + 0 = 1.00 a = 1.00 < 1.50 → does NOT fire (output = 0)

Interpretation: setting w3 = 0 removes feature 3 from the decision entirely — here that costs 0.50, enough to flip the output. A zero weight means the feature is ignored, which is exactly what ℓ1 (Lasso) regularization induces. Note (a) sits exactly on the boundary, so the convention used (≥ vs >) decides the answer — state it.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: (a) a = 1.50, threshold ke bilkul barabar — ≥ ke rule se fire karta hai. (b) a = 1.00 < 1.50 — fire nahi karta.

Kyun: w3 ko zero karne se teesre feature ka hissa (1.0 × 0.5 = 0.50) poora khatam ho gaya, aur wo 0.50 hi faisla palatne ke liye kaafi tha.

Do baatein likhein:
(1) Zero weight ka matlab hai wo feature bilkul nazarandaz ho gaya — aur ℓ1 (Lasso) regularization bilkul yehi karta hai.
(2) Part (a) mein value threshold ke bilkul barabar hai, to jawab is par depend karta hai ke rule ≥ hai ya >. Exam mein likh dein ke aap ne kaunsa rule liya — is se marks bachte hain.

96 marksfull bank #31asli Q5 jaisa

A 2D perceptron outputs 1 if w1x1 + w2x2 > t, with w = [6, 8]T and t = 20. Find the perpendicular distance from the origin to the decision boundary, and classify P = (1, 2) and Q = (2, 0).

Sawal aam alfaz mein

2D perceptron hai, w = [6, 8], threshold 20. Origin se decision boundary ka faasla nikaalein, aur do points P = (1,2) aur Q = (2,0) classify karein.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

‖w‖ = √(6² + 8²) = √100 = 10 d = |t| / ‖w‖ = 20/10 = 2.0 P = (1,2): 6(1) + 8(2) = 6 + 16 = 22 > 20 → output 1 Q = (2,0): 6(2) + 8(0) = 12 + 0 = 12 ≯ 20 → output 0

Signed distances: P: (22−20)/10 = +0.2, Q: (12−20)/10 = −0.8. So P only just fires — it sits 0.2 from the boundary and is the fragile classification of the two.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: d = 2.0; P → 1, Q → 0.

Tohfa: √(36+64) = √100 = 10 — poora number (6-8-10 triangle). Agar saaf na aaye to ghalti hai.

Extra marks: signed distance bhi likhein — P sirf 0.2 door hai boundary se, yaani bilkul kinaare par; Q 0.8 door hai. To P ka faisla Q se kam pakka hai.

106 marksfull bank #2Stanford HW7 Q1(b)

An MLP has layer dimensions d0 = 20 (input), d1 = 64, d2 = 32 (hidden), d3 = 10 (output classes), with a learnable bias at every neuron. Compute the total number of trainable parameters, showing the per-layer breakdown.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Ek MLP hai: d0 = 20 (input), d1 = 64, d2 = 32 (dono hidden layers), d3 = 10 (output classes). Har neuron ka apna bias hai. Kul kitne trainable parameters hain? Har layer ka alag alag dikhayein.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

Layer 1 (20→64): (20+1)×64 = 21×64 = 1,344 Layer 2 (64→32): (64+1)×32 = 65×32 = 2,080 Layer 3 (32→10): (32+1)×10 = 33×10 = 330 ------- Total = 3,754

Note: the +1 per layer accounts for the bias of each neuron. The input layer and the activation function hold no trainable parameters.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: 3,754 parameters.

Tareeqa: har layer ke liye (pichhle + 1) × (ab ke), phir jama.

21×64 = 1,344 65×32 = 2,080 33×10 = 330 jama = 3,754

Yaad rakhein: teen numbers ke beech teen matrices banti hain (20-64, 64-32, 32-10). Input layer ko alag se nahi ginna.

116 marksfull bank #32Stanford HW7 Q1(b)

An MLP has layer dimensions d0 = 8, d1 = 20, d2 = 20, d3 = 5 (output classes), with a bias at every neuron. Compute the total trainable parameters, and separately report the weight count and bias count.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

MLP: d0 = 8, d1 = 20, d2 = 20, d3 = 5 (output classes), har neuron par bias. Kul parameters nikaalein, aur weights aur biases alag alag bhi batayein.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

Layer 1 (8→20) : (8+1)×20 = 9×20 = 180 Layer 2 (20→20): (20+1)×20 = 21×20 = 420 Layer 3 (20→5) : (20+1)×5 = 21×5 = 105 ----- Total = 705 Split: weights = 8(20) + 20(20) + 20(5) = 160+400+100 = 660 biases = 20 + 20 + 5 = 45 660 + 45 = 705 ✓

Note: the middle 20→20 layer alone costs 420 — nearly 60% of the network — because cost grows with the product of adjacent widths.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: 705 (660 weights + 45 biases).

Do hisaab milne chahiye — (d+1)× waala tareeqa aur weights/biases alag ginna. Agar na milein to ghalti hai.

Ghaur karein: beech ki 20→20 layer akele 420 le gayi — poore network ka taqreeban 60%. Wajah: parameters do paas waali layers ke zarb se barhte hain.

126 marksfull bank #7Stanford HW7 Q1(b)

For a 2-layer network with d0 = 2, d1 = 2, d2 = 1, how many trainable parameters are there (a) without biases and (b) if biases were added at every neuron?

Sawal aam alfaz mein

sawal 1 wala network (input 2, hidden 2, output 1). Kitne trainable parameters hain — (a) bias ke bagair (jaisa sawal mein tha), aur (b) agar har neuron par bias laga dein?

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

(a) WITHOUT biases: W₁: 2×2 = 4 W₂: 2×1 = 2 Total = 6 (b) WITH biases: (2+1)×2 = 6 (2+1)×1 = 3 Total = 9

Note: the 3 extra parameters are the biases — 2 for the hidden layer, 1 for the output. For small networks the bias count is a large fraction of the total (3/9 = 33%); for large layers it becomes negligible.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: (a) 6 parameters, (b) 9 parameters.

Kyun: bias ke bagair sirf weights hain (2×2 + 2×1 = 6). Bias lagane par har neuron ka ek extra parameter aa jaata hai — hidden layer mein 2 neurons, output mein 1, yaani 3 extra.

Yaad rakhein: chhote network mein bias ka hissa bada hota hai (yahan 3 out of 9 = 33%). Bade network mein bias ka hissa bohat chhota reh jaata hai — jaise sample paper Q-2 mein 52 out of 1,012 = 5%. Phir bhi usay ginna zaroori hai.

136 marksfull bank #9Stanford HW7 Q1(b)

A network has d0 = 5, one hidden layer with d1 = h neurons, and d2 = 3 outputs, with biases. It must have at most 100 trainable parameters. What is the largest h allowed?

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Input 5, ek hidden layer jis mein h neurons hain, output 3 classes, bias ke saath. Pabandi ye hai ke parameters 100 se zyada nahi ho sakte. h sab se zyada kitna rakha ja sakta hai?

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

Total = (5+1)h + (h+1)3 = 6h + 3h + 3 = 9h + 3 9h + 3 ≤ 100 9h ≤ 97 h ≤ 10.78 → h = 10 Check: 9(10) + 3 = 93 ≤ 100 ✓ 9(11) + 3 = 102 > 100 ✗

Answer: h = 10 (93 parameters). Since h must be a whole number, round down.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: h = 10 (93 parameters).

Tareeqa: pehle parameters ka formula h ki shakal mein likhein, phir inequality hal karein.

(5+1)h = 6h ← pehli layer (h+1)3 = 3h + 3 ← doosri layer jama = 9h + 3 9h + 3 ≤ 100 → h ≤ 10.78

Yaad rakhein: neurons poore (whole number) hote hain, aadhe nahi — is liye neeche ki taraf round karein (10, 11 nahi). Aur jawab ko check kar ke dikhayein — 10 par 93 (theek), 11 par 102 (hadd se bahar).

146 marksfull bank #5Stanford HW7 Q1(b)

A layer takes an input of dimension di−1 = 8 and produces di = 5 outputs. State the dimensions of Θi, Wi and bi, and write out the layer map.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Ek layer mein 8 numbers andar aate hain aur 5 bahar nikalte hain. Batayein: parameter matrix Θi ka size kya hai, weight matrix Wi ka, aur bias vector bi ka. Aur layer ka formula likhein.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

Θi ∈ R(8+1)×5 = R9×5 (45 parameters) Wi ∈ R8×5 (40 weights) bi ∈ R5 (5 biases) Layer map: gi(zi-1) = h( (Θi)T(1, zi-1) ) = h( bi + (Wi)Tzi-1 )

Check: 40 + 5 = 45 = 9×5. The extra row of Θi is (bi)T, paired with the constant input 1.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: Θi 9×5, Wi 8×5, bi 5 ka vector.

Kyun: rows mein 8 + 1 = 9 (wo +1 bias ke liye), columns mein 5 (kyunki 5 neurons hain, aur har neuron ka apna column hota hai).

Hisaab milta hai: 40 weights + 5 biases = 45 = 9×5. Agar ye do hisaab na milein, to kuch ghalat hai.

Yaad rakhein: Θ ki pehli row hi b hai, aur wo hamesha us 1 ke saath multiply hoti hai jo input ke oopar chipkaya jaata hai.

156 marksfull bank #6

A network is described as a "4-layer neural net". How many hidden layers does it have, how many parameter matrices, and how many activation functions are applied?

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Ek network ko "4-layer neural net" kaha gaya hai. Batayein: is mein kitni hidden layers hain, kitni parameter matrices hain, aur activation function kitni baar lagta hai?

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

Hidden layers = 3 (z¹, z², z³) — input is NOT a layer Parameter matrices = 4 (Θ¹, Θ², Θ³, Θ⁴) Activations = 4 h is applied once INSIDE every layer

Why: by the definition of a layer, gi(zi−1) = h(ΘiT(1, zi−1)) for i = 1, …, M — the activation h sits inside every layer, the output layer included. So M layers give M parameter matrices and M activation applications; the input is not a layer (it computes nothing), hence M−1 = 3 hidden layers.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: 3 hidden layers, 4 parameter matrices, aur activation 4 baar.

Hidden 3 kyun: layers ginte waqt input ko nahi ginte — wo koi hisaab nahi karta, sirf numbers hain. To "4-layer" = 3 hidden (z1, z2, z3) + 1 output (z4 = ŷ).

Activation 4 kyun, 3 nahi: slide par layer ki definition hi ye hai — gi(zi−1) = h(ΘiT(1, zi−1)), i = 1 … M. Yaani affine + h milkar ek layer banti hai; h layer ke andar hi shaamil hai. 4 layers hain, to h 4 dafa laga — output layer par bhi, kyunki formula mein i = M bhi shaamil hai.

"3" ki ghalti kahan se aati hai: log sochte hain aakhri (output) layer par activation nahi lagta. Lagta hai — bas wahan h aam tor par identity h(u) = u (ya sigmoid) hoti hai. Identity bhi ek activation hai, is liye ginti 4 hi rehti hai. Sirf "non-linear activations" poochein to jawab 3 hoga.

Qaida: M-layer net → matrices = activations = M, aur hidden = M − 1. Teen ginti alag alag yaad na karein — do hi number hain: M aur M−1.

166 marksfull bank #34

A 2-layer ReLU network has W1 = [[1, 1], [2, 2]], b1 = [−5, −5], W2 = [1, 1], b2 = 0.7, and input x = [1, 1]T. Compute the output. What is wrong with this network at this input?

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Ek 2-layer ReLU network hai. Is input par output nikaalein. Aur batayein ke is input par network mein kharabi kya hai.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

W₁x + b₁ = ( 1+1-5, 2+2-5 ) = ( -3, -1 ) z¹ = ReLU(-3, -1) = ( 0, 0 ) ŷ = W₂z¹ + b₂ = 1(0) + 1(0) + 0.7 = 0.7

What is wrong: every hidden unit has a negative pre-activation, so ReLU switches the whole layer off. The output 0.7 is just the bias — it carries no information about x at all.

Why it cannot self-correct: ReLU's derivative is 0 on the negative branch, so no gradient flows back through layer 1 and the units stay dead. Fixes: better initialisation, smaller learning rate, or Leaky ReLU / ELU, which keep a non-zero slope for negative inputs.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: ŷ = 0.7 — aur ye sirf bias hai.

Kharabi: dono hidden neurons ka pre-activation negative aaya, to ReLU ne poori layer band kar di. Ab chahe x kuch bhi daal dein, jawab 0.7 hi rahega — network ne input dekha hi nahi.

Naam: "dead ReLU" problem. Aur sab se buri baat — ye khud theek nahi hota, kyunki ReLU ka derivative negative taraf zero hai, to training mein koi signal wapas nahi jaata. Ilaaj: behtar initialization, kam learning rate, ya Leaky ReLU / ELU (jo negative taraf bhi thori dhalaan rakhte hain) — aur yehi wajah hai ke asli paper ke Q-2 mein wohi do functions poochey gaye thay.

176 marksfull bank #35

A neuron fires if a > t with t = 2.5, where a = Σxiwi, x = [1, 2, 3]T, w = [0.5, 0.5, 0.5]T. (a) Does it fire? (b) Rewrite the rule in bias form Σxiwi + b > 0 and verify you get the same answer.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Neuron a > 2.5 par fire karta hai. (a) Kya ye fire karta hai? (b) Rule ko bias ki shakal mein likhein (Σxiwi + b > 0) aur tasdeeq karein ke jawab wohi aata hai.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

(a) a = 0.5(1) + 0.5(2) + 0.5(3) = 0.5 + 1.0 + 1.5 = 3.0 3.0 > 2.5 → FIRES, output = 1 (b) Threshold form : a > t Move t across : a - t > 0 So with b = -t = -2.5 : a + b > 0 Check: 3.0 + (-2.5) = 0.5 > 0 → FIRES ✓ same answer

Key relation: b = −t. The bias is the negative of the threshold. Equivalently, the threshold can be absorbed as a weight w0 = t paired with a permanent input x0 = −1 — which is exactly the augmented form used in the perceptron questions.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: (a) a = 3.0 > 2.5 → fire karta hai. (b) b = −2.5, aur 3.0 − 2.5 = 0.5 > 0 → wohi jawab.

Sab se ahem rishta: b = −t — bias threshold ka ulta hota hai. Aur isi ko ek qadam aage le jaayein to w0 = t with x0 = −1 — yaani wohi augmented shakal jo asli paper ke Q-3 mein thi (G15). Teeno shaklein ek hi cheez hain — threshold, bias, aur augmented weight.

186 marksfull bank #19

An engineer builds a 5-layer network but forgets to apply any activation function. Explain what predictor results, and why adding more layers will not help.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Ek engineer ne 5-layer network banaya, magar activation function lagana bhool gaya. Batayein ke ab asal mein kaunsa predictor bana, aur zyada layers daalne se kyun faida nahi hoga.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

Each layer is then purely affine, and a composition of affine maps is again affine:

W₅T(…(W₁Tx)) = (W₁W₂W₃W₄W₅)T x = WTx

The whole network collapses to a single linear predictor ŷ = WTx. Adding layers only multiplies more matrices together, and the product is still one matrix — so the model class does not grow at all, no matter the depth. It cannot represent any function a single linear layer cannot.

Conclusion: the nonlinearity h is the only source of a network's expressive power. Without it, depth is wasted computation.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: poora network ek hi linear predictor ban jaata hai — ŷ = WTx.

Kyun: activation ke bagair har layer sirf "matrix se multiply" hai. Aur paanch matrices ko multiply karne par phir ek hi matrix banta hai. Yaani 5 layers ka kaam ek layer se ho sakta tha.

Zyada layers se faida kyun nahi: 50 layers daal dein, to 50 matrices multiply honge — aur jawab phir bhi ek matrix hoga. Model ki taqat zarra barabar nahi barhegi, bas hisaab bekaar mein barh jaayega.

Ek line mein: network ki saari taqat h (nonlinearity) se aati hai. Us ke bagair gehrai (depth) bilkul bekaar hai.

196 marksfull bank #13

Prove that the perceptron update wnew = wold + ηyixi always increases the margin of the sample that triggered it.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Saabit karein ke perceptron ka update rule wnew = wold + ηyixi us sample ka margin hamesha barhata hai jis ki wajah se update hua tha.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

new margin = yi ( wnewTxi ) = yi ( (wold + ηyixi)T xi ) = yi woldTxi + η yi² xiTxi = old margin + η ‖xi‖₂² (since yi² = 1)

Since η > 0 and ‖xi‖22 ≥ 0, the added term is non-negative, so new margin ≥ old margin — strictly greater whenever xi ≠ 0. QED

The key step: yi2 = 1, because yi ∈ {−1, +1}. This is exactly why the ±1 embedding is chosen.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Saboot teen line ka hai: wnew ko margin ke formula mein daal dein, bracket khol dein, aur dekhein kya bacha.

naya margin = purana margin + η‖x‖² ╵╵╵╵╵╵ hamesha POSITIVE

Asli chaal: yi2 = 1. Kyun? Kyunki y sirf −1 ya +1 ho sakta hai, aur dono ka square 1 hota hai ((−1)² = 1, (+1)² = 1). Is ek line ki wajah se poora saboot nikal aata hai.

Yahan se ye bhi samajh aata hai ke ±1 embedding kyun chuni gayi — is se y² = 1 hota hai, jo hisaab ko bilkul saaf kar deta hai. Agar {0, 1} embedding hoti, to ye chaal kaam na karti.

206 marksfull bank #18

State the Perceptron Convergence Theorem precisely, and explain two things it does not guarantee.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Perceptron Convergence Theorem theek theek likhein, aur do cheezein batayein jo ye theorem zamanat nahi deta.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

Theorem: "If the data is linearly separable, then application of the Perceptron learning rule will find a separating decision boundary within a finite number of iterations."

It does NOT guarantee:

  1. The best boundary. It returns a separating boundary — the first one it reaches — which may pass arbitrarily close to the data. Maximising the margin requires an SVM.
  2. Anything at all for non-separable data. If no separating hyperplane exists, the hypothesis fails and the algorithm never terminates.

A third, if asked: "finite" does not mean "few" — the theorem bounds nothing about how many iterations are needed, which can be very large when the margin is small.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Theorem: agar data linearly separable ho, to perceptron learning rule finite iterations mein ek alag karne wali decision boundary dhoond lega.

Jin cheezon ki zamanat NAHI hai:

  1. Behtareen boundary — theorem kehta hai "ek boundary milegi", "best" nahi. Jo pehle mil gayi, wahan ruk gaya — chahe wo data ko sataayi hui ho. Sab se acha margin chahiye to SVM chahiye.
  2. Non-separable data par kuch bhi — agar koi line hi mojood na ho, to theorem ki shart hi toot gayi, aur algorithm kabhi nahi rukega.

Teesri baat (agar poochi jaaye): "finite" ka matlab "kam" nahi hota! Theorem ye nahi batata ke kitne iterations lagenge — aur agar margin chhota ho to bohat zyada lag sakte hain.

Section B — sawal 21–31

Boolean Classification — numericals

Paper mein yahan se 3 sawal (18 marks). Har loss function ka ek numerical hai, kyunki pata nahi kaunsa poocha jaayega.

216 marksfull bank #64

Using the hubristic loss (0 for u < −1; (u+1)² for −1 ≤ u ≤ 0; 1+2u for u > 0) with κ = 2 and ŷ = 0.5, compute both losses.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Hubristic loss (teen tukdon wala), κ = 2, ŷ = 0.5. Dono labels ke liye loss nikaalein.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

(a) y = -1 : evaluate p at ŷ = +0.5. Since 0.5 > 0 → third piece ℓ = 1 + 2(0.5) = 2.00 (b) y = +1 : ℓ = κ · p(-ŷ) = 2 · p(-0.5) -0.5 ∈ [-1, 0] → second piece p(-0.5) = (-0.5 + 1)² = (0.5)² = 0.25 ℓ = 2 × 0.25 = 0.50

Note: the two labels land on different pieces of the piecewise function — third for (a), second for (b). Always decide which piece before computing.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: (a) 2.00, (b) 0.50.

Dono mein alag tukda lagta hai

(a) mein ŷ = +0.5 > 0 → teesra tukda. (b) mein −ŷ = −0.5 ∈ [−1,0] → doosra tukda. Pehle tay karein kaunsa tukda, phir hisaab.

Aur yaad rakhein: (b) mein −ŷ daalna hai, ŷ nahi — yehi wo phisalan hai jo asli paper ke Q-7 mein thi.

226 marksfull bank #59

With κ = 2.0 and score ŷ = −1.5, compute the hinge loss for (a) y = −1 and (b) y = +1.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

κ = 2.0, score ŷ = −1.5. Hinge loss nikaalein — (a) jab asli label −1 ho, (b) jab +1 ho.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

(a) y = -1 : ℓ = (1 + ŷ)+ = max(0, 1 - 1.5) = max(0, -0.5) = 0 (b) y = +1 : ℓ = κ(1 - ŷ)+ = 2.0 × max(0, 1 + 1.5) = 2.0 × 2.5 = 5.00

Interpretation: for y = −1 the prediction is correct with margin ≥ 1, so hinge gives exactly zero — the point is fully forgiven and no longer influences training. For y = +1 the same score is badly wrong, and κ doubles the penalty.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: (a) 0, (b) 5.00.

(a) Zero kyun: 1 − 1.5 = −0.5 negative hai, aur (·)+ negative ko zero kar deta hai. Matlab: prediction theek bhi hai aur margin bhi poora (1 se zyada) — poori maafi.

Yaad rakhein: ek hi score (−1.5) par label badalne se loss 0 se 5.0 ho gaya. Aur κ sirf y = +1 par laga — (a) mein 2.0 se multiply nahi karna.

236 marksfull bank #60

With κ = 2.0 and ŷ = 0.8, compute the logistic loss for both labels.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

κ = 2.0, ŷ = 0.8. Logistic loss dono labels ke liye nikaalein.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

(a) y = -1 : ℓ = log(1 + e^ŷ) = log(1 + e^0.8) = log(1 + 2.2255) = log(3.2255) = 1.171 (b) y = +1 : ℓ = κ log(1 + e^(-ŷ)) = 2.0 log(1 + e^(-0.8)) = 2.0 log(1.4493) = 2.0(0.3711) = 0.742

Note: neither value is zero — logistic loss is never exactly zero, unlike hinge. Even the correct case (b) keeps a small residual pressure pushing the score further positive.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: (a) 1.171, (b) 0.742.

ŷ = 0.8 positive hai, to y = +1 wala case theek hai — is liye uska loss chhota (0.742), aur y = −1 wala ghalat, is liye bada (1.171).

Hinge se farq (ye likhein): logistic kabhi bilkul zero nahi hota. Theek case mein bhi 0.742 bacha hai — yaani wo kehta rehta hai "aur yaqeen se theek ho jao". Hinge margin 1 ke baad bilkul chup ho jaata hai.

246 marksfull bank #61

For ŷ = −2.5 with true label y = −1 and κ = 1, compute the square, hinge and logistic losses. Which loss behaves badly here, and why?

Sawal aam alfaz mein

ŷ = −2.5, asli label −1. Teeno loss nikaalein: square, hinge, logistic. Phir batayein kaunsa loss yahan bura rawayya dikha raha hai aur kyun.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

Margin = yŷ = (-1)(-2.5) = +2.5 → CONFIDENTLY CORRECT Square : (1 + ŷ)² = (1 - 2.5)² = (-1.5)² = 2.25 Hinge : (1 + ŷ)+ = max(0, -1.5) = 0 Logistic : log(1+e^ŷ) = log(1 + e^-2.5) = 0.079

Square loss behaves badly. The prediction is correct and confident, yet square loss charges 2.25 — and that penalty grows as the prediction improves further. It is not monotonically non-increasing in the margin.

Consequence: during ERM, square loss drags confident correct predictions back toward the boundary, wasting capacity and distorting the fit. Hinge (exactly 0) and logistic (0.079, decaying) both behave correctly.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: square 2.25, hinge 0, logistic 0.079. Square loss bura rawayya dikha raha hai.

Haalat: margin = +2.5 — prediction theek bhi hai aur poore yaqeen se theek hai, yaani sab se behtareen surat.

Phir bhi square loss 2.25 ki saza de raha hai — aur ŷ aur negative karein to saza aur barhegi. Yehi kharabi hai.

Amli nuqsan: training mein model is saza se bachne ke liye apni sahi predictions ko boundary ki taraf wapas kheench lega. Hinge (bilkul 0) aur logistic (0.079, gir raha) dono theek hain. Ye wohi masla hai jo I3 aur sample paper ke Q-4 mein tha.

256 marksfull bank #70Q10 jaisa

A score ŷ = −1.5 is observed with true label y = −1. Compute the hinge, hubristic, square and logistic losses (κ = 1). Which losses give exactly zero, and what does that tell you about them?

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Score ŷ = −1.5, asli label −1. Chaaron losses nikaalein: hinge, hubristic, square, logistic (κ = 1). Kaunse losses bilkul zero dete hain, aur is se un ke baare mein kya pata chalta hai?

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

Margin = yŷ = (-1)(-1.5) = +1.5 → correct, with margin > 1 Hinge : (1 + ŷ)+ = max(0, -0.5) = 0 ← ZERO Hubristic : ŷ = -1.5 < -1 → first piece = 0 ← ZERO Square : (1 + ŷ)² = (-0.5)² = 0.25 Logistic : log(1 + e^(-1.5)) = log(1.2231) = 0.201

Hinge and hubristic give exactly zero. Both are defined to be flat at zero once the margin exceeds 1 — the point is fully forgiven and contributes nothing to the gradient, so it no longer influences the fitted boundary. This is what produces support vectors: only points on or inside the margin determine the solution.

The other two: logistic decays toward zero but never reaches it, so every point keeps a small influence. Square loss is worst here — it charges 0.25 for a prediction that is correct with room to spare, and that charge grows as the prediction improves.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: hinge 0, hubristic 0, square 0.25, logistic 0.201.

Haalat: margin = +1.5, yaani prediction theek hai aur margin 1 se zyada hai — sab se achi surat.

Hinge aur hubristic bilkul zero dete hain — kyunki dono ki tareef hi aisi hai ke margin 1 ke baad wo chapte (flat) ho jaate hain. Point ko poori maafi mil gayi, aur ab wo gradient mein koi hissa nahi daalta — yaani boundary par uska koi asar nahi. Yehi "support vectors" ka asal matlab hai: sirf wo points boundary tay karte hain jo margin par ya us ke andar hain.

Baaqi do: logistic zero ki taraf girta hai magar pohonchta kabhi nahi (0.201), to har point thora thora asar deta rehta hai. Aur square loss sab se bura — 0.25 ki saza us prediction par jo zaroorat se behtar hai, aur prediction aur behtar karein to saza aur barhegi.

266 marksfull bank #65

The sigmoid loss is ℓsig(ŷ, −1) = 1/(1 + e−ŷ), with ℓ(ŷ,+1) = κℓ(−ŷ,−1). For κ = 3 and ŷ = −0.40, compute both losses. Why is this loss avoided in RERM?

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Sigmoid loss ka formula diya hai. κ = 3, ŷ = −0.40 par dono losses nikaalein. Aur batayein ke RERM mein ye loss kyun istemaal nahi kiya jaata.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

(a) y = -1 : 1/(1 + e^(0.40)) = 1/(1 + 1.4918) = 1/2.4918 = 0.401 (b) y = +1 : κ · 1/(1 + e^(-0.40)) = 3 × 1/(1 + 0.6703) = 3 × 0.5987 = 1.796

Why avoided: sigmoid loss is not convex (it is S-shaped). A non-convex loss makes the empirical risk non-convex, so RERM can have many local minima: the optimizer may stall in one, the solution depends on initialisation, and there is no global-optimality guarantee.

Contrast: logistic, hinge and hubristic are all convex, so with a convex regularizer the RERM problem is solved efficiently and reliably. Sigmoid approximates the Neyman-Pearson loss more closely, but solvability beats closeness.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: (a) 0.401, (b) 1.796.

Dhyan: (a) mein exponent ka nishaan palat jaata hai — e−(−0.40) = e+0.40. Yahan log ghalti karte hain.

Kyun istemaal nahi hota: sigmoid loss convex nahi hai ("S" ki shakal). Is se RERM mein kai local minima ban jaate hain — optimizer kisi chhote garhe mein phans sakta hai, aur jawab shuruati guess par depend karega. Koi zamanat nahi. Logistic, hinge aur hubristic teeno convex hain, is liye wohi istemaal hote hain.

276 marksfull bank #62

A Boolean classifier on 200 test points gives tn = 150, fp = 30, fn = 12, tp = 8. With κ = 5, compute the error rate, the Neyman-Pearson error, and the recall.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

200 test points par tn=150, fp=30, fn=12, tp=8. κ = 5 ke saath error rate, Neyman-Pearson error, aur recall nikaalein.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

n = 150+30+12+8 = 200 N = tn+fp = 180 P = fn+tp = 20 error rate = (fn+fp)/n = 42/200 = 0.210 = 21.0% NP (κ=5) = (5(12)+30)/200 = (60+30)/200 = 0.450 recall = tp/P = 8/20 = 0.400 = 40.0%

Interpretation: the NP error (0.450) is more than double the plain error rate (0.210), because κ = 5 weights the 12 false negatives five times over. With only 40% recall this classifier misses 3 of every 5 positives — so under a cost structure that cares about positives, it is far worse than "21% error" suggests.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: error rate 21.0%, NP error 0.450, recall 40.0%.

Pehle N aur P nikaalein — warna denominator ghalat lag jaata hai: N = 180 (asli negatives), P = 20 (asli positives).

Asal nuktah: NP error (0.450) simple error rate (0.210) se do guna se zyada hai — kyunki κ = 5 un 12 false negatives ko paanch guna ginta hai. Aur recall sirf 40% hai, yaani har 5 mein se 3 positives chhoot rahe hain. "21% error" sun kar model acha lagta hai, magar hai bura.

286 marksfull bank #63Stanford HW6 Q2(b,c)

On n = 200 test points, classifier A gives fn = 20, fp = 5 and classifier B gives fn = 8, fp = 25. Which is better at κ = 1, and at κ = 4?

Sawal aam alfaz mein

200 points par: A ka fn=20, fp=5; B ka fn=8, fp=25. Kaunsa behtar hai — κ = 1 par, aur κ = 4 par?

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

NP = (κ · fn + fp)/n κ = 1 : A = (20 + 5)/200 = 25/200 = 0.125 B = ( 8 + 25)/200 = 33/200 = 0.165 → A is better κ = 4 : A = (80 + 5)/200 = 85/200 = 0.425 B = (32 + 25)/200 = 57/200 = 0.285 → B is better

The ranking reverses. At κ = 1 (plain error rate) A wins; at κ = 4 B wins, because B has far fewer false negatives and κ weights exactly those.

Key lesson: "which classifier is better" is not a well-posed question without κ. κ must be fixed from the application's cost structure before models are compared.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: κ=1 par A behtar (0.125 vs 0.165); κ=4 par B behtar (0.285 vs 0.425).

Tarteeb palat gayi! Yehi is sawal ka asal maqsad hai.

Kyun: B ke false negatives bohat kam hain (8 vs 20), aur κ bilkul usi cheez ko weight deta hai. κ bada karein to B ka faida nikal aata hai.

Sab se ahem sabaq: "kaunsa classifier behtar hai" ye sawal κ ke bagair adhoora hai. κ ko application ki asli cost se pehle tay karein, phir models ka muqabla karein.

296 marksfull bank #66

Prototypes are a = [1, 1]T (Class 1) and b = [5, 3]T (Class 2). Find the decision hyperplane, then compute the signed distance of ŷ = [2, 2]T and give its class.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Do prototypes: a = [1,1] (Class 1) aur b = [5,3] (Class 2). Decision hyperplane nikaalein, phir ŷ = [2,2] ka signed distance aur uski class batayein.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

b - a = [4, 2] ‖a‖² = 2 ‖b‖² = 34 Hyperplane: 2(b-a)Tx - ‖b‖² + ‖a‖² = 0 8x₁ + 4x₂ - 34 + 2 = 0 8x₁ + 4x₂ - 32 = 0 ÷4 2x₁ + x₂ = 8 Check midpoint (a+b)/2 = (3,2): 2(3) + 2 = 8 ✓ Signed distance of ŷ = [2,2]: numerator = 8(2) + 4(2) - 32 = 16 + 8 - 32 = -8 denominator = 2‖b-a‖ = 2√20 = 8.944 D = -8/8.944 = -0.894 D < 0 → closer to a → CLASS 1

Cross-check: ‖ŷ−a‖² = 1+1 = 2, ‖ŷ−b‖² = 9+1 = 10. Since 2 < 10, Class 1 — agreeing with the sign of D.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: hyperplane 2x1 + x2 = 8; D = −0.894; class Class 1.

Midpoint check zaroor karein: (a+b)/2 = (3, 2), aur 2(3)+2 = 8 ✓. 20 second mein poora jawab verify ho jaata hai.

Nishaan ka matlab: D negative → a ke qareeb → Class 1. Aur seedhe faasle se bhi check kar lein: 2 < 10 ✓ wohi jawab.

306 marksfull bank #43

Write the general penalty-function framework for Boolean loss functions and explain the role of the sign flip in p(−ŷ).

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Boolean loss functions ka aam (general) penalty-function framework likhein, aur batayein ke p(−ŷ) mein nishaan palatne ka kaam kya hai.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

ℓ(ŷ, -1) = p( ŷ) ℓ(ŷ, +1) = κ · p(-ŷ) where p : R → R is the PENALTY function, which should be small when its argument is negative, large when its argument is positive.

Role of the sign flip: the two classes want opposite things — for v = −1 we want ŷ negative, for v = +1 we want it positive. Negating the argument maps the positive-class case onto the same shape as the negative-class case, so a single penalty function p defines both halves of the loss. Equivalently, p is being applied to −v·ŷ, i.e. to the negative margin.

Why this matters: all six losses in the lecture differ only in p — square (1+u)², hinge (1+u)+, logistic log(1+eu), and so on. Memorise p alone, plus this framework, and all twelve formulas follow.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Framework: ℓ(ŷ,−1) = p(ŷ) aur ℓ(ŷ,+1) = κ·p(−ŷ), jahan p chhota ho jab uska argument negative ho, aur bada jab positive.

Nishaan palatne ka kaam: dono classes ulti cheezein chahti hain — v = −1 ke liye hum chahte hain ŷ negative ho, aur v = +1 ke liye positive. Nishaan palat dein, to positive class ka masla bilkul negative class jaisa ban jaata hai — aur ek hi p dono ke liye kaafi ho jaata hai.

Doosre lafzon mein: p asal mein −v·ŷ par lag raha hai, yaani ulte margin par. Margin bada (acha) → argument chhota → loss chhota.

Ye framework kyun itna qeemti hai: lecture ke saare chhe losses mein sirf p ka farq hai — square (1+u)², hinge (1+u)+, logistic log(1+eu), waghera. Yaani 12 formulas rata lagane ke bajaye, sirf 6 chhote p aur ye framework yaad karein — baaqi sab khud nikal aayega.

316 marksfull bank #39

Name the loss function and regularizer used by (a) a least squares classifier, (b) logistic regression, and (c) a support vector machine.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

In teen classifiers ka loss function aur regularizer batayein: (a) least squares classifier, (b) logistic regression, (c) support vector machine.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

(a) Least squares classifier : SQUARE loss + square regularizer (b) Logistic regression : LOGISTIC loss + any regularizer (c) Support vector machine : HINGE loss + square regularizer

Note on (b): the regularizer is not part of the name — one says "logistic regression with ℓ1 regularizer", etc. For the SVM, square regularization is part of the definition. Also note that despite its name, logistic regression is a classification method.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Teen jore — rata laga lein:

SQUARE loss + square reg = LEAST SQUARES CLASSIFIER LOGISTIC loss + koi bhi reg = LOGISTIC REGRESSION HINGE loss + square reg = SUPPORT VECTOR MACHINE (SVM)

Logistic regression ke saath "koi bhi regularizer" kyun: kyunki regularizer us ke naam ka hissa nahi — bas saath lag jaata hai ("logistic regression with ℓ1 regularizer"). Magar SVM mein square regularizer definition ka hissa hai — ye farq exam mein poocha jaa sakta hai.

Do baatein:
(1) Sawal dono taraf se aa sakta hai — "SVM kaunsa loss use karta hai?" (hinge), ya "hinge + square reg waale classifier ka naam?" (SVM). Dono taraf tayyar rahein.
(2) Naam mein "regression" hone ke bawajood logistic regression classification ka tareeqa hai, regression ka nahi. Ye confuse karne wali baat hai.

Section C — sawal 32–37

Classifier — short theoretical

Paper mein yahan se sirf 1 sawal (6 marks). Ye chhe sab se zyada aane wale hain. Har jawab 3–4 line ka.

326 marksfull bank #71

A Boolean classifier on 200 test examples gives tn = 120, fp = 20, fn = 30, tp = 30. Compute the error rate, false positive rate, false negative rate, recall and precision.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

200 test examples par ek Boolean classifier ne ye nataij diye: tn = 120, fp = 20, fn = 30, tp = 30. Error rate, false positive rate, false negative rate, recall aur precision nikaalein.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

n = 120+20+30+30 = 200 N = tn + fp = 120 + 20 = 140 (true negatives) P = fn + tp = 30 + 30 = 60 (true positives) error rate = (fn+fp)/n = 50/200 = 0.250 = 25.0% FPR = fp/N = 20/140 = 0.143 = 14.3% FNR = fn/P = 30/60 = 0.500 = 50.0% recall = tp/P = 30/60 = 0.500 = 50.0% precision = tp/(tp+fp) = 30/50 = 0.600 = 60.0%

Interpretation: 75% accuracy looks acceptable, but the classifier misses half of all positives (recall 50%). If positives are the cases that matter — disease, fraud — this model is unfit, and κ > 1 should be used to push recall up.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Pehle do numbers nikaalein (ye qadam log chhod dete hain aur phir denominator ghalat lagate hain):

N = asli negatives = tn + fp = 140 P = asli positives = fn + tp = 60

Phir seedha formula:

error rate = 50/200 = 25.0% FPR = 20/140 = 14.3% FNR = 30/60 = 50.0% recall = 30/60 = 50.0% precision = 30/50 = 60.0%

Asal nuktah — ye likhein: 75% accuracy theek lagti hai, magar recall sirf 50% hai — yaani aadhe asli positives chhoot gaye! Agar positives ahem cheez hain (bimari, fraud), to ye model na-qaabil-e-qubool hai. Hal: κ > 1 rakhein taake false negatives ki saza barhe aur recall oopar aaye.

336 marksfull bank #73

Distinguish precision from recall, giving the formula for each and a scenario where one matters far more than the other.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Precision aur recall mein farq batayein, dono ka formula likhein, aur ek aisi misaal dein jahan ek doosre se kai guna zyada ahem ho.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

RECALL = tp / P = tp / (tp + fn) → of all the actual positives, how many did we find? PRECISION = tp / (tp + fp) → of all our positive predictions, how many were right?

They differ only in the denominator: recall divides by what truly is positive, precision by what we called positive.

Recall matters more — cancer screening. Missing a case (low recall) can be fatal; a false alarm only costs a follow-up test. Precision matters more — a spam filter. One spam reaching the inbox is trivial, but a legitimate email sent to spam (low precision) may be lost.

Trade-off: they cannot generally be raised together. Predicting "positive" more freely raises recall but lowers precision, and vice versa — this is exactly what κ tunes.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Farq sirf neeche waale (denominator) ka hai:

RECALL = tp/P ← neeche: jo ASLI mein positive thay PRECISION = tp/(tp+fp) ← neeche: jo HUM NE positive kaha

Machhli ki misaal: talaab mein 100 machhliyan hain. Aap ne jaal se 30 cheezein nikaleen — 24 machhliyan, 6 joote.

  • Recall = 24/100 = 24% — "talaab mein se kitni nikaaleen"
  • Precision = 24/30 = 80% — "jo nikaala us mein kitna machhli tha"

Kahan kya ahem hai:

  • Recall zyada ahem — cancer screening. Ek case chhoot gaya to banda mar sakta hai. Jhoota alarm sirf ek aur test karwa deta hai.
  • Precision zyada ahem — spam filter. Ek spam inbox mein aa jaaye to sirf delete karna hai. Magar zaroori email spam folder mein chala gaya to wo gum ho sakta hai.

Trade-off: dono ek saath nahi barhte. Zyada khul kar "positive" kehna shuru karein → recall barhega, precision girega. Aur κ ka kaam bilkul yehi tawazun tay karna hai.

346 marksfull bank #72

In a dataset of 1000 patients, 10 have a rare disease. A classifier predicts "no disease" for everyone. Compute its error rate and recall, and comment on whether it is a good classifier.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

1000 patients hain, jin mein se 10 ko ek kam-yaab bimari hai. Ek classifier har aik ko "bimari nahi" keh deta hai. Us ka error rate aur recall nikaalein, aur batayein ke ye acha classifier hai ya nahi.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

tn = 990, fn = 10, fp = 0, tp = 0 N = 990, P = 10 error rate = (fn+fp)/n = 10/1000 = 1.0% ("99% accurate") recall = tp/P = 0/10 = 0% precision = tp/(tp+fp)= 0/0 = undefined

It is a useless classifier despite 99% accuracy. It detects none of the 10 diseased patients (recall 0%), which is the only thing the test exists to do. Its "accuracy" comes entirely from the class imbalance — 99% of patients are healthy, so predicting the majority class is nearly always right.

Fix: never judge an imbalanced problem by error rate. Use the Neyman-Pearson metric (κ·fn + fp)/n with κ ≫ 1, which assigns this classifier a cost of 10κ/1000 — large for large κ, correctly marking it as bad. Also report recall alongside.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: error rate 1% (yaani "99% accurate"), magar recall 0%. Ye classifier bilkul bekaar hai.

Kyun bekaar: us ne 10 bimar logon mein se ek bhi nahi pakda. Aur test ka maqsad sirf yehi tha! Uski "99% accuracy" poori tarah class imbalance se aayi hai — 99% log sehatmand hain, to "sab theek hain" kehne par wo khud ba khud 99% baar sahi ho jaata hai.

Precision ka hisaab bhi nahi ho sakta — tp + fp = 0, yaani zero se taqseem. (Ye likhna bhi marks deta hai.)

Ilaaj: imbalanced data mein error rate par faisla kabhi na karein. Neyman-Pearson metric istemaal karein bade κ ke saath — phir is classifier ka cost 10κ/1000 ho jaayega, jo bade κ par bohat bada hai, aur metric khud bata dega ke model bura hai. Saath recall bhi zaroor report karein.

356 marksfull bank #76

Explain the difference between a Type I and a Type II error, and give a scenario in which each is the more costly.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Type I aur Type II error mein farq batayein, aur har aik ke liye ek aisi misaal dein jahan wohi zyada mehngi padti hai.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

TYPE I = FALSE POSITIVE : v-hat = +1 but v = -1 we raised an alarm that was not real TYPE II = FALSE NEGATIVE : v-hat = -1 but v = +1 we missed something that was real

Type I costlier — spam filtering. Marking a legitimate email as spam may lose it entirely; letting one spam through is trivial.

Type II costlier — cancer screening. Missing a real case delays treatment and can be fatal; a false alarm costs only a confirmatory test.

How this enters the model: the asymmetry is encoded by κ in the Neyman-Pearson metric (κ·fn + fp)/n — set κ > 1 when Type II is worse, κ < 1 when Type I is worse, and κ = 1 for the plain error rate.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Type I = false positive — jhoota alarm. Hum ne "haan" kaha, magar tha nahi.
Type II = false negative — chook gaye. Cheez mojood thi, hum ne "nahi" keh diya.

Yaad rakhne ka tareeqa: Type I → ek "I" → ek cheez paida kar di jo nahi thi (false positive). Type II → doosri qism → jo thi usay chhod diya (false negative).

Misaalein:

  • Type I zyada mehnga — spam filter. Zaroori email spam mein chala gaya to gum ho sakta hai. Ek spam inbox mein aa jaaye to sirf delete karna hai.
  • Type II zyada mehnga — cancer screening. Asli case chhoot gaya to ilaaj der se hoga, aur jaan ja sakti hai. Jhoota shaq sirf ek confirmation test maangta hai.

Model mein ye kaise aata hai: κ ke zariye. κ > 1 jab Type II buri ho, κ < 1 jab Type I buri ho, aur κ = 1 par simple error rate.

366 marksfull bank #87

State the three-stage structure of a classifier built by embedding, and write the final classifier as a composition of functions.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Embedding ke zariye bane classifier ke teen hisse batayein, aur aakhri classifier ko functions ke composition ki shakal mein likhein.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

1. EMBED x = φ(u) raw input → feature vector (and y = ψ(v) for the output in training) 2. PREDICT ŷ = gθ(x) g : Rd → Rm, chosen by (R)ERM 3. UN-EMBED v-hat = ⌜ŷ⌟ nearest-neighbour rule, ⌜·⌟ : Rm → V Final classifier: v-hat = fθ(u) = ⌜ gθ( φ(u) ) ⌟

Why the detour is needed: the predictor g can only output numbers, but v is a category. Embedding turns the category into a vector so a numerical predictor can be fitted; un-embedding converts the numbers back. Read the composition inside out: φ first, ⌜·⌟ last.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Teen hisse:

  1. EMBED — x = φ(u): raw input ko numbers ki list banao. (Aur training mein output ko bhi: y = ψ(v).)
  2. PREDICT — ŷ = gθ(x): numbers se numbers ka andaza lagao. θ ko ERM/RERM se chunte hain.
  3. UN-EMBED — v-hat = ⌜ŷ⌟: numbers ko wapas class banao (nearest-neighbour rule se).

Poora classifier: v-hat = ⌜ gθ(φ(u)) ⌟

Padhne ka tareeqa: ANDAR se BAHAR. Sab se andar φ hai, to wo pehle chalta hai; ⌜·⌟ sab se bahar, to wo aakhir mein.

Ye ghoom kar kyun jaana padta hai: kyunki predictor g sirf numbers nikaal sakta hai, aur v ek qism hai. Model kabhi "SPAM" nahi likh sakta. To qism ko number banaya, number ka andaza lagaya, aur number ko wapas qism bana diya. Beech ka poora hissa model ki duniya hai — wahan sirf numbers hain.

376 marksfull bank #81

A 4-class problem uses one-hot embedding. The predictor outputs ŷ = (0.1, 0.6, 0.2, 0.5). What class is predicted, and by what rule?

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Chaar classes hain, one-hot embedding istemaal hui hai. Model ne ŷ = (0.1, 0.6, 0.2, 0.5) diya. Kaunsi class predict hui, aur kis rule se?

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

Rule: nearest-neighbour un-embedding, which for one-hot embedding reduces to argmax:

v-hat = arg min ‖ŷ - ek‖₂ = arg max ŷk k k largest entry = 0.6 at position 2 → v-hat = class 2

Justification: ‖ŷ−ek‖2 = ‖ŷ‖2 + 1 − 2ŷk; the first two terms do not depend on k, so minimising the distance is the same as maximising ŷk. Note the entries need not sum to 1 — these are raw scores, not probabilities, and argmax does not require normalisation.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: class 2 (kyunki sab se badi entry 0.6 hai, position 2 par).

Rule: nearest-neighbour un-embedding — magar one-hot ke saath wo argmax ban jaata hai, yaani "sab se badi entry dekho".

Wajah (ek line): ‖ŷ−ek‖² khol kar dekhein to do terms k par depend nahi karte, aur sirf −2ŷk bachta hai. Usay chhota karna = ŷk ko bada karna. Is liye distance nikaalne ki zaroorat hi nahi.

Dhyan dein: entries ka total 1 nahi hai (0.1+0.6+0.2+0.5 = 1.4) — aur ye theek hai. Ye raw scores hain, probabilities nahi. Argmax ko normalization ki zaroorat nahi hoti.

Aakhri raat ka cheat sheet — bas itna

FORWARD PASS har layer: W×input → +b → ReLU aakhri layer par aksar ReLU NAHI (linear output) ReLU: negative → 0, positive → waisa hi ACTIVATIONS a > 0 par ReLU = LeakyReLU = ELU = a (teeno barabar) a < 0 par: LeakyReLU = 0.1a ELU = e^a - 1 sigmoid = 1/(1+e^-a) PARAMETERS har layer: (di-1 + 1) × di phir JAMA karo "+1" = bias | input layer mein 0 parameters PERCEPTRON margin = y(wTx) margin ≤ 0 → w ← w + ηyx warna kuch mat karo x₀ = -1 diya ho to usay vector ke AAGE lagao aakhir mein VERIFY karo BOUNDARY origin se faasla = |t| / ‖w‖ signed = (score - t)/‖w‖ LOSS ℓ(ŷ,-1) = p(ŷ) ℓ(ŷ,+1) = κ · p(-ŷ) ← κ SIRF +1 par hinge p(u) = (1+u)+ logistic p(u) = log(1+eu) square p(u) = (1+u)² ← sahi predictions ko bhi saza = BURA hubristic 0 / (u+1)² / 1+2u NP METRIC (κ·fn + fp)/n κ = 1 → error rate CONFUSION error rate = 1 - trace/n recall = tp/P (jo thay) precision = tp/(tp+fp) (jo kaha) pehle N aur P nikaalo! UN-EMBED Boolean: sign(ŷ) one-hot: argmax ŷk HYPERPLANE 2(b-a)Tx - ‖b‖² + ‖a‖² = 0 midpoint se guzarta hai (CHECK!)

Char aadatein jo marks bachati hain: (1) formula pehle likho, numbers baad mein; (2) minus ke nishaan par bracket lagao — asli paper ke chhe sawalon mein phisalan thi; (3) sawal ki pehli line dhyan se padho ("without biases", "rows are true classes", "linear output layer"); (4) har jawab ke baad ek line wajah — paper khud kehta hai "provide brief technical justifications".

Ye page kaise bana. Asli OHT-II paper ka tareeqa dekha (numbers do, hisaab maango, phir "why" poocho), aur wohi tareeqa Lecture 6, 7, 8 ke saare topics par lagaya. Saare sawal 1 se shuru kar ke lagataar number kiye gaye hain, aur sir ke bataye hue paper pattern ke mutabiq teen sections mein baante gaye hain.

Jawab jaan boojh kar chhote rakhe gaye hain — 6 minute aur 3–4 line ki jagah mein jo likha ja sake, bas wohi.

Section A — paper mein 6 sawal

Neural Network — numericals

Paper ka aadha hissa (36 marks) yahan se hai. Sawal 1–35. Chaar cheezein baar baar aati hain: forward pass, parameter counting, activation functions, aur perceptron update.

A1 — sawal 1–10

Neural network — forward pass aur parameter counting

Sab se zyada yaqeeni qism (sample paper ke Q-1 aur Q-2 yahan se thay). In sab mein sirf do tareeqe chahiye: forward pass ke 4 qadam aur (di-1+1)×di.

16 marks

A 2-layer network without biases has x = (1.0, 3.0), W1 = [[2.0, −1.0], [0.5, 1.0]], W2 = [1.0, 2.0], with ReLU activation. Compute z, h and f(x).

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Ek 2-layer network hai (bias nahi). Input x = (1.0, 3.0). Pehla matrix W1 diya hai, doosra W2. Activation ReLU hai. Teen cheezein nikaalein: pre-activation z, hidden activation h, aur aakhri output f(x).

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

z₁ = 2.0(1.0) + (-1.0)(3.0) = 2.0 - 3.0 = -1.0 z₂ = 0.5(1.0) + 1.0 (3.0) = 0.5 + 3.0 = 3.5 z = (-1.0, 3.5) h = ( max(0,-1.0), max(0,3.5) ) = (0.0, 3.5) f(x) = 1.0(0.0) + 2.0(3.5) = 7.0

Note: ReLU switches off the first hidden unit, so f(x) depends only on the second.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: z = (−1.0, 3.5), h = (0.0, 3.5), f(x) = 7.0

Kyun: pehla z negative aaya (−1.0), to ReLU ne usay zero kar diya. Is liye W2 ka pehla weight (1.0) bekaar gaya, aur output poora doosre neuron se bana: 2.0 × 3.5 = 7.0.

Yaad rakhein: hamesha teeno (z, h, f) alag likhein, aur ek line mein batayein ke kaunsa neuron band hua.

26 marksStanford HW7 Q1(b)

An MLP has layer dimensions d0 = 20 (input), d1 = 64, d2 = 32 (hidden), d3 = 10 (output classes), with a learnable bias at every neuron. Compute the total number of trainable parameters, showing the per-layer breakdown.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Ek MLP hai: d0 = 20 (input), d1 = 64, d2 = 32 (dono hidden layers), d3 = 10 (output classes). Har neuron ka apna bias hai. Kul kitne trainable parameters hain? Har layer ka alag alag dikhayein.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

Layer 1 (20→64): (20+1)×64 = 21×64 = 1,344 Layer 2 (64→32): (64+1)×32 = 65×32 = 2,080 Layer 3 (32→10): (32+1)×10 = 33×10 = 330 ------- Total = 3,754

Note: the +1 per layer accounts for the bias of each neuron. The input layer and the activation function hold no trainable parameters.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: 3,754 parameters.

Tareeqa: har layer ke liye (pichhle + 1) × (ab ke), phir jama.

21×64 = 1,344 65×32 = 2,080 33×10 = 330 jama = 3,754

Yaad rakhein: teen numbers ke beech teen matrices banti hain (20-64, 64-32, 32-10). Input layer ko alag se nahi ginna.

36 marksStanford HW7 Q1(b)

Two engineers build networks for the same task with d0 = 10 and 4 output classes. Engineer A uses one hidden layer with d1 = 100; Engineer B uses two hidden layers with d1 = d2 = 50. All neurons have biases. Which network has more trainable parameters?

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Do engineers ek hi kaam ke liye network bana rahe hain. Input 10, output 4 classes. A ne ek hidden layer banayi — 100 neurons ki. B ne do hidden layers banayin — 50 aur 50. Sab mein bias hai. Kis ke network mein zyada parameters hain?

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

A (10→100→4): (10+1)×100 = 1,100 (100+1)×4 = 404 Total = 1,504 B (10→50→50→4): (10+1)×50 = 550 (50+1)×50 = 2,550 (50+1)×4 = 204 Total = 3,304

B has more — 3,304 vs 1,504, more than double.

Why: the 50→50 layer alone costs 2,550, because parameters grow with the product of adjacent layer widths, not the total neuron count. Both networks have 100 hidden neurons, yet B is far larger.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: B ke paas zyada hain — 3,304 vs 1,504.

Kyun (ye asal nuktah hai): dono ke paas barabar hidden neurons hain (100). Magar parameters neuron ki ginti se nahi, balki do paas waali layers ke zarb (product) se barhte hain. B mein ek beech ki layer 50→50 hai, jo akele 51×50 = 2,550 kha gayi.

Yaad rakhein: gehra (deep) network zaroori nahi chhota ho. Beech ki har layer apne doosri taraf waali layer se multiply hoti hai — yahi "parameter explosion" hai.

46 marks

For the network in sawal 1, recompute h and f(x) if the activation is changed from ReLU to the sigmoid h(a) = ea/(1+ea). Comment on the difference.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Wohi network jo sawal 1 mein tha (z = (−1.0, 3.5)), magar ab ReLU ki jagah sigmoid lagayein. h aur f(x) dobara nikaalein, aur batayein ke farq kya pada.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

z = (-1.0, 3.5) (unchanged) h₁ = e^-1.0/(1+e^-1.0) = 0.368/1.368 = 0.269 h₂ = e^3.5 /(1+e^3.5 ) = 33.12/34.12 = 0.971 h = (0.269, 0.971) f(x) = 1.0(0.269) + 2.0(0.971) = 2.21

Difference: ReLU set h1 to exactly 0, removing that unit entirely; sigmoid gives 0.269 — small but non-zero, so the unit still contributes. Sigmoid also saturates: h2 = 0.971 is near its ceiling of 1, so further increases in z2 barely change the output, which slows learning.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: h = (0.269, 0.971), f(x) = 2.21

Do bade farq:

(1) ReLU ne h1 ko bilkul 0 kar diya tha — neuron poora band. Sigmoid ne 0.269 diya — kam, magar zero nahi, to neuron ka hissa phir bhi rahega. (ReLU sparsity deta hai, sigmoid nahi.)

(2) Sigmoid "bhar" (saturate) jaata hai — h2 = 0.971 apni hadd (1.0) ke bilkul paas hai. Ab z2 ko 3.5 se 10 kar dein, h2 sirf 0.9999 hoga — taqreeban koi farq nahi. Yaani wahan derivative taqreeban zero hai, aur training sust pad jaati hai.

Yaad rakhein: isi liye slide kehti hai "ReLU is a good default choice for most problems".

56 marksStanford HW7 Q1(b)

A layer takes an input of dimension di−1 = 8 and produces di = 5 outputs. State the dimensions of Θi, Wi and bi, and write out the layer map.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Ek layer mein 8 numbers andar aate hain aur 5 bahar nikalte hain. Batayein: parameter matrix Θi ka size kya hai, weight matrix Wi ka, aur bias vector bi ka. Aur layer ka formula likhein.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

Θi ∈ R(8+1)×5 = R9×5 (45 parameters) Wi ∈ R8×5 (40 weights) bi ∈ R5 (5 biases) Layer map: gi(zi-1) = h( (Θi)T(1, zi-1) ) = h( bi + (Wi)Tzi-1 )

Check: 40 + 5 = 45 = 9×5. The extra row of Θi is (bi)T, paired with the constant input 1.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: Θi 9×5, Wi 8×5, bi 5 ka vector.

Kyun: rows mein 8 + 1 = 9 (wo +1 bias ke liye), columns mein 5 (kyunki 5 neurons hain, aur har neuron ka apna column hota hai).

Hisaab milta hai: 40 weights + 5 biases = 45 = 9×5. Agar ye do hisaab na milein, to kuch ghalat hai.

Yaad rakhein: Θ ki pehli row hi b hai, aur wo hamesha us 1 ke saath multiply hoti hai jo input ke oopar chipkaya jaata hai.

66 marks

A network is described as a "4-layer neural net". How many hidden layers does it have, how many parameter matrices, and how many activation functions are applied?

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Ek network ko "4-layer neural net" kaha gaya hai. Batayein: is mein kitni hidden layers hain, kitni parameter matrices hain, aur activation function kitni baar lagta hai?

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

Hidden layers = 3 (z¹, z², z³) — input is NOT a layer Parameter matrices = 4 (Θ¹, Θ², Θ³, Θ⁴) Activations = 4 h is applied once INSIDE every layer

Why: by the definition of a layer, gi(zi−1) = h(ΘiT(1, zi−1)) for i = 1, …, M — the activation h sits inside every layer, the output layer included. So M layers give M parameter matrices and M activation applications; the input is not a layer (it computes nothing), hence M−1 = 3 hidden layers.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: 3 hidden layers, 4 parameter matrices, aur activation 4 baar.

Hidden 3 kyun: layers ginte waqt input ko nahi ginte — wo koi hisaab nahi karta, sirf numbers hain. To "4-layer" = 3 hidden (z1, z2, z3) + 1 output (z4 = ŷ).
