Activation 4 kyun, 3 nahi: slide par layer ki definition hi ye hai — gi(zi−1) = h(ΘiT(1, zi−1)), i = 1 … M. Yaani affine + h milkar ek layer banti hai; h layer ke andar hi shaamil hai. 4 layers hain, to h 4 dafa laga — output layer par bhi, kyunki formula mein i = M bhi shaamil hai.

"3" ki ghalti kahan se aati hai: log sochte hain aakhri (output) layer par activation nahi lagta. Lagta hai — bas wahan h aam tor par identity h(u) = u (ya sigmoid) hoti hai. Identity bhi ek activation hai, is liye ginti 4 hi rehti hai. Sirf "non-linear activations" poochein to jawab 3 hoga.

Qaida: M-layer net → matrices = activations = M, aur hidden = M − 1. Teen ginti alag alag yaad na karein — do hi number hain: M aur M−1.

76 marksStanford HW7 Q1(b)

For the network in sawal 1, how many trainable parameters are there (a) without biases, as stated, and (b) if biases were added at every neuron?

Sawal aam alfaz mein

sawal 1 wala network (input 2, hidden 2, output 1). Kitne trainable parameters hain — (a) bias ke bagair (jaisa sawal mein tha), aur (b) agar har neuron par bias laga dein?

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

(a) WITHOUT biases: W₁: 2×2 = 4 W₂: 2×1 = 2 Total = 6 (b) WITH biases: (2+1)×2 = 6 (2+1)×1 = 3 Total = 9

Note: the 3 extra parameters are the biases — 2 for the hidden layer, 1 for the output. For small networks the bias count is a large fraction of the total (3/9 = 33%); for large layers it becomes negligible.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: (a) 6 parameters, (b) 9 parameters.

Kyun: bias ke bagair sirf weights hain (2×2 + 2×1 = 6). Bias lagane par har neuron ka ek extra parameter aa jaata hai — hidden layer mein 2 neurons, output mein 1, yaani 3 extra.

Yaad rakhein: chhote network mein bias ka hissa bada hota hai (yahan 3 out of 9 = 33%). Bade network mein bias ka hissa bohat chhota reh jaata hai — jaise sample paper Q-2 mein 52 out of 1,012 = 5%. Phir bhi usay ginna zaroori hai.

86 marks

A 3-layer network with ReLU produces hidden activations z1 = (0, 0, 0) for a particular input. What is the output, and what has gone wrong?

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Ek 3-layer network (ReLU ke saath) hai. Ek khaas input par pehli hidden layer ka output poora zero aa gaya: z1 = (0,0,0). Ab network ka aakhri output kya hoga, aur masla kya hai?

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

With z1 = 0, layer 2 receives only its bias: z2 = h(b2), and the output is h(b3 + (W3)Th(b2)) — i.e. the output is determined entirely by the biases and is independent of the input.

What has gone wrong: every unit in layer 1 has a negative pre-activation, so ReLU has switched the whole layer off. This is the dead ReLU problem. Since ReLU's derivative is 0 for negative inputs, no gradient flows back through layer 1 and those units can never recover.

Fixes: better initialisation, a lower learning rate, or a Leaky ReLU (which keeps a small non-zero slope for negative inputs).

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: output poora biases se banega, aur input par bilkul depend nahi karega.

Kyun: z1 = 0 hai, to agli layer ko W²T × 0 = 0 milta hai — yaani sirf bias bachta hai. Chahe input kuch bhi daal dein, agar pehli layer zero hi deti rahe, to jawab wohi rahega. Network bilkul bekaar ho gaya.

Masle ka naam: "dead ReLU". Pehli layer ke saare neurons ka pre-activation negative ho gaya, to ReLU ne poori layer band kar di.

Aur sab se buri baat: ye khud theek nahi hota. ReLU ka derivative negative taraf zero hai, to training ke waqt koi signal wapas nahi jaata — neurons hamesha ke liye marre rehte hain. Ilaaj: behtar initialization, kam learning rate, ya Leaky ReLU (jo negative taraf bhi thori si dhalaan rakhta hai).

96 marksStanford HW7 Q1(b)

A network has d0 = 5, one hidden layer with d1 = h neurons, and d2 = 3 outputs, with biases. It must have at most 100 trainable parameters. What is the largest h allowed?

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Input 5, ek hidden layer jis mein h neurons hain, output 3 classes, bias ke saath. Pabandi ye hai ke parameters 100 se zyada nahi ho sakte. h sab se zyada kitna rakha ja sakta hai?

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

Total = (5+1)h + (h+1)3 = 6h + 3h + 3 = 9h + 3 9h + 3 ≤ 100 9h ≤ 97 h ≤ 10.78 → h = 10 Check: 9(10) + 3 = 93 ≤ 100 ✓ 9(11) + 3 = 102 > 100 ✗

Answer: h = 10 (93 parameters). Since h must be a whole number, round down.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: h = 10 (93 parameters).

Tareeqa: pehle parameters ka formula h ki shakal mein likhein, phir inequality hal karein.

(5+1)h = 6h ← pehli layer (h+1)3 = 3h + 3 ← doosri layer jama = 9h + 3 9h + 3 ≤ 100 → h ≤ 10.78

Yaad rakhein: neurons poore (whole number) hote hain, aadhe nahi — is liye neeche ki taraf round karein (10, 11 nahi). Aur jawab ko check kar ke dikhayein — 10 par 93 (theek), 11 par 102 (hadd se bahar).

106 marks

A pre-trained image network maps images to zM−1 ∈ R1000. An engineer wants to classify X-rays into 3 categories using only 400 labelled X-rays. Describe the approach and the number of parameters to be trained.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Ek pre-trained image network hai jo tasveer ko 1000 numbers mein badalta hai. Ek engineer ko X-ray ki 3 qismein pehchanni hain, magar us ke paas sirf 400 labelled X-rays hain. Wo kya tareeqa istemaal kare, aur usay kitne parameters train karne padenge?

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

Approach: use the pre-trained network as a fixed feature extractor. Freeze all its parameters, pass each X-ray through it to obtain x = zM−1 ∈ R1000, and train only a small classifier (e.g. one linear layer) on those 1000 features.

New layer (1000 → 3), with bias: (1000 + 1) × 3 = 3,003 parameters

Justification: with only 400 examples, training a full network from scratch would badly overfit. The pre-trained features are general (edges, textures, shapes), so they transfer even though X-rays differ from the original training images. Note 3,003 parameters still exceeds 400 examples, so strong regularization is essential — or reduce the feature count first.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: pre-trained network ko feature extractor ki tarah istemaal karein (uske parameters jam dein), aur sirf aakhir mein ek chhoti classifier layer train karein — (1000+1)×3 = 3,003 parameters.

Kyun: 400 examples par poora network train karna bilkul nahi ho sakta — wo overfit ho jaayega (lakhon parameters, 400 misaalein). Magar pre-trained features aam (general) hain — kinaare, texture, shakalein — aur ye X-ray mein bhi kaam karti hain, chahe original training normal tasveeron par hui thi.

Ek ahem baat zaroor likhein: 3,003 parameters phir bhi 400 examples se zyada hain! Is liye sakht regularization (bada λ) lazmi hai — warna ye chhoti layer bhi overfit kar legi. Ya pehle features kam kar lein.

A2 — sawal 11–18

Perceptron — margin, update, convergence

Sample paper ka Q-3 yahan se tha. Tareeqa hamesha wohi 5 qadam: score → margin → faisla → update → verify.

116 marks

A perceptron has w = [0.5, 0.3, −0.4] (with x0 = 1 the bias input). A sample x = [1.0, −1.0, 2.0] arrives with y = −1 and η = 0.1. Compute the margin and state whether an update occurs.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Perceptron ke weights [0.5, 0.3, −0.4] hain. Ek sample aaya: x = [1.0, −1.0, 2.0], asli label y = −1, learning rate 0.1. Margin nikaalein aur batayein ke update hoga ya nahi.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

wTx = 0.5(1.0) + 0.3(-1.0) + (-0.4)(2.0) = 0.5 - 0.3 - 0.8 = -0.60 margin = y(wTx) = (-1)(-0.60) = +0.60 > 0

Margin is positive, so the sample is correctly classified and NO update occurs — wnew = wold = [0.5, 0.3, −0.4].

Note: the score −0.60 and the label −1 have the same sign, so their product is positive. A negative score is correct here, since the true class is negative.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: margin = +0.60, aur update NAHI hoga — weights waise hi rahenge.

Kyun: score −0.60 aaya (negative), aur asli label bhi −1 tha (negative). Dono ek hi taraf hain, yaani prediction theek hai. Do negative multiply hone par jawab positive aata hai — isi liye margin positive hai.

Yaad rakhein: negative score ka matlab "ghalti" nahi! Agar asli class negative ho, to negative score sahi hai. Hamesha margin dekhein (y × score), sirf score nahi — margin ka poora maqsad yehi hai ke wo label ko hisaab mein le aata hai.

126 marks

A perceptron has w = [0, 1.0, −1.0]. Sample x = [1.0, 1.0, 2.0] with y = +1 and η = 0.5. Compute the margin, the updated weights, and verify the result.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Weights [0, 1.0, −1.0]. Sample x = [1.0, 1.0, 2.0], label y = +1, learning rate 0.5. Margin, naye weights, aur verification — teeno nikaalein.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

wTx = 0(1.0) + 1.0(1.0) + (-1.0)(2.0) = 0 + 1 - 2 = -1.00 margin = (+1)(-1.00) = -1.00 < 0 → misclassified, update Δw = ηyx = 0.5(+1)[1.0, 1.0, 2.0] = [0.5, 0.5, 1.0] wnew = [0+0.5, 1.0+0.5, -1.0+1.0] = [0.5, 1.5, 0.0] Verify: wnewTx = 0.5(1) + 1.5(1) + 0.0(2) = 2.00 > 0 ✓

Check against theory: new margin = old margin + η‖x‖² = −1.00 + 0.5(1+1+4) = −1.00 + 3.00 = 2.00 — matching the direct computation.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: margin = −1.00 (ghalat), wnew = [0.5, 1.5, 0.0], naya score = +2.00 (ab theek).

Qadam: score −1.00 → margin −1.00 (negative, yaani ghalti) → Δw = 0.5 × [1,1,2] = [0.5, 0.5, 1.0] → jama kar dein.

Khoobsurat check: theory kehti hai naya margin = purana + η‖x‖². Dekhein: ‖x‖² = 1+1+4 = 6, to −1.00 + 0.5(6) = +2.00 — bilkul wohi jawab. Agar ye do tareeqe na milein, to kahin hisaab ghalat hua hai. Exam mein ye check 1 minute mein ho jaata hai aur ghalti pakad leta hai.

136 marks

Prove that the perceptron update wnew = wold + ηyixi always increases the margin of the sample that triggered it.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Saabit karein ke perceptron ka update rule wnew = wold + ηyixi us sample ka margin hamesha barhata hai jis ki wajah se update hua tha.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

new margin = yi ( wnewTxi ) = yi ( (wold + ηyixi)T xi ) = yi woldTxi + η yi² xiTxi = old margin + η ‖xi‖₂² (since yi² = 1)

Since η > 0 and ‖xi‖22 ≥ 0, the added term is non-negative, so new margin ≥ old margin — strictly greater whenever xi ≠ 0. QED

The key step: yi2 = 1, because yi ∈ {−1, +1}. This is exactly why the ±1 embedding is chosen.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Saboot teen line ka hai: wnew ko margin ke formula mein daal dein, bracket khol dein, aur dekhein kya bacha.

naya margin = purana margin + η‖x‖² ╵╵╵╵╵╵ hamesha POSITIVE

Asli chaal: yi2 = 1. Kyun? Kyunki y sirf −1 ya +1 ho sakta hai, aur dono ka square 1 hota hai ((−1)² = 1, (+1)² = 1). Is ek line ki wajah se poora saboot nikal aata hai.

Yahan se ye bhi samajh aata hai ke ±1 embedding kyun chuni gayi — is se y² = 1 hota hai, jo hisaab ko bilkul saaf kar deta hai. Agar {0, 1} embedding hoti, to ye chaal kaam na karti.

146 marks

A dataset contains two points of class +1 and two of class −1, arranged so that no straight line can separate them (an XOR pattern). What happens when the Perceptron Learning Algorithm is run on it, and what would you do instead?

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Ek data set mein do points class +1 ke hain aur do class −1 ke, magar wo aise rakhe hain ke koi seedhi line unhein alag nahi kar sakti (XOR jaisi shakal). Perceptron Learning Algorithm chalane par kya hoga, aur aap us ki jagah kya karenge?

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

The algorithm never terminates. Its stopping condition is "all points correctly classified", which is unreachable for non-separable data, so it cycles forever, updating weights on whichever point is currently misclassified. The Perceptron Convergence Theorem gives no guarantee here, since its hypothesis (linear separability) fails.

What to do instead — any one of:

  • Cap the iterations and keep the best w seen (pocket algorithm).
  • Replace "zero errors" with loss minimization — RERM with hinge or logistic loss (SVM / logistic regression), which converges on any data.
  • Make the data separable in a richer space — feature engineering or a hidden layer (a 2-layer network solves XOR).

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: algorithm kabhi nahi rukega — hamesha chalta rahega.

Kyun: perceptron ka rukne ka shart hai "jab saare points theek ho jaayein". Magar jab koi line hi sab ko theek nahi kar sakti, to ye shart kabhi poori nahi hogi. Wo ek point theek karega, doosra bigad jaayega, phir doosra theek karega, pehla bigad jaayega — hamesha ke liye.

Teen hal (koi ek likh dein):

  • Iterations ki had lagayein aur jo best w mila usay rakh lein.
  • "Zero ghalti" ki zidd chhod dein — hinge ya logistic loss ke saath RERM karein (yaani SVM ya logistic regression). Ye har qism ke data par chalte hain.
  • Data ko separable bana dein — feature engineering se, ya ek hidden layer daal kar (2-layer network XOR hal kar leta hai).

Yaad rakhein: yehi wajah hai ke Lecture-8 aata hai. Perceptron ki ye kamzori hi loss minimization wale raste ki buniyad hai.

156 marks

A perceptron uses the rule "if Σxiwi ≥ t then output 1". Show how to absorb the threshold t into the weight vector, and state the benefit.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Perceptron ka rule hai: "agar Σxiwi ≥ t ho to output 1". Dikhayein ke threshold t ko weight vector ke andar kaise shaamil kiya jaata hai, aur is ka faida kya hai.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

Σ xiwi ≥ t Σ xiwi - t ≥ 0 Σ xiwi + (-1)(t) ≥ 0 Set x₀ = -1 and w₀ = t : M Σ xiwi ≥ 0 i=0

The threshold is now "a weight with a permanent input of −1". The enlarged vector is called the augmented weight vector.

Benefit: the threshold becomes learnable by the same update rule as every other weight — no separate treatment, and the decision rule reduces to the single test wTx ≥ 0. Equivalently, with x0 = +1 one writes w0 = b = −t.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: t ko doosri taraf le jaayein, usay (−1)(t) likhein, aur phir x0 = −1, w0 = t rakh dein. Ab rule sirf Σi=0 xiwi ≥ 0 ban jaata hai — threshold ghayab.

Faida (do cheezein):

  • Ek hi update rule sab par lagta hai — threshold ko alag se seekhne ki zaroorat nahi. Wo khud ba khud ek aam weight ban gaya.
  • Faisla saaf — bas wTx ≥ 0 dekhna hai, koi number yaad rakhne ki zaroorat nahi.

Yaad rakhein: is naye vector ko "augmented" kehte hain — aur yehi lafz sample paper ke Q-3 mein tha. Aur x0 = +1 wala tareeqa bhi theek hai, bas us mein bias = −threshold hota hai.

166 marks

A neuron has x = [2.0, 1.0, 0.5], w = [0.3, 0.4, 1.0] and threshold t = 1.5. (a) Does it fire? (b) What happens if w3 is set to zero?

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Ek neuron hai: x = [2.0, 1.0, 0.5], w = [0.3, 0.4, 1.0], threshold t = 1.5. (a) Kya ye fire karta hai? (b) Agar teesra weight w3 zero kar dein to kya hoga?

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

(a) a = 0.3(2.0) + 0.4(1.0) + 1.0(0.5) = 0.60 + 0.40 + 0.50 = 1.50 a = 1.50 ≥ t = 1.50 → it FIRES (output = 1), exactly at threshold (b) a = 0.60 + 0.40 + 0 = 1.00 a = 1.00 < 1.50 → does NOT fire (output = 0)

Interpretation: setting w3 = 0 removes feature 3 from the decision entirely — here that costs 0.50, enough to flip the output. A zero weight means the feature is ignored, which is exactly what ℓ1 (Lasso) regularization induces. Note (a) sits exactly on the boundary, so the convention used (≥ vs >) decides the answer — state it.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: (a) a = 1.50, threshold ke bilkul barabar — ≥ ke rule se fire karta hai. (b) a = 1.00 < 1.50 — fire nahi karta.

Kyun: w3 ko zero karne se teesre feature ka hissa (1.0 × 0.5 = 0.50) poora khatam ho gaya, aur wo 0.50 hi faisla palatne ke liye kaafi tha.

Do baatein likhein:
(1) Zero weight ka matlab hai wo feature bilkul nazarandaz ho gaya — aur ℓ1 (Lasso) regularization bilkul yehi karta hai.
(2) Part (a) mein value threshold ke bilkul barabar hai, to jawab is par depend karta hai ke rule ≥ hai ya >. Exam mein likh dein ke aap ne kaunsa rule liya — is se marks bachte hain.

176 marks

Two samples both have margin > 0: sample A has margin 0.02, sample B has margin 5.0. Both are "correctly classified". Why should an engineer still be concerned about sample A?

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Do samples hain, dono ka margin positive hai: A ka 0.02, B ka 5.0. Dono "theek classify" hue hain. Phir bhi engineer ko A ki fikar kyun honi chahiye?

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

The sign of the margin says correct/incorrect; its magnitude says how confidently. Sample A is correct by only 0.02 — it sits essentially on the decision boundary. Any small perturbation (measurement noise, a slightly different training run, a small weight change) flips it to misclassified. Sample B at margin 5.0 is robust.

Consequence: the perceptron cannot distinguish these cases — it stops as soon as all margins are positive, so it may return a boundary that grazes the data. This is exactly why hinge loss demands a margin of at least 1 and still penalises A, and why SVM maximises the margin rather than merely achieving a positive one.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: kyunki A boundary ke bilkul paas hai — zara si tabdeeli us ko ghalat kar degi.

Margin ke do hisse:

  • Nishaan (sign) → theek hai ya ghalat
  • Size → kitne yaqeen se theek hai

A sirf 0.02 se theek hai — yaani wo taqreeban boundary par baitha hai. Measurement mein zara sa shor (noise), ya weights mein chhoti si tabdeeli, aur wo doosri taraf chala jaayega. B 5.0 par hai — wo mehfooz hai.

Sab se ahem nateeja: perceptron in dono mein farq nahi kar sakta — wo "saare margins positive" dekh kar ruk jaata hai, chahe boundary data ko chhoo rahi ho. Isi liye hinge loss margin "kam az kam 1" maangta hai aur A ko phir bhi saza deta hai, aur isi liye SVM margin ko sab se bada karta hai — sirf positive karna kaafi nahi samajhta.

186 marks

State the Perceptron Convergence Theorem precisely, and explain two things it does not guarantee.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Perceptron Convergence Theorem theek theek likhein, aur do cheezein batayein jo ye theorem zamanat nahi deta.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

Theorem: "If the data is linearly separable, then application of the Perceptron learning rule will find a separating decision boundary within a finite number of iterations."

It does NOT guarantee:

  1. The best boundary. It returns a separating boundary — the first one it reaches — which may pass arbitrarily close to the data. Maximising the margin requires an SVM.
  2. Anything at all for non-separable data. If no separating hyperplane exists, the hypothesis fails and the algorithm never terminates.

A third, if asked: "finite" does not mean "few" — the theorem bounds nothing about how many iterations are needed, which can be very large when the margin is small.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Theorem: agar data linearly separable ho, to perceptron learning rule finite iterations mein ek alag karne wali decision boundary dhoond lega.

Jin cheezon ki zamanat NAHI hai:

  1. Behtareen boundary — theorem kehta hai "ek boundary milegi", "best" nahi. Jo pehle mil gayi, wahan ruk gaya — chahe wo data ko sataayi hui ho. Sab se acha margin chahiye to SVM chahiye.
  2. Non-separable data par kuch bhi — agar koi line hi mojood na ho, to theorem ki shart hi toot gayi, aur algorithm kabhi nahi rukega.

Teesri baat (agar poochi jaaye): "finite" ka matlab "kam" nahi hota! Theorem ye nahi batata ke kitne iterations lagenge — aur agar margin chhota ho to bohat zyada lag sakte hain.

A3 — sawal 19–26

Neural network — conceptual sawal

In mein hisaab nahi, wajah chahiye. Sab se zyada aane wala: "activation function ke bagair kya hoga" (G7).

196 marks

An engineer builds a 5-layer network but forgets to apply any activation function. Explain what predictor results, and why adding more layers will not help.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Ek engineer ne 5-layer network banaya, magar activation function lagana bhool gaya. Batayein ke ab asal mein kaunsa predictor bana, aur zyada layers daalne se kyun faida nahi hoga.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

Each layer is then purely affine, and a composition of affine maps is again affine:

W₅T(…(W₁Tx)) = (W₁W₂W₃W₄W₅)T x = WTx

The whole network collapses to a single linear predictor ŷ = WTx. Adding layers only multiplies more matrices together, and the product is still one matrix — so the model class does not grow at all, no matter the depth. It cannot represent any function a single linear layer cannot.

Conclusion: the nonlinearity h is the only source of a network's expressive power. Without it, depth is wasted computation.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: poora network ek hi linear predictor ban jaata hai — ŷ = WTx.

Kyun: activation ke bagair har layer sirf "matrix se multiply" hai. Aur paanch matrices ko multiply karne par phir ek hi matrix banta hai. Yaani 5 layers ka kaam ek layer se ho sakta tha.

Zyada layers se faida kyun nahi: 50 layers daal dein, to 50 matrices multiply honge — aur jawab phir bhi ek matrix hoga. Model ki taqat zarra barabar nahi barhegi, bas hisaab bekaar mein barh jaayega.

Ek line mein: network ki saari taqat h (nonlinearity) se aati hai. Us ke bagair gehrai (depth) bilkul bekaar hai.

206 marks

Compare neural networks with manual feature engineering. State two advantages and two disadvantages of the neural-network approach.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Neural network aur haath se ki gayi feature engineering ka muqabla karein. Neural network ke do faide aur do nuqsanat batayein.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

Both follow the same shape — start with x and apply a sequence of transforms. The difference is who chooses the transforms.

Advantages of a NN:

  • Automatic, data-driven feature engineering — hidden layers learn the transforms, so no domain expertise is needed to design them.
  • Can capture relationships a human would not think of, and performs very well given large training sets.

Disadvantages:

  • Not interpretable — a hidden neuron has no meaning, unlike an engineered feature such as BMI. In a linear predictor θj directly gives feature j's effect.
  • Very many parameters, so it needs large data and long, tricky training.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Buniyadi baat: dono ka tareeqa ek hi hai — x se shuru karo aur transforms lagao. Farq sirf itna: transform kaun chunta hai — insaan ya data.

Faide (2):

  • Features khud seekh leta hai — aap ko sochna nahi padta ke kaunsa transform daalein.
  • Wo rishte bhi pakad leta hai jo insaan ke dimaag mein na aate, aur bade data par bohat acha chalta hai.

Nuqsanat (2):

  • Samajh mein nahi aata — hidden neuron ka koi naam ya matlab nahi hota. Haath se banaye feature ka hota hai (jaise "BMI"). Linear model mein θj dekh kar seedha bata sakte hain ke feature j ka asar kitna hai.
  • Parameters bohat zyada — to bada data chahiye, aur training lambi aur tricky hoti hai.

Yaad rakhein: NN ka asal faida "automatic feature engineering" hai, aur asal qeemat "interpretability" hai. Ye ek jumla likh dein to poore marks mil jaate hain.

216 marks

A hospital must justify every automated diagnosis to a regulator. A neural network achieves 94% accuracy; a regularized linear classifier achieves 91%. Which would you recommend, and why?

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Ek hospital ko har automated diagnosis ki wajah regulator ko batani padti hai. Neural network 94% accuracy deta hai, aur regularized linear classifier 91%. Aap kaunsa recommend karenge, aur kyun?

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

Recommend the linear classifier. The binding constraint is not accuracy but interpretability: the regulator requires a justification for each decision, and a NN cannot provide one — its hidden units have no meaning and the slides state a NN predictor is "very hard to interpret".

The linear classifier gives this directly: ŷ = θTx, so each θj is the signed contribution of feature j, and a decision can be reported as "elevated on features A and C". With ℓ1 regularization it is also sparse, leaving only a few features to explain.

Trade-off stated: 3 percentage points of accuracy is a real cost, but an unexplainable model is unusable in this setting — a model that cannot be deployed has effectively 0% accuracy.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: linear classifier — 91% waala.

Kyun: yahan asli pabandi accuracy nahi, interpretability hai. Regulator har faisle ki wajah maangta hai, aur neural network wajah bata hi nahi sakta — uske hidden neurons ka koi matlab nahi hota (slide ka lafz: "very hard to interpret").

Linear model ye kaam seedha kar deta hai: ŷ = θTx mein har θj batata hai ke feature j ne faisle mein kitna hissa daala. To report likhi ja sakti hai: "ye faisla feature A aur C ki bari values ki wajah se hua". Aur ℓ1 regularization ke saath model sparse bhi hoga, yaani sirf chand features bachenge — samjhana aur aasaan.

Trade-off saaf likhein: 3% accuracy ka nuqsan waqai nuqsan hai. Magar jo model istemaal hi nahi ho sakta, uski 94% accuracy ka koi faida nahi — wo amli tor par 0% hai.

226 marks

Explain what a pre-trained neural network is, and why it can help on a task quite different from the one it was trained on.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Pre-trained neural network kya hota hai, aur wo us kaam mein bhi kaise madad kar deta hai jo us ke asli kaam se bilkul mukhtalif ho?

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

What it is — four steps: (1) train a NN on some target, usually with a very large dataset and considerable time; (2) fix its parameters; (3) take the last hidden layer's output zM−1 ∈ RdM−1; (4) use it as a feature vector for other prediction tasks.

Why it transfers: the learned features are general rather than task-specific — early layers capture edges and textures, later layers shapes and parts. Those are useful for any task on that data type, so they remain informative even when the new task differs.

Examples from the slides: word2vec maps English words to R300; VGG16 maps images to R1000.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Kya hai (4 qadam): (1) ek NN ko bade data par train karein; (2) uske parameters jam dein; (3) aakhri hidden layer ka output utha lein; (4) usay kisi doosre kaam ke features ki tarah istemaal karein.

Mukhtalif kaam par bhi kyun chalta hai: kyunki jo features us ne seekhe, wo kisi ek kaam ke liye makhsoos nahi thay — wo aam (general) thay. Shuru ki layers kinaare aur texture pakadti hain, baad ki shakalein aur hisse. Ye cheezein har image waale kaam mein kaam aati hain.

Misaalein: word2vec (lafz → R300), VGG16 (image → R1000).

Asaan misaal: 20 saal ka tajurbaykaar darzi, jisay parde banane ko kaha jaaye. Kaam bilkul alag hai, magar uski buniyadi samajh (kapda, naap, sutli) wahi kaam aayegi. Pre-trained network wohi darzi hai.

236 marks

In the layer map gi(zi−1) = h((Θi)T(1, zi−1)), explain the role of the constant 1, and what would be lost without it.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Layer ke formula mein jo (1, zi−1) likha hai — us 1 ka kaam kya hai, aur agar wo na ho to kya cheez khoti hai?

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

The constant 1 is what produces the bias. The row of Θi it multiplies is (bi)T, so (Θi)T(1, zi−1) = bi + (Wi)Tzi−1 — the affine map, rather than a purely linear one.

Without it, every neuron would compute h((wij)Tzi−1), which is 0 whenever the input is 0 — so every decision boundary would be forced to pass through the origin. The network could rotate boundaries but never shift them, a severe and unnecessary restriction.

Note: this is the same device as the perceptron's threshold absorbed as w0 — it lets one matrix multiplication express both weights and offsets.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Kaam: wo 1 hi bias paida karta hai. Θ ki jo row us ke saath multiply hoti hai, wohi bias row hai — is liye poora hisaab b + WTz ban jaata hai (yaani affine, sirf linear nahi).

Us ke bagair kya khota hai: har neuron h(wTz) hota, aur agar input zero ho to jawab bhi zero. Yaani har decision boundary ko origin se guzarna padta.

Matlab: network boundary ko ghuma sakta (weights se), magar khisaka nahi sakta (bias ke bagair). Aur ye bohat bari pabandi hai — data aksar origin ke aas paas nahi hota.

Yaad rakhein: ye bilkul wohi tarkeeb hai jo perceptron mein threshold ko w0 bana kar ki gayi thi (G15) — ek hi matrix multiplication mein weights aur offset dono aa jaate hain.

246 marks

Why is the activation function applied elementwise rather than to the vector as a whole? What would break otherwise?

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Activation function poore vector par ek saath lagane ke bajaye har entry par alag alag (elementwise) kyun lagta hai? Agar aisa na ho to kya kharab hoga?

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

Because h : R → R is defined as a scalar function — it takes one number and returns one number. Applying it elementwise is what lets each neuron make its own independent decision to activate or not, and it keeps the output dimension equal to di.

If it mixed entries instead (e.g. summing the vector first), all di neurons in the layer would share a single value, collapsing the layer to one effective unit and destroying its capacity. Elementwise application is also what makes ReLU's selective switching possible — in the sample paper's Q-1, h1 = 2.0 survived while h2 = 0 was switched off, which only makes sense per-entry.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Kyun: kyunki h : R → R ki tareef hi scalar function ki hai — ek number leta hai, ek deta hai. Har entry par alag lagane se har neuron apna alag faisla kar sakta hai (chalna hai ya band hona), aur output ki dimension bhi wohi (di) rehti hai.

Agar entries mila di jaayein (jaise pehle sab jama kar dein), to poori layer ke saare neurons ko ek hi value milti, aur layer sikud kar ek neuron ke barabar reh jaati — uski saari taqat khatam.

Sab se acha saboot sample paper mein hi hai: Q-1 mein h1 = 2.0 bach gaya aur h2 = 0 band ho gaya. Ye sirf tab mumkin hai jab ReLU har number par alag lage — poore vector par ek saath lagne se aisa chunav (selective switching) na hota.

256 marks

A dataset of red points surrounded by blue points cannot be separated by a linear classifier. Explain how a hidden layer solves this, relating it to the polar-coordinate transform in the slides.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Ek data set mein red points beech mein hain aur blue chaaron taraf — koi linear classifier unhein alag nahi kar sakta. Batayein ke hidden layer ye masla kaise hal karti hai, aur slides ke polar-coordinate transform se is ka taalluq kya hai.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

A linear classifier can only draw a straight boundary, and no straight line separates a centre from its surroundings. The slides' remedy is to change coordinates first: apply f(x,y) = (r(x,y), θ(x,y)). In (r, θ) space the red points have small r and the blue large r, so a single straight boundary works.

A hidden layer does exactly this, but learns the transform. Its outputs z1 are new coordinates for the data, chosen during training, and the output layer is then a linear classifier in those new coordinates. The data need not be linearly separable in x — only in z1.

Key point: the r, θ transform was chosen by hand; the hidden layer's transform is learned from data. This is the sense in which a NN performs automatic feature engineering.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Masla: linear classifier sirf seedhi line kheench sakta hai, aur beech waali cheez ko chaaron taraf se koi seedhi line alag nahi kar sakti.

Slides ka hal: pehle coordinates badal dein — (x,y) ki jagah (r, θ). Ab red points ka r (markaz se faasla) chhota hai aur blue ka bada. To ek seedhi line kaafi ho gayi!

Hidden layer bilkul yehi karti hai — uska output z1 data ke naye coordinates hain. Aur output layer un naye coordinates mein linear classifier hai. Yaani data ko x mein separable hona zaroori nahi, sirf z1 mein hona chahiye.

Asal farq: r, θ wala transform insaan ne chuna tha. Hidden layer ka transform data se seekha jaata hai. Yehi "automatic feature engineering" ka matlab hai.

266 marks

The slides note that a hidden layer lets us "learn 100 templates instead of 10" and "share templates between classes". Explain what this means and why it increases expressive power.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Slides par likha hai ke hidden layer se hum "10 ke bajaye 100 templates seekh sakte hain" aur "templates classes ke darmiyan share ho sakte hain". Is ka matlab kya hai, aur is se model ki taqat kyun barhti hai?

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

In a linear classifier with K classes, each class gets one row of θ — effectively one template per class, and a class is scored by how well the input matches its single template. With 10 classes that is 10 templates, which fails whenever a class appears in several distinct forms (e.g. a car seen from the front and from the side).

A hidden layer of 100 units learns 100 templates, and the output layer scores each class as a weighted combination of all of them. So (i) one class may be recognised by several templates, and (ii) a template useful to more than one class is shared rather than duplicated — which is why the parameter cost does not grow as K × templates.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Linear classifier mein: har class ko θ ki ek row milti hai — yaani ek template (naqsha). Class ka score is se nikalta hai ke input us ek template se kitna milta hai. 10 classes = 10 templates.

Masla: agar ek class kai shaklon mein aati ho — jaise gaari saamne se bhi dikhti hai aur bagal se bhi — to ek template kaafi nahi.

Hidden layer mein: 100 neurons = 100 templates. Aur output layer har class ka score in sab ka weighted mixture bana kar nikaalti hai. To:

  • Ek class ko kai templates se pehchana ja sakta hai.
  • Jo template do classes ke kaam ka ho, wo share ho jaata hai — dobara banane ki zaroorat nahi.

Yaad rakhein: sharing ki wajah se parameters K × templates ke hisaab se nahi barhte — yehi is tareeqe ki efficiency hai.

A4 — sawal 27–35

Naye NN numericals — asli paper ke mizaaj par

Ye sawal asli OHT-II ke Q1–Q6 ko saamne rakh kar banaye gaye hain — wohi shakal, naye numbers. Agar aap ye nau hal kar lein, to Section A ke chhe sawal aap ke liye nayi cheez nahi honge.

276 marksasli Q1 jaisa

A 3-layer network with ReLU hidden layers and a linear output layer has x = [2.0, 1.0]T, W1 = [[1.0, −1.0], [0.5, 2.0]], b1 = [0.5, −3.0], W2 = [[2.0, 0.5], [−1.0, 1.0]], b2 = [−1.0, 0.5], W3 = [1.0, −3.0], b3 = 0.5. Compute z1, z2 and ŷ.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

3-layer network hai — do hidden layers par ReLU, aur output layer linear (us par ReLU nahi). Saare matrices aur biases diye hain. Teen cheezein nikaalein: z1, z2, aur aakhri output ŷ.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

W₁x = ( 1.0(2)+(-1.0)(1), 0.5(2)+2.0(1) ) = ( 1.0, 3.0 ) + b₁ = ( 1.0+0.5, 3.0-3.0 ) = ( 1.5, 0.0 ) z¹ = ReLU = ( 1.5, 0 ) W₂z¹ = ( 2.0(1.5)+0.5(0), -1.0(1.5)+1.0(0) ) = ( 3.0, -1.5 ) + b₂ = ( 3.0-1.0, -1.5+0.5 ) = ( 2.0, -1.0 ) z² = ReLU = ( 2.0, 0 ) ŷ = W₃z² + b₃ = 1.0(2.0) + (-3.0)(0) + 0.5 = 2.5

Note: ReLU kills one unit in each hidden layer, so W3's weight −3.0 contributes nothing. No ReLU on the output layer.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: z1 = (1.5, 0), z2 = (2.0, 0), ŷ = 2.5

Har layer par tarteeb: Wx → +b → ReLU. Aakhri layer par ReLU nahi.

Yaad rakhein: dono hidden layers mein ek ek neuron mar gaya (ReLU ne zero kar diya) — is liye W3 ka doosra weight −3.0 bekaar gaya. Ye line likhne par justification ke marks milte hain.

286 marksasli Q2 jaisa

A neuron has x = [1.0, 2.0, −1.0]T, w = [2.0, −1.0, 1.0]T, bias b = −1.5, with a = wTx + b. Evaluate the output under Leaky ReLU max(0.1a, a), ELU (α=1), and sigmoid.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Ek neuron hai, bias ke saath. Pehle a nikaalein, phir teen activation functions lagayein: Leaky ReLU, ELU, aur sigmoid.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

a = 2.0(1.0) + (-1.0)(2.0) + 1.0(-1.0) + (-1.5) = 2.0 - 2.0 - 1.0 - 1.5 = -2.5 Leaky ReLU : max(0.1(-2.5), -2.5) = max(-0.25, -2.5) = -0.25 ELU : e^(-2.5) - 1 = 0.0821 - 1 = -0.918 Sigmoid : 1/(1+e^(2.5)) = 1/13.182 = 0.076

Note: all three sit on their negative branch. Leaky ReLU and ELU stay negative; sigmoid maps it into (0,1) and so is always positive.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: a = −2.5; Leaky ReLU −0.25; ELU −0.918; sigmoid 0.076.

a sirf ek baar nikaalein — teeno usi par lagte hain. Bias ko bhoolna nahi.

Dhyan: max(−0.25, −2.5) = −0.25 — bada wo hai jo zero ke qareeb hai. Aur sigmoid hamesha positive jawab deta hai (0 aur 1 ke beech), chahe a kitna bhi negative ho — ye baaqi do se buniyadi farq hai.

296 marks

A neuron has x = [1.0, 1.0]T, w = [2.0, 1.0]T, b = 0.5. Evaluate ReLU, Leaky ReLU, ELU and sigmoid. What do you notice, and why?

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Ek neuron hai. Chaaron activation functions lagayein: ReLU, Leaky ReLU, ELU, sigmoid. Phir batayein ke aap ne kya ghaur kiya aur uski wajah kya hai.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

a = 2.0(1.0) + 1.0(1.0) + 0.5 = 3.5 (POSITIVE) ReLU : max(0, 3.5) = 3.5 Leaky ReLU : max(0.35, 3.5) = 3.5 ELU : a ≥ 0, so h(a) = a = 3.5 Sigmoid : 1/(1+e^(-3.5)) = 0.971

Observation: the three ReLU-family activations give the identical answer 3.5.

Why: ReLU, Leaky ReLU and ELU are all defined to be exactly a on the positive branch — they differ only in how they treat negative inputs. Sigmoid differs because it squashes every input into (0,1); at a = 3.5 it is already near its ceiling of 1, i.e. saturated, so its gradient there is nearly zero.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: a = 3.5; ReLU, Leaky ReLU aur ELU teeno ka jawab 3.5; sigmoid 0.971.

Kya ghaur kiya: ReLU-family ke teeno ka jawab bilkul ek jaisa hai.

Kyun: kyunki teeno ki tareef positive taraf h(a) = a hai — wo sirf negative taraf mein ek doosre se mukhtalif hain. Is liye jab a > 0 ho, teeno bilkul barabar.

Sabaq (ye likhein): Leaky ReLU aur ELU ka poora faida sirf negative taraf hai (dead ReLU se bachna). Aur sigmoid 3.5 par saturate ho chuka hai (0.971, yaani 1 ke bilkul paas) — wahan derivative taqreeban zero hai, jo training sust kar deta hai. Isi liye A4.2 (jahan a < 0 tha) asal mein zyada maaloomati sawal hai.

306 marksasli Q3 jaisa

A perceptron has augmented weights w(0) = [1.0, −0.5, 0.5]T with permanent input x0 = −1, and η = 0.2. Process two samples in sequence: (a) [2.0, 1.0]T with y = −1; (b) [1.0, 3.0]T with y = −1. Give the weight vector after each sample.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Perceptron ke augmented weights diye hain (x0 = −1 ke saath), learning rate 0.2. Do samples ek ke baad ek process karein, aur har sample ke baad weight vector batayein.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

Augmented: x₁ = [-1, 2.0, 1.0], x₂ = [-1, 1.0, 3.0] SAMPLE (a): w(0)Tx₁ = 1.0(-1) + (-0.5)(2.0) + 0.5(1.0) = -1 - 1 + 0.5 = -1.5 margin = (-1)(-1.5) = +1.5 > 0 → CORRECT, NO UPDATE w(1) = w(0) = [1.0, -0.5, 0.5] SAMPLE (b): w(1)Tx₂ = 1.0(-1) + (-0.5)(1.0) + 0.5(3.0) = -1 - 0.5 + 1.5 = 0 margin = (-1)(0) = 0 ≤ 0 → UPDATE Δw = 0.2(-1)[-1, 1.0, 3.0] = [0.2, -0.2, -0.6] w(2) = [1.0, -0.5, 0.5] + [0.2, -0.2, -0.6] = [1.2, -0.7, -0.1]

Answer: w(1) = [1.0, −0.5, 0.5] (unchanged), w(2) = [1.2, −0.7, −0.1].

Verification: w(2)Tx1 = −2.7 (margin +2.7) and w(2)Tx2 = −2.2 (margin +2.2) — both correct.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: w(1) = [1.0, −0.5, 0.5] — koi tabdeeli nahi; w(2) = [1.2, −0.7, −0.1].

Ye sawal aap ko phansaane ke liye bana hai

Pehle sample ka margin +1.5 hai — yaani positive, to point theek hai, to UPDATE NAHI HOGA. Jo log seedha Δw nikaal kar laga dete hain (bina margin check kiye), un ka poora jawab ghalat ho jaata hai. Hamesha pehle margin check karein.

Doosra sample: score bilkul 0 aaya, to margin bhi 0 — aur rule kehta hai margin ≤ 0 par update. 0 bhi shaamil hai (point boundary par hai, "theek classify" nahi maana jaata), to update hoga.

Verification likhein: update ke baad dono samples ka margin positive ho jaata hai (+2.7 aur +2.2) — yaani ab dono theek hain.

316 marksasli Q5 jaisa

A 2D perceptron outputs 1 if w1x1 + w2x2 > t, with w = [6, 8]T and t = 20. Find the perpendicular distance from the origin to the decision boundary, and classify P = (1, 2) and Q = (2, 0).

Sawal aam alfaz mein

2D perceptron hai, w = [6, 8], threshold 20. Origin se decision boundary ka faasla nikaalein, aur do points P = (1,2) aur Q = (2,0) classify karein.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

‖w‖ = √(6² + 8²) = √100 = 10 d = |t| / ‖w‖ = 20/10 = 2.0 P = (1,2): 6(1) + 8(2) = 6 + 16 = 22 > 20 → output 1 Q = (2,0): 6(2) + 8(0) = 12 + 0 = 12 ≯ 20 → output 0

Signed distances: P: (22−20)/10 = +0.2, Q: (12−20)/10 = −0.8. So P only just fires — it sits 0.2 from the boundary and is the fragile classification of the two.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: d = 2.0; P → 1, Q → 0.

Tohfa: √(36+64) = √100 = 10 — poora number (6-8-10 triangle). Agar saaf na aaye to ghalti hai.

Extra marks: signed distance bhi likhein — P sirf 0.2 door hai boundary se, yaani bilkul kinaare par; Q 0.8 door hai. To P ka faisla Q se kam pakka hai.

326 marksStanford HW7 Q1(b)

An MLP has layer dimensions d0 = 8, d1 = 20, d2 = 20, d3 = 5 (output classes), with a bias at every neuron. Compute the total trainable parameters, and separately report the weight count and bias count.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

MLP: d0 = 8, d1 = 20, d2 = 20, d3 = 5 (output classes), har neuron par bias. Kul parameters nikaalein, aur weights aur biases alag alag bhi batayein.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

Layer 1 (8→20) : (8+1)×20 = 9×20 = 180 Layer 2 (20→20): (20+1)×20 = 21×20 = 420 Layer 3 (20→5) : (20+1)×5 = 21×5 = 105 ----- Total = 705 Split: weights = 8(20) + 20(20) + 20(5) = 160+400+100 = 660 biases = 20 + 20 + 5 = 45 660 + 45 = 705 ✓

Note: the middle 20→20 layer alone costs 420 — nearly 60% of the network — because cost grows with the product of adjacent widths.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: 705 (660 weights + 45 biases).

Do hisaab milne chahiye — (d+1)× waala tareeqa aur weights/biases alag ginna. Agar na milein to ghalti hai.

Ghaur karein: beech ki 20→20 layer akele 420 le gayi — poore network ka taqreeban 60%. Wajah: parameters do paas waali layers ke zarb se barhte hain.

336 marksasli Q6 jaisa

For f(x) = W2φ(W1x) with W1 = [[2, 1], [1, −3]], W2 = [1, 2], x = [1, 2]T: evaluate f(x) with ReLU, and with identity activation. Show the collapsed weight vector.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

2-layer network hai. f(x) do tarah nikaalein — ReLU ke saath, aur identity (linear) activation ke saath. Aur linear case mein collapsed weight vector bhi dikhayein.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

z = W₁x = ( 2(1)+1(2), 1(1)+(-3)(2) ) = ( 4, -5 ) ReLU : φ(z) = (4, 0) → f = 1(4) + 2(0) = 4 Linear : φ(z) = (4, -5) → f = 1(4) + 2(-5) = -6 Collapsed: W₂W₁ = [1(2)+2(1), 1(1)+2(-3)] = [4, -5] f = [4,-5]·[1,2] = 4 - 10 = -6 ✓

Note: the outputs have opposite signs (+4 vs −6), so under ±1 un-embedding the two models predict different classes for the same input.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: ReLU = 4; linear = −6; collapsed W2W1 = [4, −5].

Do baatein likhein: (1) ReLU ke bagair poora network sirf ek linear predictor [4, −5] hai — do layers ka faida zero. (2) Dono jawabon ka nishaan ulta hai, to dono models is input ko alag alag class denge.

346 marks

A 2-layer ReLU network has W1 = [[1, 1], [2, 2]], b1 = [−5, −5], W2 = [1, 1], b2 = 0.7, and input x = [1, 1]T. Compute the output. What is wrong with this network at this input?

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Ek 2-layer ReLU network hai. Is input par output nikaalein. Aur batayein ke is input par network mein kharabi kya hai.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

W₁x + b₁ = ( 1+1-5, 2+2-5 ) = ( -3, -1 ) z¹ = ReLU(-3, -1) = ( 0, 0 ) ŷ = W₂z¹ + b₂ = 1(0) + 1(0) + 0.7 = 0.7

What is wrong: every hidden unit has a negative pre-activation, so ReLU switches the whole layer off. The output 0.7 is just the bias — it carries no information about x at all.

Why it cannot self-correct: ReLU's derivative is 0 on the negative branch, so no gradient flows back through layer 1 and the units stay dead. Fixes: better initialisation, smaller learning rate, or Leaky ReLU / ELU, which keep a non-zero slope for negative inputs.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: ŷ = 0.7 — aur ye sirf bias hai.

Kharabi: dono hidden neurons ka pre-activation negative aaya, to ReLU ne poori layer band kar di. Ab chahe x kuch bhi daal dein, jawab 0.7 hi rahega — network ne input dekha hi nahi.

Naam: "dead ReLU" problem. Aur sab se buri baat — ye khud theek nahi hota, kyunki ReLU ka derivative negative taraf zero hai, to training mein koi signal wapas nahi jaata. Ilaaj: behtar initialization, kam learning rate, ya Leaky ReLU / ELU (jo negative taraf bhi thori dhalaan rakhte hain) — aur yehi wajah hai ke asli paper ke Q-2 mein wohi do functions poochey gaye thay.

356 marks

A neuron fires if a > t with t = 2.5, where a = Σxiwi, x = [1, 2, 3]T, w = [0.5, 0.5, 0.5]T. (a) Does it fire? (b) Rewrite the rule in bias form Σxiwi + b > 0 and verify you get the same answer.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Neuron a > 2.5 par fire karta hai. (a) Kya ye fire karta hai? (b) Rule ko bias ki shakal mein likhein (Σxiwi + b > 0) aur tasdeeq karein ke jawab wohi aata hai.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

(a) a = 0.5(1) + 0.5(2) + 0.5(3) = 0.5 + 1.0 + 1.5 = 3.0 3.0 > 2.5 → FIRES, output = 1 (b) Threshold form : a > t Move t across : a - t > 0 So with b = -t = -2.5 : a + b > 0 Check: 3.0 + (-2.5) = 0.5 > 0 → FIRES ✓ same answer

Key relation: b = −t. The bias is the negative of the threshold. Equivalently, the threshold can be absorbed as a weight w0 = t paired with a permanent input x0 = −1 — which is exactly the augmented form used in the perceptron questions.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: (a) a = 3.0 > 2.5 → fire karta hai. (b) b = −2.5, aur 3.0 − 2.5 = 0.5 > 0 → wohi jawab.

Sab se ahem rishta: b = −t — bias threshold ka ulta hota hai. Aur isi ko ek qadam aage le jaayein to w0 = t with x0 = −1 — yaani wohi augmented shakal jo asli paper ke Q-3 mein thi (G15). Teeno shaklein ek hi cheez hain — threshold, bias, aur augmented weight.

Section B — paper mein 3 sawal

Boolean Classification — numericals

Sawal 36–70 (18 marks). Har loss function ka numerical aa sakta hai, is liye saare diye gaye hain. Asli paper ka Q-10 isi section se tha — dekhein pattern.

B1 — sawal 36–45

Boolean loss functions — hinge, square, logistic

Sample paper ka Q-4 yahan se tha. Har numerical sawal mein wohi teen qadam: label dekho → sahi formula uthao (κ lagega ya nahi) → hisaab karo. I9 ka table saamne rakhein.

366 marks

With κ = 3.0 and ŷ = 0.5, compute the hinge loss for (a) v = +1 and (b) v = −1.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

κ = 3.0 aur ŷ = 0.5 ke saath hinge loss nikaalein — (a) jab v = +1 ho, aur (b) jab v = −1 ho.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

(a) v = +1 : ℓ = κ(1 - ŷ)+ = 3.0 × max(0, 1 - 0.5) = 3.0 × 0.5 = 1.50 (b) v = -1 : ℓ = (1 + ŷ)+ = max(0, 1 + 0.5) = 1.50 (no κ)

Note: the two happen to be equal here, but for different reasons. In (a) the prediction is correct yet still penalised — the margin 0.5 is below the required 1. In (b) the prediction is wrong. And κ applies only to the positive class, since only there can a false negative occur.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: (a) 1.50, (b) 1.50.

Dono barabar aa gaye — magar wajuhat bilkul alag hain, aur yehi likhna zaroori hai:

  • (a) prediction theek hai (+0.5, aur class bhi positive). Phir bhi saza lagi, kyunki margin sirf 0.5 hai — aur hinge kam az kam 1 maangta hai.
  • (b) prediction ghalat hai (score positive, class negative).

Sab se ahem baat: κ sirf positive class par lagta hai. Kyun? Kyunki κ false negative ki saza barhata hai, aur false negative sirf tab hota hai jab asli class positive ho. Part (b) mein 3.0 se multiply karna sab se aam ghalti hai.

376 marks

Compute the hinge, square and logistic loss for ŷ = −2.0 with v = −1 and κ = 1. What do the three values tell you?

Sawal aam alfaz mein

ŷ = −2.0, v = −1, κ = 1 ke saath teeno loss nikaalein: hinge, square, logistic. In teen numbers se kya pata chalta hai?

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

For v = -1, use p(ŷ) with ŷ = -2.0 : HINGE : (1 + ŷ)+ = max(0, 1 - 2.0) = max(0,-1.0) = 0.00 SQUARE : (1 + ŷ)² = (1 - 2.0)² = (-1.0)² = 1.00 LOGISTIC : log(1+eŷ) = log(1+e-2) = log(1.135) = 0.127

This is a confident correct prediction (margin = 2.0), so an appropriate loss should be small or zero:

  • Hinge = 0 — ideal. Beyond margin 1 the point is fully forgiven and no longer influences training.
  • Logistic = 0.127 — nearly ideal. Small but never exactly zero, so it keeps a slight pressure.
  • Square = 1.00 — wrong. It penalises a prediction that is better than required, and that penalty grows as the prediction improves.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: hinge 0.00, square 1.00, logistic 0.127.

Haalat samajhein: margin = (−1)(−2.0) = +2.0 — yaani prediction theek hai aur poore yaqeen se theek hai. Acha loss yahan chhota ya zero hona chahiye.

  • Hinge = 0 — bilkul theek. Margin 1 se aage poori maafi, aur ab ye point training par koi asar nahi daalta.
  • Logistic = 0.127 — taqreeban theek. Chhota hai magar zero nahi — thora sa dabaav baqi rehta hai ("aur yaqeen se theek ho jao").
  • Square = 1.00 — GHALAT. Jo prediction zaroorat se behtar hai, usay saza di ja rahi hai. Aur prediction jitni behtar hogi, saza utni barhegi (ŷ = −3 par 4.0!).

Ye teen numbers hi poora Lecture-8 hain — yehi dikhate hain ke hinge aur logistic kyun istemaal hote hain, aur square kyun nahi. Agar "compare the losses" poocha jaaye, yehi misaal likh dein.

386 marks

Why is the sigmoid loss, despite being a differentiable and close approximation of the Neyman-Pearson loss, not used in practice?

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Sigmoid loss differentiable bhi hai aur Neyman-Pearson loss ke bohat qareeb bhi — phir bhi usay practice mein istemaal nahi kiya jaata. Kyun?

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

Because it is not convex. The slides describe it exactly as a "differentiable approximation of Neyman-Pearson loss but not convex".

Consequence: a non-convex loss makes the empirical risk L(θ) non-convex, so it may have many local minima. Gradient-based optimization can stall in one and report it as the solution, while the global minimum lies elsewhere — so the result depends on initialisation and no optimality guarantee exists.

The lesson: matching the metric closely is not the top priority — convexity is. Hinge and logistic loss are worse approximations of the NP loss, yet they are preferred because with a convex loss and convex regularizer the RERM problem can be solved efficiently and reliably.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: kyunki wo convex nahi hai.

Nuqsan kya hai: non-convex loss se poora empirical risk L(θ) non-convex ho jaata hai, aur us mein kai local minima (chhote chhote garhe) ban jaate hain. Optimizer kisi ek garhe mein phans kar samajh lega ke kaam ho gaya, jabke asli (global) minimum kahin aur tha.

Kanchi (marble) ki misaal: convex = ek saaf pyaali — kanchi kahin se bhi chhodein, hamesha ek hi sab se neeche wale nuqte par aayegi. Non-convex = oonchi neechi zameen — kanchi us garhe mein ruk jaayegi jo paas tha, zaroori nahi ke sab se gehra ho.

Sab se bada sabaq: metric ke qareeb hona sab se zaroori cheez nahi hai — convexity hai. Hinge aur logistic NP loss ki buri naqal hain, phir bhi wohi istemaal hote hain — kyunki convex loss aur convex regularizer ke saath RERM aasaani se aur yaqeeni tor par hal ho jaata hai.

396 marks

Name the loss function and regularizer used by (a) a least squares classifier, (b) logistic regression, and (c) a support vector machine.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

In teen classifiers ka loss function aur regularizer batayein: (a) least squares classifier, (b) logistic regression, (c) support vector machine.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

(a) Least squares classifier : SQUARE loss + square regularizer (b) Logistic regression : LOGISTIC loss + any regularizer (c) Support vector machine : HINGE loss + square regularizer

Note on (b): the regularizer is not part of the name — one says "logistic regression with ℓ1 regularizer", etc. For the SVM, square regularization is part of the definition. Also note that despite its name, logistic regression is a classification method.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Teen jore — rata laga lein:

SQUARE loss + square reg = LEAST SQUARES CLASSIFIER LOGISTIC loss + koi bhi reg = LOGISTIC REGRESSION HINGE loss + square reg = SUPPORT VECTOR MACHINE (SVM)

Logistic regression ke saath "koi bhi regularizer" kyun: kyunki regularizer us ke naam ka hissa nahi — bas saath lag jaata hai ("logistic regression with ℓ1 regularizer"). Magar SVM mein square regularizer definition ka hissa hai — ye farq exam mein poocha jaa sakta hai.

Do baatein:
(1) Sawal dono taraf se aa sakta hai — "SVM kaunsa loss use karta hai?" (hinge), ya "hinge + square reg waale classifier ka naam?" (SVM). Dono taraf tayyar rahein.
(2) Naam mein "regression" hone ke bawajood logistic regression classification ka tareeqa hai, regression ka nahi. Ye confuse karne wali baat hai.

406 marks

Explain the significance of the lines ŷ = 0 and ŷ = ±1 in an SVM, and what a "support vector" is.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

SVM mein ŷ = 0 aur ŷ = ±1 waali lines ki ahmiyat batayein, aur "support vector" kya hota hai.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

  • ŷ = 0 is the decision boundary — where the predicted class changes, since un-embedding is sign(ŷ).
  • ŷ = ±1 are the margin lines. Hinge loss is exactly zero outside them on the correct side, and non-zero anywhere inside — even for correctly classified points.

A support vector is a training point lying on or inside the margin (or misclassified) — i.e. one with non-zero hinge loss. These are the only points that determine the boundary: all others contribute zero loss, and moving them does not change the fitted θ.

Hence the training risk is 1/n times the hinge loss of the support vectors alone, and is exactly zero if every point lies outside the margin on its correct side — which square loss can never achieve.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

  • ŷ = 0 → decision boundary. Yahan class badal jaati hai, kyunki un-embedding sirf sign(ŷ) hai.
  • ŷ = ±1 → margin ki lines. In ke bahar (sahi taraf) hinge loss bilkul zero hai; in ke andar jo bhi aaye, us par saza lagti hai — chahe wo theek classify hua ho.

Support vector: wo training point jo margin par ya us ke andar ho (ya ghalat taraf) — yaani jis ka hinge loss zero nahi hai.

Aur yehi SVM ki sab se khaas baat hai: sirf yehi points boundary tay karte hain. Baaqi saare points ka loss zero hai — unhein hilaa dein, boundary bilkul nahi badlegi. Isi liye naam "support vector" hai: wohi boundary ko "sahaara (support)" de rahe hain.

Nateeja: training risk = sirf support vectors ke hinge loss ka average. Aur agar saare points margin ke bahar sahi taraf hon, to training risk bilkul zero — jo square loss ke saath kabhi mumkin nahi (wahan har point kuch na kuch loss deta hai).

416 marks

Compare hinge and logistic loss. State two differences and say when each is preferable.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Hinge aur logistic loss ka muqabla karein. Do farq batayein, aur batayein ke kab kaunsa behtar hai.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

Both are convex approximations of the NP loss and both are non-increasing in the margin. They differ in two ways:

  1. Differentiability. Logistic is differentiable everywhere; hinge has a kink at margin 1 and is not.
  2. Exact zero. Hinge is exactly zero beyond margin 1, so well-classified points drop out of training entirely (support vectors). Logistic is never zero, so all points keep contributing.

Prefer hinge when a sparse, margin-based solution is wanted — the boundary then depends only on the hard cases. Prefer logistic when smooth gradients are needed, or when a calibrated confidence for every point is useful rather than a hard cut-off.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Mushtarik baat: dono convex hain aur dono margin barhne par loss kam karte hain. Do farq:

  1. Differentiable: logistic har jagah smooth hai; hinge mein margin 1 par moad hai, is liye wo differentiable nahi.
  2. Zero hona: hinge margin 1 ke baad bilkul zero ho jaata hai — to door ke sahi points training se bahar ho jaate hain (sirf support vectors bachte hain). Logistic kabhi zero nahi hota — saare points thora thora hissa dete rehte hain.

Kab kaunsa:

  • Hinge — jab sparse, margin-based hal chahiye, yaani boundary sirf mushkil cases par bane.
  • Logistic — jab smooth gradients chahiye (optimization ke liye behtar), ya jab har point ka "kitna yaqeen hai" ka andaza chahiye, sirf haan/nahi nahi.

Yaad rakhein: hinge "ek had ke baad bhool jao" kehta hai; logistic "hamesha thora behtar hone ki koshish karo" kehta hai.

426 marks

Write the hubristic loss as given in the lectures and verify that it is continuous at u = −1 and u = 0. What problem does it solve?

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Hubristic loss likhein (jaisa lectures mein diya hai) aur tasdeeq karein ke wo u = −1 aur u = 0 par jurta (continuous) hai. Ye kaunsa masla hal karta hai?

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

┌ 0, u < -1 p(u) = │ (u+1)², -1 ≤ u ≤ 0 └ 1 + 2u, u > 0 Continuity at u = -1 : 0 and (-1+1)² = 0 ✓ Continuity at u = 0 : (0+1)² = 1 and 1+2(0) = 1 ✓ Derivatives at u = 0 : 2(u+1) = 2 and 2 ✓ (also smooth)

What it solves: it fixes two weaknesses at once. The middle quadratic piece removes hinge loss's kink, making it differentiable, while the outer linear piece keeps growth linear for large errors — so it stays robust to outliers, unlike a square loss which grows quadratically. It also retains hinge's exact zero for well-classified points, and is convex.

Name: "hubristic" = Huber + logistic — the same quadratic-centre/linear-tails construction as the Huber loss for regression.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Formula: teen tukde — u < −1 par 0; −1 ≤ u ≤ 0 par (u+1)²; u > 0 par 1 + 2u.

Jurne ki tasdeeq (dono kinaron par values milni chahiye):

u = -1 par: 0 aur (-1+1)² = 0 → barabar ✓ u = 0 par: (0+1)² = 1 aur 1+2(0) = 1 → barabar ✓

Aur derivative bhi milta hai u = 0 par (2(0+1) = 2 aur 2) — yaani sirf jurta nahi, smooth bhi hai. Ye likhna extra marks deta hai.

Kaunsa masla hal karta hai — do masle ek saath:

  • Beech ka quadratic tukda hinge ka moad hata deta hai → ab differentiable hai.
  • Bahar ka linear tukda bari ghaltiyon par saza sirf seedhi barhata hai → outliers se mehfooz (robust), square loss ki tarah phatta nahi.

Naam: "hubristic" = Huber + logistic. Aur ye bilkul wohi tarkeeb hai jo OHT-1 ke Huber loss mein thi — beech mein square, kinaron par linear.

436 marks

Write the general penalty-function framework for Boolean loss functions and explain the role of the sign flip in p(−ŷ).

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Boolean loss functions ka aam (general) penalty-function framework likhein, aur batayein ke p(−ŷ) mein nishaan palatne ka kaam kya hai.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

ℓ(ŷ, -1) = p( ŷ) ℓ(ŷ, +1) = κ · p(-ŷ) where p : R → R is the PENALTY function, which should be small when its argument is negative, large when its argument is positive.

Role of the sign flip: the two classes want opposite things — for v = −1 we want ŷ negative, for v = +1 we want it positive. Negating the argument maps the positive-class case onto the same shape as the negative-class case, so a single penalty function p defines both halves of the loss. Equivalently, p is being applied to −v·ŷ, i.e. to the negative margin.

Why this matters: all six losses in the lecture differ only in p — square (1+u)², hinge (1+u)+, logistic log(1+eu), and so on. Memorise p alone, plus this framework, and all twelve formulas follow.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Framework: ℓ(ŷ,−1) = p(ŷ) aur ℓ(ŷ,+1) = κ·p(−ŷ), jahan p chhota ho jab uska argument negative ho, aur bada jab positive.

Nishaan palatne ka kaam: dono classes ulti cheezein chahti hain — v = −1 ke liye hum chahte hain ŷ negative ho, aur v = +1 ke liye positive. Nishaan palat dein, to positive class ka masla bilkul negative class jaisa ban jaata hai — aur ek hi p dono ke liye kaafi ho jaata hai.

Doosre lafzon mein: p asal mein −v·ŷ par lag raha hai, yaani ulte margin par. Margin bada (acha) → argument chhota → loss chhota.

Ye framework kyun itna qeemti hai: lecture ke saare chhe losses mein sirf p ka farq hai — square (1+u)², hinge (1+u)+, logistic log(1+eu), waghera. Yaani 12 formulas rata lagane ke bajaye, sirf 6 chhote p aur ye framework yaad karein — baaqi sab khud nikal aayega.

446 marks

The Australian weather model achieves 16% error while the rain frequency is 22%. Explain why the second number must be reported alongside the first.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Australian weather model ka error 16% hai, aur baarish 22% dinon mein hoti hai. Batayein ke doosra number pehle ke saath report karna kyun zaroori hai.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

Because 22% is the baseline: a trivial classifier that always predicts "no rain" is wrong exactly on the rainy days, giving a 22% error rate with no model at all.

So the model's real contribution is 22% − 16% = 6 percentage points, not "84% accuracy". Reporting 16% alone would overstate the model's value, because most of that accuracy is explained by class imbalance (78% of days are dry).

General principle: accuracy is meaningless without a baseline. Always compare against the majority-class predictor, and report recall or the Neyman-Pearson error as well — the same reasoning that makes the "99% accurate" rare-disease classifier worthless (sawal 72).

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Kyun: kyunki 22% baseline hai — yaani wo classifier jo hamesha "baarish nahi" keh de, wo theek un 22% dinon par ghalat hoga. Yaani bagair kisi model ke 22% error mil jaata hai.

To model ka asli faida: 22% − 16% = 6 percentage points — "84% accuracy" nahi.

Sirf 16% likhna model ki qeemat barha kar batana hoga, kyunki us accuracy ka bara hissa class imbalance se aaya hai (78% din sookhe hote hain).

Aam qaida: accuracy baseline ke bagair bemani hai. Hamesha majority-class predictor se muqabla karein, aur saath recall ya NP error bhi report karein. Yehi wajah hai ke sawal 72 ka "99% accurate" cancer classifier bilkul bekaar tha — ek hi usool, do misaalein.

456 marks

The Australian weather study found that retraining with only 6 of the 62 features raised the error from 16% to 16.5%. Which model would you deploy, and why?

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Australian weather study mein 62 features ki jagah sirf 6 features se dobara train kiya, to error 16% se 16.5% ho gaya. Aap kaunsa model deploy karenge, aur kyun?

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

Deploy the 6-feature model. It drops 90% of the features for a cost of only 0.5 percentage points, and buys several substantial advantages:

  • Interpretability — six coefficients can be read and explained; sixty-two cannot.
  • Lower data-collection cost — only six measurements must be gathered, validated and maintained per prediction.
  • Better robustness and less overfitting risk — fewer parameters relative to n, and fewer inputs that can be missing or faulty at deployment.

Caveat worth adding: confirm the 0.5-point gap is on test data and larger than its run-to-run variation — if the difference is within noise, the case for the small model is stronger still. This is the practical payoff of sparsity and feature selection.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: 6-feature model.

Kyun: 90% features kam ho gaye, aur qeemat sirf 0.5 percentage point — jabke faide kai hain:

  • Samajh mein aata hai — 6 coefficients padh kar samjhaye ja sakte hain; 62 nahi.
  • Data jama karna sasta — har prediction ke liye sirf 6 cheezein naapni hain, 62 nahi. (Aur har feature ko maintain karna, check karna bhi kharcha hai.)
  • Zyada mazboot (robust) — parameters kam, to overfitting ka khatra kam; aur kam inputs ka matlab kam cheezein kharab ya ghayab ho sakti hain.

Ek ehtiyat ki baat bhi likh dein: tasdeeq karein ke ye 0.5% ka farq test data par hai aur run-to-run ke utaar charhaav se bada hai. Agar farq shor (noise) ke andar hi hai, to chhote model ka case aur mazboot ho jaata hai. Ye sparsity aur feature selection ka asli amli faida hai.

B2 — sawal 46–52

Multi-class — signed distance aur multi-class losses

Log is hisse ko mushkil samajh kar chhod dete hain — isi liye examiner yahan se poochta hai. Haqeeqat mein sirf ek nayi cheez hai: signed distance. Baaqi sab Boolean waala hi hai.

466 marks

Derive the condition under which a point x is closer to a than to b, and show that the resulting decision boundary is a hyperplane.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Wo shart nikaalein jis ke tehat ek point x a ke b se zyada qareeb hota hai, aur dikhayein ke is se banne wali decision boundary ek hyperplane hai.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

‖x - a‖₂ < ‖x - b‖₂ Square both sides (both are nonnegative): ‖x‖² - 2aTx + ‖a‖² < ‖x‖² - 2bTx + ‖b‖² ‖x‖² CANCELS from both sides: -2aTx + ‖a‖² < -2bTx + ‖b‖² 2(b-a)Tx - ‖b‖₂² + ‖a‖₂² < 0 Boundary (equality): 2(b-a)Tx - ‖b‖₂² + ‖a‖₂² = 0

This is affine in x, hence a hyperplane, with normal vector b − a, passing through the midpoint (a+b)/2.

The key step is the cancellation of ‖x‖2 — it is what removes the quadratic term and leaves a linear condition. This is precisely why Voronoi regions are polyhedra with straight boundaries.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Teen qadam: (1) ‖x−a‖ < ‖x−b‖ likhein; (2) dono taraf square karein; (3) bracket khol dein.

Asal chaal: khulne par dono taraf ‖x‖² aa jaata hai, aur wo kat jaata hai. Jo bacha:

2(b-a)Tx - ‖b‖² + ‖a‖² < 0

Aur ye x mein linear hai — yaani boundary ek hyperplane hai, jis ka normal vector b − a hai aur jo midpoint (a+b)/2 se guzarta hai.

Poora saboot us ek kaatne par khada hai. Agar ‖x‖² na kaatta, to boundary quadratic (tedhi) hoti. Isi ek wajah se Voronoi regions ke kinaare seedhe hote hain — aur yehi is derivation ka asli paigham hai.

476 marks

Define the signed distance d(x) from x to the boundary between a and b. State its four properties and explain why it is normalized.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

a aur b ke darmiyan ki boundary se x ka signed distance d(x) define karein. Us ki chaar khoobiyan batayein, aur wajah batayein ke usay normalize kyun kiya jaata hai.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

2(b-a)Tx - ‖b‖₂² + ‖a‖₂² d(x) = --------------------------- 2 ‖b - a‖₂

Four properties:

  • d(x) < 0 when x is closer to a than to b
  • |d(x)| is the true distance from x to the boundary
  • d(x) = 0 gives the decision boundary
  • d is an affine function of x

Why normalized: dividing by 2‖b−a‖ turns the raw expression into a true geometric distance. Without it the sign would still be right, but the magnitude would scale with how far apart a and b happen to be, so it would not be comparable across different pairs — and a statement like hinge loss's "margin of at least one" would be meaningless.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Formula: J2 waala expression, taqseem 2‖b−a‖.

Chaar khoobiyan: (1) d < 0 jab x a ke qareeb ho; (2) |d| boundary se asli faasla hai; (3) d = 0 boundary hai; (4) d x ka affine function hai.

Normalize kyun kiya: taqseem ke bagair sirf nishaan theek hota (kis taraf hai), magar size ka koi mustaqil matlab na hota — wo is par depend karti ke a aur b ittefaqan kitne door hain. Taqseem karne ke baad number asli geometric faasla ban jaata hai, jo har jode ke liye ek jaise paimane par hota hai.

Aur yehi wajah hai ke hinge loss ka "margin kam az kam 1" ka jumla maayne rakhta hai — agar d normalized na hota, to "1" ka koi mutlaq matlab na hota.

486 marks

Show that for the Boolean embedding y1 = −1, y2 = +1, the signed distance reduces to d(ŷ) = ŷ.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Dikhayein ke Boolean embedding (y1 = −1, y2 = +1) ke saath signed distance bilkul d(ŷ) = ŷ ban jaata hai.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

Take a = y₁ = -1, b = y₂ = +1, m = 1 : 2(1 - (-1))ŷ - (1)² + (-1)² 4ŷ - 1 + 1 4ŷ d(ŷ) = --------------------------- = ------------ = --- = ŷ 2 |1 - (-1)| 4 4

So d(ŷ) = ŷ. The scalar prediction is the signed distance to the decision boundary.

Why this matters: it explains why signed distance never appeared in the Boolean case — ŷ was already doing that job, which is why the boundary is ŷ = 0 and the margin is v·ŷ. With K representatives there is no single such number, so dj|k must be computed per competing pair. This identity is also the step needed to show the multi-class hinge and logistic losses reduce to their Boolean forms.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Tareeqa: formula mein a = −1, b = +1 daal dein. Oopar 4ŷ − 1 + 1 = 4ŷ, neeche 2|1−(−1)| = 4. To d = 4ŷ/4 = ŷ.

Matlab: Boolean case mein ŷ khud hi signed distance hai — alag se kuch nikaalne ki zaroorat nahi.

Teen cheezein is se khul jaati hain:
(1) Boolean mein signed distance ka zikr kyun nahi aaya — kyunki ŷ wohi kaam kar raha tha.
(2) Boundary ŷ = 0 kyun hai, aur margin v·ŷ kyun hai.
(3) Multi-class mein K representatives hote hain, to aisa ek number nahi milta — is liye har competitor ke liye dj|k alag nikaalna padta hai.
Aur yehi identity multi-class hinge/logistic ko Boolean par reduce karne ke liye zaroori qadam hai (dekhein sawal 50, sawal 51).

496 marks

In the multi-class loss condition, why is a max over j ≠ k used rather than a sum?

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Multi-class loss ki shart mein j ≠ k par max kyun liya jaata hai, jama (sum) kyun nahi?

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

Because correct un-embedding requires ŷ to be closer to yk than to every other representative — losing to even one competitor gives the wrong class. The compact way to say "all dj|k are negative" is "the largest of them is negative":

max dj|k(ŷ) < 0 ⇔ dj|k(ŷ) < 0 for all j ≠ k j≠k

A sum would be wrong: it could be negative while one individual term is strongly positive — i.e. the point is misclassified yet the loss reports success, because other comfortable margins mask the failure. The max tracks only the single most dangerous competitor, which is exactly what decides the class.

Analogy: to win a race you must beat everyone, so it suffices to check that you beat the fastest rival.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Kyun: sahi un-embedding ke liye ŷ ko saari doosri classes se jeetna zaroori hai — ek se bhi haar gaye to jawab ghalat. Aur "saari d negative hain" kehne ka sab se chhota tareeqa ye hai: "sab se bari d bhi negative hai".

Jama (sum) se kaam kyun nahi chalta — ye asal jawab hai: socha jaaye ek term bohat positive hai (yaani ek class se buri tarah haar gaye) magar baaqi terms bohat negative hain. To total negative aa jaayega, aur loss kehega "sab theek hai" — jabke point ghalat classify hua hai! Doosre comfortable margins is nakami ko chhupa lenge.

Max is masle se bachata hai — wo sirf sab se khatarnaak competitor dekhta hai, aur class ka faisla bilkul wohi karta hai.

Race ki misaal: jeetne ke liye sab ko haraana hai — to bas ye dekh lein ke aap ne sab se tez wale ko haraya. Baaqi ka hisaab bekaar hai.

506 marks

Write the multi-class hinge loss and show that for K = 2 with κ1 = κ2 = 1 it reduces to the usual hinge loss.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Multi-class hinge loss likhein, aur dikhayein ke K = 2 aur κ1 = κ2 = 1 ke saath wo aam (usual) hinge loss ban jaata hai.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

ℓk(ŷ) = κk · max ( 1 + dj|k(ŷ) )+ j≠k For K = 2, y₁ = -1, y₂ = +1, there is only ONE competitor each, and from sawal 48: d2|1 = ŷ, d1|2 = -ŷ. k = 1 (v = -1): ℓ₁(ŷ) = κ₁(1 + ŷ)+ = (1 + ŷ)+ k = 2 (v = +1): ℓ₂(ŷ) = κ₂(1 - ŷ)+ = (1 - ŷ)+

These are exactly the Boolean hinge losses. QED

Note: the maxj≠k disappears because with K = 2 there is a single competitor, and the maximum of one item is that item. Properties: convex but not differentiable; zero when ŷ is correctly un-embedded with margin at least one; with quadratic regularization it is the multi-class SVM.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Formula: ℓk(ŷ) = κk · maxj≠k (1 + dj|k(ŷ))+

Reduce karne ka tareeqa — do qadam:

  1. K = 2 mein competitor sirf ek hota hai, to max ghayab ho jaata hai (ek cheez ka max wohi cheez hai).
  2. sawal 48 se d = ŷ (class 1 ke liye) aur d = −ŷ (class 2 ke liye) daal dein.
v = -1 → (1 + ŷ)+ v = +1 → (1 - ŷ)+ ← bilkul Boolean hinge loss!

Teen khoobiyan yaad rakhein: convex hai magar differentiable nahi; sahi un-embedding par margin kam az kam 1 ke saath zero ho jaata hai; aur quadratic regularization ke saath isay multi-class SVM kehte hain.

516 marks

Write the multi-class logistic loss. Why does the sum include j = k, and what does that term contribute?

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Multi-class logistic loss likhein. Us ke summation mein j = k bhi shaamil kyun hai, aur wo term kya deta hai?

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

K ℓk(ŷ) = κk · log Σ exp( dj|k(ŷ) ) where dk|k = 0 j=1

The j = k term contributes exp(dk|k) = exp(0) = 1. That constant 1 is what keeps the sum strictly greater than zero, so the loss stays positive and well defined no matter how negative the other distances become — and it is exactly the 1 that appears in the Boolean form log(1 + eu):

k = 1 (v = -1): κ₁ log(e0 + eŷ) = κ₁ log(1 + eŷ) k = 2 (v = +1): κ₂ log(e-ŷ + e0) = κ₂ log(1 + e-ŷ)

Properties: convex and differentiable — both, unlike hinge. Called multi-class logistic regression. Note log Σ exp(·) is a smooth approximation of max, so this loss is essentially a smoothed version of the multi-class hinge.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Formula: ℓk(ŷ) = κk log Σj=1..K exp(dj|k(ŷ)), jahan dk|k = 0.

j = k kya deta hai: exp(0) = 1. Aur wo 1 hi loss ko hamesha positive rakhta hai — chahe baaqi saari distances kitni bhi negative ho jaayein, total 1 se kam nahi hota, to log ka jawab mojood rehta hai.

Aur yehi wo 1 hai jo Boolean formula mein nazar aata hai:

v = -1 → log(e⁰ + e^ŷ) = log(1 + e^ŷ) v = +1 → log(e^-ŷ + e⁰) = log(1 + e^-ŷ)

Do ahem baatein:
(1) Ye convex aur differentiable — dono hai (hinge sirf convex hai). Naam: multi-class logistic regression.
(2) log Σ exp(·) asal mein max ka smooth naqal hai. Yaani logistic loss hinge loss ka smooth version hai — dono ek hi baat keh rahe hain, ek sakhti se aur ek narmi se. (Agar aap ne "softmax" suna hai, to wo yehi cheez hai.)

526 marksStanford HW7 Q2

The Iris data gives error 16.67% with two sepal features and 1.33% with all four. Interpret both confusion matrices and recommend a next step.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Iris data par do sepal features se error 16.67% aaya aur chaaron features se 1.33%. Dono confusion matrices ka tajziya karein aur agla qadam tajweez karein.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

2 features: C = [[50,0,0],[0,38,13],[0,12,37]] trace 125 → 16.67% 4 features: C = [[50,0,0],[0,49, 1],[0, 1,49]] trace 148 → 1.33%

Interpretation — three findings:

  • Setosa is perfectly separable in both: column 1 is (50,0,0) even with only two features.
  • All errors are between versicolor and virginica, and are nearly symmetric (13/12, then 1/1) — indicating genuine class overlap, not model bias toward one class.
  • The petal features carry the discriminating information: adding them cuts the error by a factor of ~12.5, so sepal measurements alone cannot separate those two species.

Recommended next step: these figures are training errors ("trained on all data"), so they are optimistic. Re-evaluate with a train/test split before claiming 1.33%.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Teen nataij (tarteeb se likhein):

  • Setosa bilkul alag hai — dono matrices mein pehla column (50,0,0), yaani do features par bhi ek ghalti nahi.
  • Saari ghaltiyan versicolor aur virginica ke beech hain, aur taqreeban symmetric (13/12, phir 1/1). Yaani model kisi ek taraf jhuka hua nahi — ye do classes waqai aapas mein ghuli mili hain.
  • Asli maloomat petal features mein hai — unhein milaane se error 12.5 guna kam ho gaya. Sepal ki measurements in do species mein farq nahi kar sakti.

Agla qadam: slide likhti hai "trained on all data" — yaani ye training error hai, test error nahi. Is liye 1.33% par bharosa karne se pehle train/test split kar ke dobara naapein.

Muqabla kar ke dikhayein: Australian weather study mein 80/20 split kiya gaya tha aur likha gaya ke train/test nataij taqreeban barabar thay — isi liye wahan 16% par bharosa kiya ja sakta hai, aur yahan 1.33% par nahi.

Aakhri 10 minute — sirf ye dekhein

FORWARD PASS z = W₁x → h = ReLU(z) → f = W₂h (negative ko zero karo; teeno alag likho) PARAMETERS (di-1 + 1) × di per layer, phir JAMA "+1" = bias; input layer mein parameter NAHI PERCEPTRON margin = y(wTx) margin < 0 → w ← w + ηyx → VERIFY karo CONFUSION error rate = 1 - trace/n recall = tp/P precision = tp/(tp+fp) NEYMAN-PEARSON NP = (κ·fn + fp)/n κ = 1 → error rate UN-EMBED Boolean: sign(ŷ) one-hot: argmax ŷk LOSS FRAMEWORK ℓ(ŷ,-1) = p(ŷ) ℓ(ŷ,+1) = κ p(-ŷ) hinge p(u) = (1+u)+ logistic p(u) = log(1+eu) square p(u) = (1+u)² — sahi predictions ko bhi saza NAAM square+square = LS | logistic = log.reg | hinge+square = SVM

Teen aakhri nasihatein: (1) numerical sawal mein formula pehle likhein, phir numbers — hisaab ghalat ho jaaye to formula ke marks bach jaate hain. (2) "without biases" aur "includes a bias" dhyan se padhein — ek lafz poora jawab badal deta hai. (3) Har jawab ke aakhir mein ek line wajah likhein — rubric mein "technical justification" ke marks isi mein hain.

B3 — sawal 53–58

Neyman-Pearson metric aur κ

κ teen lectures mein chhe jagah aata hai (Lec-7 Boolean, Lec-7 multi-class, Lec-7 ERM, Lec-8 setup, Lec-8 losses, Lec-8 multi-class). Yaani is par sawal aana taqreeban yaqeeni hai.

536 marksStanford HW6 Q2(a)

Define the Neyman-Pearson metric for Boolean classification. What does κ control, and what happens when κ = 1?

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Boolean classification ke liye Neyman-Pearson metric ki tareef likhein. κ kis cheez ko control karta hai, aur κ = 1 par kya hota hai?

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

NP = ( κ · fn + fp ) / n κ > 0

κ sets how much we care about false negatives relative to false positives:

  • κ > 1 — false negatives upset us more (e.g. cancer screening)
  • κ < 1 — false negatives upset us less (e.g. spam filtering)
  • κ = 1 — NP = e, the overall error rate

Why it exists: we have two objectives (false positive and false negative rate) and want both small, but model selection needs a single number. κ combines them — a scalarization of two objectives.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Formula: NP = (κ · fn + fp) / n, jahan κ > 0.

κ kya control karta hai: ye batata hai ke false negatives ki, false positives ke muqable mein, hamein kitni fikar hai.

  • κ > 1 → false negatives zyada buri (cancer test)
  • κ < 1 → false negatives kam buri (spam filter)
  • κ = 1 → NP bilkul error rate ban jaata hai

Ye cheez kyun banayi gayi: hamare paas do maqsad hain (FP rate aur FN rate chhoti karna), magar model chunne ke liye ek number chahiye — do numbers par muqabla nahi ho sakta. κ dono ko ek mein mila deta hai. Is kaam ka technical naam scalarization hai.

546 marksStanford HW6 Q2(a)

A bank's fraud detector must not miss fraud, but blocking legitimate cards is also expensive. Explain how you would set κ, and how it enters both validation and training.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Ek bank ka fraud detector fraud ko chhod nahi sakta, magar sahi customers ke cards block karna bhi mehnga padta hai. Aap κ kaise tay karenge, aur wo validation aur training — dono mein kaise aata hai?

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

Setting κ: take fraud as the positive class and set κ to the ratio of real costs. If an undetected fraud costs on average Rs 50,000 and a wrongly blocked card costs Rs 2,000 in support and goodwill, then κ ≈ 25. κ should come from the business cost ratio, not be guessed.

In validation: report NP = (κ·fn + fp)/n on test data and select the model minimising it — not the error rate, which would under-weight missed fraud.

In training: scale the loss of positive-class examples by κ: use ℓ(ŷ,+1) = κ·p(−ŷ). ERM then automatically pushes the boundary toward catching fraud.

Consistency matters: use the same κ in both. Training with κ=1 and validating with κ=25 optimises one thing and measures another.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

κ kaise tay karein: fraud ko positive class maanein, aur κ ko asli qeematon ka nisbat (ratio) rakhein. Misaal: ek chhoota hua fraud औsatan Rs 50,000 ka nuqsan, aur ghalti se block kiya card Rs 2,000 (support call + customer ki naraazi). To κ ≈ 25.
