Ahem: κ ko andaaze se nahi, business ke cost ratio se nikaalein — yehi sahi tareeqa hai aur yehi likhna chahiye.

Validation mein: test data par NP = (κ·fn + fp)/n report karein aur wo model chunein jis ka NP sab se kam ho — error rate par faisla na karein, kyunki wo chhoote hue fraud ko kam ahmiyat dega.

Training mein: positive class ke examples ka loss κ se multiply kar dein: ℓ(ŷ,+1) = κ·p(−ŷ). Phir ERM khud ba khud boundary ko fraud pakadne ki taraf kheench lega.

Sab se ahem baat: dono jagah wohi κ rakhein. Agar training κ=1 se karein aur validation κ=25 se, to aap ek cheez optimize kar rahe hain aur doosri naap rahe hain — ye sab se badi ghalti hai.

556 marks

On the plane with fn/n on one axis and fp/n on the other, what do lines of constant Neyman-Pearson error look like, and what is their slope?

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Ek graph banayein jis ke ek axis par fn/n ho aur doosre par fp/n. Jin nuqton par Neyman-Pearson error barabar ho, wo kaisi shakal banate hain, aur un ka slope kya hota hai?

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

Setting κ(fn/n) + (fp/n) = c and solving for fp/n:

fp/n = c - κ (fn/n)

These are straight parallel lines with slope −κ. Every classifier lying on one such line has the same NP error — they are iso-cost lines. Lines closer to the origin correspond to smaller c, i.e. better classifiers.

Consequence: changing κ changes the tilt of the lines, which can change which classifier is judged best. This is why κ must be fixed before comparing models (see sawal 78).

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: seedhi, aapas mein mutawazi (parallel) lines, jin ka slope −κ hota hai.

Nikaalne ka tareeqa: κ(fn/n) + (fp/n) = c likhein aur fp/n ko alag karein:

fp/n = c - κ(fn/n) ← y = c - κx ki shakal, slope = -κ

Matlab: ek line par baithe saare classifiers hamare liye barabar ache hain (isi liye inhein "iso-cost lines" kehte hain). Jo line origin ke zyada qareeb ho, wo behtar classifiers ki hai.

Sab se ahem nateeja: κ badalne se lines ka tedhapan (tilt) badal jaata hai — aur phir mumkin hai ke koi doosra classifier "best" nikal aaye. Isi liye model ka muqabla karne se pehle κ tay karna zaroori hai (dekhein sawal 78).

566 marks

For a 3-class medical classifier with V = {COVID19, FLU, COLD}, explain how the multi-class Neyman-Pearson metric is defined and how you would choose κ.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Teen classes waale medical classifier ke liye (COVID19, FLU, COLD): multi-class Neyman-Pearson metric ki tareef likhein, aur batayein ke aap κ kaise chunenge.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

K NP = Σ κk rk / n rk = Σj≠k Cjk k=1 rk = number of times class k was mistaken for another class κ = (κ₁,…,κK), a weight vector with nonnegative entries κk = how much we care about mistaking class k

Choosing κ: weight by the cost of missing each disease. Missing COVID is worst (delayed treatment plus onward transmission), missing flu is moderate, missing a cold is minor — so for example κ = (10, 3, 1).

Effect and check: the model will lean toward predicting COVID when uncertain — raising COVID recall at the cost of some false COVID alarms, which is exactly the intent. With κk = 1 for all k, NP reduces to the plain error rate.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Formula: NP = Σk κk rk / n, jahan rk = "class k ko kitni baar kisi doosri class samjha" (yaani column k ka off-diagonal total).

Boolean se farq: wahan κ ek number tha, yahan ek vector hai — har class ke liye ek weight.

κ kaise chunein: har bimari chhodne ke nuqsan ke hisaab se:

κ = ( 10 , 3 , 1 ) COVID FLU COLD

COVID chhodna sab se bura (ilaaj der se + marz doosron mein phailega), FLU darmiyana, COLD sab se kam nuqsan.

Asar: model shaq hone par COVID ki taraf jhuk jaayega — COVID ka recall barhega, magar kuch sehatmand logon ko bhi COVID keh dega. Aur hamein yehi chahiye tha. Aur hamesha wo check line likhein: saare κk = 1 par NP bilkul error rate ban jaata hai.

576 marks

Why is the Neyman-Pearson loss not used for training, even though it matches the Neyman-Pearson metric exactly?

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Neyman-Pearson loss bilkul Neyman-Pearson metric jaisa hi hai — phir bhi usay training ke liye istemaal nahi kiya jaata. Kyun?

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

Because it cannot be optimized. The NP loss is a step function: p(u) = 1 for u ≥ 0 and 0 otherwise. Hence L(θ) is discontinuous and has zero derivative almost everywhere, so gradient-based methods get no direction to move in — small parameter changes leave the loss unchanged, then it jumps.

What is done instead: use a proxy (surrogate) loss that approximates the NP loss but is convex and/or differentiable — hinge or logistic. With a convex loss and convex regularizer, RERM can be solved efficiently.

The surprising part, worth stating: proxy losses do not merely make optimization possible — the slides note we often get better performance with them. They reward confident correctness, not just correctness, which improves generalization.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: kyunki wo optimize hi nahi ho sakta.

Kyun: NP loss ek seedhi (step) shakal hai — theek par 0, ghalat par 1. Is liye:

  • Wo discontinuous hai (beech mein chhalaang hai)
  • Uska derivative taqreeban har jagah zero hai

Optimizer gradient par chalta hai, aur gradient har jagah zero hai — to usay koi rasta nahi milta. Weights thora badlein, loss bilkul nahi badalta; phir achanak poora 1 badal jaata hai.

Andhere kamre ki misaal: bilkul chapta farsh — koi ishaara nahi milta ke darwaza kis taraf hai. Proxy loss farsh ko dhalwaan bana deta hai.

Aur ek hairat ki baat zaroor likhein: proxy loss sirf optimization mumkin nahi banata — slides kehti hain ke us se aksar behtar performance bhi milti hai. Wajah: wo sirf "theek hona" nahi, "yaqeen se theek hona" ka inaam deta hai, aur isi se generalization behtar hota hai.

586 marks

Write the Boolean loss function that implements the Neyman-Pearson metric with square loss, as given in the lectures, and explain which deviation it weights more.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Wo Boolean loss function likhein jo square loss ke saath Neyman-Pearson metric ko lागू karta hai (jaisa lectures mein diya hai), aur batayein ke wo kis taraf ke farq ko zyada weight deta hai.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

With embedding y1 = −1, y2 = +1, and NP metric (κ·fn + fp)/n:

┌ ‖ŷ - y₁‖² if v = -1 ℓ(ŷ, v) = │ └ κ ‖ŷ - y₂‖² otherwise (v = +1)

It gives more weight to deviating from the positive representative y2 (for κ > 1). So errors on positive examples — which become false negatives — are penalised κ times harder.

General pattern: this is the rule "scale the losses by κ" — take the unweighted losses ℓk and use κkℓk instead. It applies to any loss, not just square: the cost-sensitive hinge loss κ(1−ŷ)+ of the sample paper's Q-4 is the same device.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Loss function:

v = -1 → ‖ŷ - y₁‖² (normal weight) v = +1 → κ ‖ŷ - y₂‖² (κ guna weight)

Kis taraf zyada weight: positive representative y2 se hatne par (jab κ > 1 ho). Yaani positive examples par hone wali ghalti — jo false negative banti hai — us ki saza κ guna hai.

Aam qaida jo yaad rakhna hai: "loss ko κ se scale kar do" — bagair-weight waale ℓk ki jagah κkℓk istemaal karein. Aur ye kisi bhi loss par lagta hai, sirf square par nahi — sample paper ke Q-4 ka κ(1−ŷ)+ (cost-sensitive hinge) bilkul yehi tarkeeb hai.

B4 — sawal 59–70

Naye Boolean classification numericals

Paper mein is section se 3 sawal aate hain. Asli paper mein wo Q7 (hubristic loss), Q8 (signed distance) aur Q10 thay. Ye bara set is liye diya ja raha hai ke har loss function ka numerical aa sakta hai — aur B4.12 khaas tor par Q10 jaisa sawal hai.

596 marks

With κ = 2.0 and score ŷ = −1.5, compute the hinge loss for (a) y = −1 and (b) y = +1.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

κ = 2.0, score ŷ = −1.5. Hinge loss nikaalein — (a) jab asli label −1 ho, (b) jab +1 ho.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

(a) y = -1 : ℓ = (1 + ŷ)+ = max(0, 1 - 1.5) = max(0, -0.5) = 0 (b) y = +1 : ℓ = κ(1 - ŷ)+ = 2.0 × max(0, 1 + 1.5) = 2.0 × 2.5 = 5.00

Interpretation: for y = −1 the prediction is correct with margin ≥ 1, so hinge gives exactly zero — the point is fully forgiven and no longer influences training. For y = +1 the same score is badly wrong, and κ doubles the penalty.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: (a) 0, (b) 5.00.

(a) Zero kyun: 1 − 1.5 = −0.5 negative hai, aur (·)+ negative ko zero kar deta hai. Matlab: prediction theek bhi hai aur margin bhi poora (1 se zyada) — poori maafi.

Yaad rakhein: ek hi score (−1.5) par label badalne se loss 0 se 5.0 ho gaya. Aur κ sirf y = +1 par laga — (a) mein 2.0 se multiply nahi karna.

606 marks

With κ = 2.0 and ŷ = 0.8, compute the logistic loss for both labels.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

κ = 2.0, ŷ = 0.8. Logistic loss dono labels ke liye nikaalein.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

(a) y = -1 : ℓ = log(1 + e^ŷ) = log(1 + e^0.8) = log(1 + 2.2255) = log(3.2255) = 1.171 (b) y = +1 : ℓ = κ log(1 + e^(-ŷ)) = 2.0 log(1 + e^(-0.8)) = 2.0 log(1.4493) = 2.0(0.3711) = 0.742

Note: neither value is zero — logistic loss is never exactly zero, unlike hinge. Even the correct case (b) keeps a small residual pressure pushing the score further positive.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: (a) 1.171, (b) 0.742.

ŷ = 0.8 positive hai, to y = +1 wala case theek hai — is liye uska loss chhota (0.742), aur y = −1 wala ghalat, is liye bada (1.171).

Hinge se farq (ye likhein): logistic kabhi bilkul zero nahi hota. Theek case mein bhi 0.742 bacha hai — yaani wo kehta rehta hai "aur yaqeen se theek ho jao". Hinge margin 1 ke baad bilkul chup ho jaata hai.

616 marks

For ŷ = −2.5 with true label y = −1 and κ = 1, compute the square, hinge and logistic losses. Which loss behaves badly here, and why?

Sawal aam alfaz mein

ŷ = −2.5, asli label −1. Teeno loss nikaalein: square, hinge, logistic. Phir batayein kaunsa loss yahan bura rawayya dikha raha hai aur kyun.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

Margin = yŷ = (-1)(-2.5) = +2.5 → CONFIDENTLY CORRECT Square : (1 + ŷ)² = (1 - 2.5)² = (-1.5)² = 2.25 Hinge : (1 + ŷ)+ = max(0, -1.5) = 0 Logistic : log(1+e^ŷ) = log(1 + e^-2.5) = 0.079

Square loss behaves badly. The prediction is correct and confident, yet square loss charges 2.25 — and that penalty grows as the prediction improves further. It is not monotonically non-increasing in the margin.

Consequence: during ERM, square loss drags confident correct predictions back toward the boundary, wasting capacity and distorting the fit. Hinge (exactly 0) and logistic (0.079, decaying) both behave correctly.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: square 2.25, hinge 0, logistic 0.079. Square loss bura rawayya dikha raha hai.

Haalat: margin = +2.5 — prediction theek bhi hai aur poore yaqeen se theek hai, yaani sab se behtareen surat.

Phir bhi square loss 2.25 ki saza de raha hai — aur ŷ aur negative karein to saza aur barhegi. Yehi kharabi hai.

Amli nuqsan: training mein model is saza se bachne ke liye apni sahi predictions ko boundary ki taraf wapas kheench lega. Hinge (bilkul 0) aur logistic (0.079, gir raha) dono theek hain. Ye wohi masla hai jo I3 aur sample paper ke Q-4 mein tha.

626 marks

A Boolean classifier on 200 test points gives tn = 150, fp = 30, fn = 12, tp = 8. With κ = 5, compute the error rate, the Neyman-Pearson error, and the recall.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

200 test points par tn=150, fp=30, fn=12, tp=8. κ = 5 ke saath error rate, Neyman-Pearson error, aur recall nikaalein.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

n = 150+30+12+8 = 200 N = tn+fp = 180 P = fn+tp = 20 error rate = (fn+fp)/n = 42/200 = 0.210 = 21.0% NP (κ=5) = (5(12)+30)/200 = (60+30)/200 = 0.450 recall = tp/P = 8/20 = 0.400 = 40.0%

Interpretation: the NP error (0.450) is more than double the plain error rate (0.210), because κ = 5 weights the 12 false negatives five times over. With only 40% recall this classifier misses 3 of every 5 positives — so under a cost structure that cares about positives, it is far worse than "21% error" suggests.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: error rate 21.0%, NP error 0.450, recall 40.0%.

Pehle N aur P nikaalein — warna denominator ghalat lag jaata hai: N = 180 (asli negatives), P = 20 (asli positives).

Asal nuktah: NP error (0.450) simple error rate (0.210) se do guna se zyada hai — kyunki κ = 5 un 12 false negatives ko paanch guna ginta hai. Aur recall sirf 40% hai, yaani har 5 mein se 3 positives chhoot rahe hain. "21% error" sun kar model acha lagta hai, magar hai bura.

636 marksStanford HW6 Q2(b,c)

On n = 200 test points, classifier A gives fn = 20, fp = 5 and classifier B gives fn = 8, fp = 25. Which is better at κ = 1, and at κ = 4?

Sawal aam alfaz mein

200 points par: A ka fn=20, fp=5; B ka fn=8, fp=25. Kaunsa behtar hai — κ = 1 par, aur κ = 4 par?

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

NP = (κ · fn + fp)/n κ = 1 : A = (20 + 5)/200 = 25/200 = 0.125 B = ( 8 + 25)/200 = 33/200 = 0.165 → A is better κ = 4 : A = (80 + 5)/200 = 85/200 = 0.425 B = (32 + 25)/200 = 57/200 = 0.285 → B is better

The ranking reverses. At κ = 1 (plain error rate) A wins; at κ = 4 B wins, because B has far fewer false negatives and κ weights exactly those.

Key lesson: "which classifier is better" is not a well-posed question without κ. κ must be fixed from the application's cost structure before models are compared.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: κ=1 par A behtar (0.125 vs 0.165); κ=4 par B behtar (0.285 vs 0.425).

Tarteeb palat gayi! Yehi is sawal ka asal maqsad hai.

Kyun: B ke false negatives bohat kam hain (8 vs 20), aur κ bilkul usi cheez ko weight deta hai. κ bada karein to B ka faida nikal aata hai.

Sab se ahem sabaq: "kaunsa classifier behtar hai" ye sawal κ ke bagair adhoora hai. κ ko application ki asli cost se pehle tay karein, phir models ka muqabla karein.

646 marks

Using the hubristic loss (0 for u < −1; (u+1)² for −1 ≤ u ≤ 0; 1+2u for u > 0) with κ = 2 and ŷ = 0.5, compute both losses.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Hubristic loss (teen tukdon wala), κ = 2, ŷ = 0.5. Dono labels ke liye loss nikaalein.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

(a) y = -1 : evaluate p at ŷ = +0.5. Since 0.5 > 0 → third piece ℓ = 1 + 2(0.5) = 2.00 (b) y = +1 : ℓ = κ · p(-ŷ) = 2 · p(-0.5) -0.5 ∈ [-1, 0] → second piece p(-0.5) = (-0.5 + 1)² = (0.5)² = 0.25 ℓ = 2 × 0.25 = 0.50

Note: the two labels land on different pieces of the piecewise function — third for (a), second for (b). Always decide which piece before computing.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: (a) 2.00, (b) 0.50.

Dono mein alag tukda lagta hai

(a) mein ŷ = +0.5 > 0 → teesra tukda. (b) mein −ŷ = −0.5 ∈ [−1,0] → doosra tukda. Pehle tay karein kaunsa tukda, phir hisaab.

Aur yaad rakhein: (b) mein −ŷ daalna hai, ŷ nahi — yehi wo phisalan hai jo asli paper ke Q-7 mein thi.

656 marks

The sigmoid loss is ℓsig(ŷ, −1) = 1/(1 + e−ŷ), with ℓ(ŷ,+1) = κℓ(−ŷ,−1). For κ = 3 and ŷ = −0.40, compute both losses. Why is this loss avoided in RERM?

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Sigmoid loss ka formula diya hai. κ = 3, ŷ = −0.40 par dono losses nikaalein. Aur batayein ke RERM mein ye loss kyun istemaal nahi kiya jaata.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

(a) y = -1 : 1/(1 + e^(0.40)) = 1/(1 + 1.4918) = 1/2.4918 = 0.401 (b) y = +1 : κ · 1/(1 + e^(-0.40)) = 3 × 1/(1 + 0.6703) = 3 × 0.5987 = 1.796

Why avoided: sigmoid loss is not convex (it is S-shaped). A non-convex loss makes the empirical risk non-convex, so RERM can have many local minima: the optimizer may stall in one, the solution depends on initialisation, and there is no global-optimality guarantee.

Contrast: logistic, hinge and hubristic are all convex, so with a convex regularizer the RERM problem is solved efficiently and reliably. Sigmoid approximates the Neyman-Pearson loss more closely, but solvability beats closeness.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: (a) 0.401, (b) 1.796.

Dhyan: (a) mein exponent ka nishaan palat jaata hai — e−(−0.40) = e+0.40. Yahan log ghalti karte hain.

Kyun istemaal nahi hota: sigmoid loss convex nahi hai ("S" ki shakal). Is se RERM mein kai local minima ban jaate hain — optimizer kisi chhote garhe mein phans sakta hai, aur jawab shuruati guess par depend karega. Koi zamanat nahi. Logistic, hinge aur hubristic teeno convex hain, is liye wohi istemaal hote hain.

666 marks

Prototypes are a = [1, 1]T (Class 1) and b = [5, 3]T (Class 2). Find the decision hyperplane, then compute the signed distance of ŷ = [2, 2]T and give its class.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Do prototypes: a = [1,1] (Class 1) aur b = [5,3] (Class 2). Decision hyperplane nikaalein, phir ŷ = [2,2] ka signed distance aur uski class batayein.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

b - a = [4, 2] ‖a‖² = 2 ‖b‖² = 34 Hyperplane: 2(b-a)Tx - ‖b‖² + ‖a‖² = 0 8x₁ + 4x₂ - 34 + 2 = 0 8x₁ + 4x₂ - 32 = 0 ÷4 2x₁ + x₂ = 8 Check midpoint (a+b)/2 = (3,2): 2(3) + 2 = 8 ✓ Signed distance of ŷ = [2,2]: numerator = 8(2) + 4(2) - 32 = 16 + 8 - 32 = -8 denominator = 2‖b-a‖ = 2√20 = 8.944 D = -8/8.944 = -0.894 D < 0 → closer to a → CLASS 1

Cross-check: ‖ŷ−a‖² = 1+1 = 2, ‖ŷ−b‖² = 9+1 = 10. Since 2 < 10, Class 1 — agreeing with the sign of D.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: hyperplane 2x1 + x2 = 8; D = −0.894; class Class 1.

Midpoint check zaroor karein: (a+b)/2 = (3, 2), aur 2(3)+2 = 8 ✓. 20 second mein poora jawab verify ho jaata hai.

Nishaan ka matlab: D negative → a ke qareeb → Class 1. Aur seedhe faasle se bhi check kar lein: 2 < 10 ✓ wohi jawab.

676 marks

A 3-class model with one-hot embedding outputs ŷ = (1.2, −0.3, 0.9). Un-embed it by both the distance rule and the argmax rule, and show they agree.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

3-class model (one-hot embedding) ne ŷ = (1.2, −0.3, 0.9) diya. Dono tareeqon se un-embed karein — distance se aur argmax se — aur dikhayein ke dono ka jawab ek hai.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

DISTANCE RULE: ‖ŷ-e₁‖² = (0.2)²+(0.3)²+(0.9)² = 0.04+0.09+0.81 = 0.94 ← smallest ‖ŷ-e₂‖² = (1.2)²+(1.3)²+(0.9)² = 1.44+1.69+0.81 = 3.94 ‖ŷ-e₃‖² = (1.2)²+(0.3)²+(0.1)² = 1.44+0.09+0.01 = 1.54 ARGMAX RULE: largest entry = 1.2 at position 1 BOTH give v-hat = CLASS 1 ✓

Why they must agree: ‖ŷ−ek‖² = ‖ŷ‖² + 1 − 2ŷk; the first two terms don't depend on k, so minimising the distance is identical to maximising ŷk. Note the entries need not sum to 1 — these are raw scores, not probabilities.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: Class 1 — dono tareeqon se.

Distance se: teen faasle nikaalein, sab se chhota 0.94 (class 1). Argmax se: sab se badi entry 1.2, position 1. Bas.

Dono hamesha milenge, kyunki ‖ŷ−ek‖² khol kar dekhein to do terms k par depend nahi karte — sirf −2ŷk bachta hai, aur usay chhota karna = ŷk bada karna. Exam mein argmax likhein, aur ye ek line wajah bhi (H16).

686 marksStanford HW6 Q2(b,c)

A positive example (y = +1) receives score ŷ = −0.2. Compute its hinge loss for κ = 1, κ = 3 and κ = 10, and explain what the trend does to the trained classifier.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Ek positive example (y = +1) ko score −0.2 mila. Hinge loss nikaalein teen values par: κ = 1, 3, 10. Aur batayein ke is trend ka trained classifier par kya asar hoga.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

ℓ(ŷ, +1) = κ(1 - ŷ)+ = κ × max(0, 1 + 0.2) = κ × 1.2 κ = 1 : 1.2 κ = 3 : 3.6 κ = 10 : 12.0

Effect: this point is a false negative (true +1, score negative). Raising κ multiplies its penalty, so during ERM the optimizer is pushed harder to move the boundary until this point is on the positive side.

Resulting trade-off: higher κ → fewer false negatives and higher recall, but the boundary shifts toward predicting positive more freely, so false positives rise and precision falls. That is exactly the trade-off κ exists to control.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: 1.2, 3.6, 12.0.

Ye point ek false negative hai — asli class +1 thi magar score negative aaya. κ barhne se uski saza kai guna barh jaati hai.

Asar (ye likhein): bada κ → optimizer boundary ko hilane par majboor → false negatives kam, recall zyada. Magar qeemat ye hai ke model khul kar "positive" kehne lagta hai, to false positives barh jaate hain aur precision girti hai. κ ka poora maqsad yehi tawazun chunna hai.

696 marks

A least squares classifier produces ŷ = 0.6. With κ = 2, compute the square loss for both labels.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Least squares classifier ne ŷ = 0.6 diya. κ = 2 ke saath dono labels ka square loss nikaalein.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

(a) y = -1 : (1 + ŷ)² = (1 + 0.6)² = (1.6)² = 2.56 (b) y = +1 : κ(1 - ŷ)² = 2(1 - 0.6)² = 2(0.16) = 0.32

Name the model: square loss + square regularizer = least squares classifier. Its advantage is that ERM reduces to a least-squares problem solvable exactly in closed form; its defect is the one shown in B4.3 — it penalises confident correct predictions.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: (a) 2.56, (b) 0.32.

ŷ = 0.6 positive hai, to y = +1 wala case theek → chhota loss (0.32), aur y = −1 wala ghalat → bada (2.56).

Naam yaad rakhein: square loss + square regularizer = least squares classifier. Faida: ERM formula se seedha hal ho jaata hai. Nuqsan: B4.3 wali kharabi — sahi aur yaqeeni predictions ko bhi saza.

706 marksQ10 jaisa

A score ŷ = −1.5 is observed with true label y = −1. Compute the hinge, hubristic, square and logistic losses (κ = 1). Which losses give exactly zero, and what does that tell you about them?

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Score ŷ = −1.5, asli label −1. Chaaron losses nikaalein: hinge, hubristic, square, logistic (κ = 1). Kaunse losses bilkul zero dete hain, aur is se un ke baare mein kya pata chalta hai?

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

Margin = yŷ = (-1)(-1.5) = +1.5 → correct, with margin > 1 Hinge : (1 + ŷ)+ = max(0, -0.5) = 0 ← ZERO Hubristic : ŷ = -1.5 < -1 → first piece = 0 ← ZERO Square : (1 + ŷ)² = (-0.5)² = 0.25 Logistic : log(1 + e^(-1.5)) = log(1.2231) = 0.201

Hinge and hubristic give exactly zero. Both are defined to be flat at zero once the margin exceeds 1 — the point is fully forgiven and contributes nothing to the gradient, so it no longer influences the fitted boundary. This is what produces support vectors: only points on or inside the margin determine the solution.

The other two: logistic decays toward zero but never reaches it, so every point keeps a small influence. Square loss is worst here — it charges 0.25 for a prediction that is correct with room to spare, and that charge grows as the prediction improves.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: hinge 0, hubristic 0, square 0.25, logistic 0.201.

Haalat: margin = +1.5, yaani prediction theek hai aur margin 1 se zyada hai — sab se achi surat.

Hinge aur hubristic bilkul zero dete hain — kyunki dono ki tareef hi aisi hai ke margin 1 ke baad wo chapte (flat) ho jaate hain. Point ko poori maafi mil gayi, aur ab wo gradient mein koi hissa nahi daalta — yaani boundary par uska koi asar nahi. Yehi "support vectors" ka asal matlab hai: sirf wo points boundary tay karte hain jo margin par ya us ke andar hain.

Baaqi do: logistic zero ki taraf girta hai magar pohonchta kabhi nahi (0.201), to har point thora thora asar deta rehta hai. Aur square loss sab se bura — 0.25 ki saza us prediction par jo zaroorat se behtar hai, aur prediction aur behtar karein to saza aur barhegi.

Section C — paper mein 1 sawal

Classifier — short theoretical

Sawal 71–108 (6 marks). Sirf ek sawal aayega magar kaunsa, ye pata nahi — is liye poora Lecture-7 chhote sawalon mein tor diya gaya hai. Har jawab 3–4 line.

C1 — sawal 71–80

Confusion matrix, error rates aur metrics

Ye set sab se khatarnaak hai. Sample paper mein Lecture-7 se ek bhi sawal nahi aaya, jabke ye poora lecture hai — aur numerical sawal banane ke liye sab se aasaan. H6 ka table saamne rakh kar ye set karein.

716 marks

A Boolean classifier on 200 test examples gives tn = 120, fp = 20, fn = 30, tp = 30. Compute the error rate, false positive rate, false negative rate, recall and precision.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

200 test examples par ek Boolean classifier ne ye nataij diye: tn = 120, fp = 20, fn = 30, tp = 30. Error rate, false positive rate, false negative rate, recall aur precision nikaalein.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

n = 120+20+30+30 = 200 N = tn + fp = 120 + 20 = 140 (true negatives) P = fn + tp = 30 + 30 = 60 (true positives) error rate = (fn+fp)/n = 50/200 = 0.250 = 25.0% FPR = fp/N = 20/140 = 0.143 = 14.3% FNR = fn/P = 30/60 = 0.500 = 50.0% recall = tp/P = 30/60 = 0.500 = 50.0% precision = tp/(tp+fp) = 30/50 = 0.600 = 60.0%

Interpretation: 75% accuracy looks acceptable, but the classifier misses half of all positives (recall 50%). If positives are the cases that matter — disease, fraud — this model is unfit, and κ > 1 should be used to push recall up.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Pehle do numbers nikaalein (ye qadam log chhod dete hain aur phir denominator ghalat lagate hain):

N = asli negatives = tn + fp = 140 P = asli positives = fn + tp = 60

Phir seedha formula:

error rate = 50/200 = 25.0% FPR = 20/140 = 14.3% FNR = 30/60 = 50.0% recall = 30/60 = 50.0% precision = 30/50 = 60.0%

Asal nuktah — ye likhein: 75% accuracy theek lagti hai, magar recall sirf 50% hai — yaani aadhe asli positives chhoot gaye! Agar positives ahem cheez hain (bimari, fraud), to ye model na-qaabil-e-qubool hai. Hal: κ > 1 rakhein taake false negatives ki saza barhe aur recall oopar aaye.

726 marks

In a dataset of 1000 patients, 10 have a rare disease. A classifier predicts "no disease" for everyone. Compute its error rate and recall, and comment on whether it is a good classifier.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

1000 patients hain, jin mein se 10 ko ek kam-yaab bimari hai. Ek classifier har aik ko "bimari nahi" keh deta hai. Us ka error rate aur recall nikaalein, aur batayein ke ye acha classifier hai ya nahi.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

tn = 990, fn = 10, fp = 0, tp = 0 N = 990, P = 10 error rate = (fn+fp)/n = 10/1000 = 1.0% ("99% accurate") recall = tp/P = 0/10 = 0% precision = tp/(tp+fp)= 0/0 = undefined

It is a useless classifier despite 99% accuracy. It detects none of the 10 diseased patients (recall 0%), which is the only thing the test exists to do. Its "accuracy" comes entirely from the class imbalance — 99% of patients are healthy, so predicting the majority class is nearly always right.

Fix: never judge an imbalanced problem by error rate. Use the Neyman-Pearson metric (κ·fn + fp)/n with κ ≫ 1, which assigns this classifier a cost of 10κ/1000 — large for large κ, correctly marking it as bad. Also report recall alongside.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: error rate 1% (yaani "99% accurate"), magar recall 0%. Ye classifier bilkul bekaar hai.

Kyun bekaar: us ne 10 bimar logon mein se ek bhi nahi pakda. Aur test ka maqsad sirf yehi tha! Uski "99% accuracy" poori tarah class imbalance se aayi hai — 99% log sehatmand hain, to "sab theek hain" kehne par wo khud ba khud 99% baar sahi ho jaata hai.

Precision ka hisaab bhi nahi ho sakta — tp + fp = 0, yaani zero se taqseem. (Ye likhna bhi marks deta hai.)

Ilaaj: imbalanced data mein error rate par faisla kabhi na karein. Neyman-Pearson metric istemaal karein bade κ ke saath — phir is classifier ka cost 10κ/1000 ho jaayega, jo bade κ par bohat bada hai, aur metric khud bata dega ke model bura hai. Saath recall bhi zaroor report karein.

736 marks

Distinguish precision from recall, giving the formula for each and a scenario where one matters far more than the other.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Precision aur recall mein farq batayein, dono ka formula likhein, aur ek aisi misaal dein jahan ek doosre se kai guna zyada ahem ho.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

RECALL = tp / P = tp / (tp + fn) → of all the actual positives, how many did we find? PRECISION = tp / (tp + fp) → of all our positive predictions, how many were right?

They differ only in the denominator: recall divides by what truly is positive, precision by what we called positive.

Recall matters more — cancer screening. Missing a case (low recall) can be fatal; a false alarm only costs a follow-up test. Precision matters more — a spam filter. One spam reaching the inbox is trivial, but a legitimate email sent to spam (low precision) may be lost.

Trade-off: they cannot generally be raised together. Predicting "positive" more freely raises recall but lowers precision, and vice versa — this is exactly what κ tunes.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Farq sirf neeche waale (denominator) ka hai:

RECALL = tp/P ← neeche: jo ASLI mein positive thay PRECISION = tp/(tp+fp) ← neeche: jo HUM NE positive kaha

Machhli ki misaal: talaab mein 100 machhliyan hain. Aap ne jaal se 30 cheezein nikaleen — 24 machhliyan, 6 joote.

  • Recall = 24/100 = 24% — "talaab mein se kitni nikaaleen"
  • Precision = 24/30 = 80% — "jo nikaala us mein kitna machhli tha"

Kahan kya ahem hai:

  • Recall zyada ahem — cancer screening. Ek case chhoot gaya to banda mar sakta hai. Jhoota alarm sirf ek aur test karwa deta hai.
  • Precision zyada ahem — spam filter. Ek spam inbox mein aa jaaye to sirf delete karna hai. Magar zaroori email spam folder mein chala gaya to wo gum ho sakta hai.

Trade-off: dono ek saath nahi barhte. Zyada khul kar "positive" kehna shuru karein → recall barhega, precision girega. Aur κ ka kaam bilkul yehi tawazun tay karna hai.

746 marks

A 3-class confusion matrix is C = [[40, 2, 1], [3, 35, 8], [2, 6, 28]] (rows = predicted, columns = actual), with n = 125. Compute the overall error rate and identify the most serious confusion.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Ek 3-class confusion matrix di gayi hai (rows = jo predict kiya, columns = jo asal mein tha), n = 125. Overall error rate nikaalein aur batayein ke sab se bari confusion kaunsi hai.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

trace = 40 + 35 + 28 = 103 errors = 125 - 103 = 22 error rate = 22/125 = 0.176 = 17.6%

Most serious confusion: classes 2 and 3. C23 = 8 (class 3 mistaken for 2) and C32 = 6 (class 2 mistaken for 3) account for 14 of the 22 errors (64%).

Class 1 is nearly separable: its column has only 3 + 2 = 5 errors out of 45 examples, and it is rarely predicted wrongly.

Recommendation: the confusion is roughly symmetric (8 vs 6), indicating genuine overlap between classes 2 and 3 rather than model bias. Adding features that separate those two specifically would help far more than a larger model — exactly as petal features did for the Iris data.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: error rate 17.6%; sab se bari confusion class 2 aur 3 ke darmiyan.

Tez tareeqa: off-diagonal ke 6 numbers jama karne ke bajaye, diagonal jama karein aur ghata dein:

diagonal (trace) = 40 + 35 + 28 = 103 errors = 125 - 103 = 22 error rate = 22/125 = 17.6%

Sab se bari confusion: 8 + 6 = 14 ghaltiyan class 2 aur 3 ke beech hain — yaani 22 mein se 14 (64%). Class 1 taqreeban saaf hai (sirf 5 ghaltiyan).

Sifarish (ye likhein): confusion taqreeban symmetric hai (8 vs 6), yaani model kisi ek taraf jhuka hua nahi — ye do classes waqai aapas mein ghuli mili hain. Is liye bada model banane se kuch nahi hoga — aise features chahiye jo khaas tor par in do classes mein farq karein. (Iris mein bilkul yehi hua tha — petal features ne versicolor/virginica ka masla hal kiya.)

756 marks

For K classes, how many entries does the confusion matrix have, how many correspond to correct predictions, and how many distinct error types exist? Evaluate for K = 5.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

K classes ke liye: confusion matrix mein kitne khaane hote hain, un mein se kitne sahi predictions ke hain, aur kitni alag alag qism ki ghaltiyan mumkin hain? K = 5 par nikaalein.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

total entries = K² (all (v-hat, v) pairs) correct = K (the diagonal) error types = K² - K = K(K-1) (the off-diagonal) For K = 5: entries = 25 correct = 5 error types = 20

Why this matters: with 20 distinct error types, a single error rate collapses 20 numbers into one and hides which confusions are happening. This is why the full confusion matrix is reported, and why the multi-class Neyman-Pearson metric uses a weight vector κ = (κ1,…,κK) — different confusions can carry very different costs.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab:

kul khaane = K² sahi = K (diagonal) ghalti ki qismein = K(K-1) (off-diagonal) K = 5 → 25 khaane, 5 sahi, 20 qism ki ghaltiyan

Kyun ye ahem hai: 20 mukhtalif qism ki ghaltiyan hain — aur ek error rate in 20 numbers ko ek number mein daba deta hai. Us se pata hi nahi chalta ke kaunsi class kis se confuse ho rahi hai.

Do nataij likhein: (1) isi liye poori confusion matrix report ki jaati hai, sirf error rate nahi; (2) isi liye multi-class Neyman-Pearson metric mein κ ek vector hota hai — kyunki har confusion ki qeemat alag hoti hai (COVID ko cold samajhna, aur cold ko COVID samajhna — dono barabar nahi).

766 marks

Explain the difference between a Type I and a Type II error, and give a scenario in which each is the more costly.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Type I aur Type II error mein farq batayein, aur har aik ke liye ek aisi misaal dein jahan wohi zyada mehngi padti hai.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

TYPE I = FALSE POSITIVE : v-hat = +1 but v = -1 we raised an alarm that was not real TYPE II = FALSE NEGATIVE : v-hat = -1 but v = +1 we missed something that was real

Type I costlier — spam filtering. Marking a legitimate email as spam may lose it entirely; letting one spam through is trivial.

Type II costlier — cancer screening. Missing a real case delays treatment and can be fatal; a false alarm costs only a confirmatory test.

How this enters the model: the asymmetry is encoded by κ in the Neyman-Pearson metric (κ·fn + fp)/n — set κ > 1 when Type II is worse, κ < 1 when Type I is worse, and κ = 1 for the plain error rate.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Type I = false positive — jhoota alarm. Hum ne "haan" kaha, magar tha nahi.
Type II = false negative — chook gaye. Cheez mojood thi, hum ne "nahi" keh diya.

Yaad rakhne ka tareeqa: Type I → ek "I" → ek cheez paida kar di jo nahi thi (false positive). Type II → doosri qism → jo thi usay chhod diya (false negative).

Misaalein:

  • Type I zyada mehnga — spam filter. Zaroori email spam mein chala gaya to gum ho sakta hai. Ek spam inbox mein aa jaaye to sirf delete karna hai.
  • Type II zyada mehnga — cancer screening. Asli case chhoot gaya to ilaaj der se hoga, aur jaan ja sakti hai. Jhoota shaq sirf ek confirmation test maangta hai.

Model mein ye kaise aata hai: κ ke zariye. κ > 1 jab Type II buri ho, κ < 1 jab Type I buri ho, aur κ = 1 par simple error rate.

776 marks

A classifier is evaluated on its training data and achieves 2% error. Can we conclude it will achieve about 2% error on new data? Justify.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Ek classifier ko usi data par naapa gaya jis se wo bana tha, aur 2% error aaya. Kya hum keh sakte hain ke naye data par bhi taqreeban 2% error hoga? Wajah batayein.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

No. Training error is a biased, optimistic estimate of true risk, because the predictor's parameters were chosen to minimise the error on exactly that data. The model may have fitted noise specific to those examples (overfitting), which will not recur in new data.

Correct procedure: evaluate the error rate on held-out (test) data the model never saw during fitting — as the slides require, "we can validate a classifier by evaluating its error rate on unseen or held back (test) data".

Example from the slides: the Iris results (1.33% error) were "trained on all data", so that figure is a training error and not a performance guarantee. By contrast the Australian weather study split 80/20 and reported that train and test results were very similar — which is what justifies trusting its 16%.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: NAHI.

Kyun: kyunki model ke parameters usi data par ghalti kam karne ke liye chune gaye thay. Yaani apna paper khud check kiya — aisa andaza hamesha umeed se acha (optimistic) aata hai. Mumkin hai model ne us data ka shor (noise) bhi yaad kar liya ho (overfitting), jo naye data mein nahi hoga.

Sahi tareeqa: error rate alag rakhe hue (test) data par naapein — jo model ne training mein kabhi dekha hi na ho.

Slides se do misaalein — muqabla karein:
Iris: "trained on all data" — to 1.33% training error hai, zamanat nahi.
Australian weather: 80/20 split kiya, aur likha ke "test aur train nataij taqreeban barabar thay". Isi liye wahan 16% par bharosa kiya ja sakta hai.

786 marks

Two classifiers are compared. A has fp/n = 0.05, fn/n = 0.20; B has fp/n = 0.15, fn/n = 0.08. Which is better? Answer for κ = 1 and for κ = 4.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Do classifiers ka muqabla: A ka fp/n = 0.05, fn/n = 0.20; B ka fp/n = 0.15, fn/n = 0.08. Kaunsa behtar hai? Jawab κ = 1 ke liye aur κ = 4 ke liye — dono surat mein.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

NP = κ(fn/n) + (fp/n) κ = 1: A = 1(0.20) + 0.05 = 0.25 B = 1(0.08) + 0.15 = 0.23 → B better κ = 4: A = 4(0.20) + 0.05 = 0.85 B = 4(0.08) + 0.15 = 0.47 → B better, by far

B is better in both cases, and its advantage grows with κ (0.02 at κ=1, 0.38 at κ=4), because B has the lower false-negative rate and κ weights exactly that.

Key point: "which classifier is better" is not well posed without κ — the ranking can reverse. Here, had A's rates been fn/n = 0.09, fp/n = 0.05, A would win at κ=1 (0.14 vs 0.23) but lose at large κ. Always fix κ before comparing.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: dono surat mein B behtar hai — magar κ barhne par uski bartari bohat barh jaati hai.

κ = 1: A = 0.25, B = 0.23 (farq sirf 0.02) κ = 4: A = 0.85, B = 0.47 (farq poora 0.38)

Kyun: B ka false negative rate kam hai (0.08 vs 0.20), aur κ bilkul usi cheez ko weight deta hai. κ bada karein to B ka faida bada ho jaata hai.

Sab se ahem sabaq: "kaunsa classifier behtar hai" ye sawal κ ke bagair adhoora hai — aur tarteeb palat bhi sakti hai. Agar A ke numbers fn/n = 0.09, fp/n = 0.05 hote, to κ=1 par A jeet jaata (0.14 vs 0.23) magar bade κ par haar jaata. Is liye κ pehle tay karein, muqabla baad mein.

796 marks

In a 3-class problem, class 2 has 60 examples of which 48 are correctly classified, 7 are predicted as class 1 and 5 as class 3. Write the relevant column of C and compute r2/n if n = 180.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Teen classes hain. Class 2 ke 60 examples thay: 48 theek pehchane gaye, 7 ko class 1 kaha gaya aur 5 ko class 3. Confusion matrix ka mutaliq column likhein, aur r2/n nikaalein jab n = 180 ho.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

Column 2 of C (actual class 2): C₁₂ = 7 (class 2 mistaken for 1) C₂₂ = 48 (correct) C₃₂ = 5 (class 2 mistaken for 3) column sum = 60 ✓ matches the 60 examples r₂ = Σj≠2 Cj2 = 7 + 5 = 12 r₂/n = 12/180 = 0.0667 = 6.67%

Note: r2/n is the rate of mistaking class 2, measured against the whole dataset n, not against the 60 class-2 examples. Per-class it would be 12/60 = 20%. The Neyman-Pearson metric uses the /n form, so that the weighted terms sum to the error rate when all κk = 1.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: column 2 = (7, 48, 5), aur r2/n = 12/180 = 6.67%.

Tareeqa: r2 ka matlab hai "class 2 ko kitni baar kisi doosri class samjha" — yaani column 2 ka total minus jo theek thay: 60 − 48 = 12. Ya seedha: 7 + 5 = 12.

Column ka total check karein: 7 + 48 + 5 = 60 — bilkul 60 examples. Agar ye total na mile to kuch ghalat hai.

Dhyan — ye ghalti aam hai: r2 ko n (poora data) se taqseem karte hain, 60 se nahi. Yaani 6.67%, 20% nahi. Wajah: NP metric mein /n hone ki wajah se jab saare κk = 1 hon, to saare terms jama hone par bilkul error rate ban jaata hai. (Agar per-class rate chahiye to wo 12/60 = 20% hai — magar wo doosri cheez hai.)

806 marks

A colleague reports a confusion matrix but does not say whether rows are predictions or actuals. The matrix is [[85, 4], [11, 50]]. How can you determine the convention, and what are fp and fn?

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Ek sathi ne confusion matrix di magar ye nahi bataya ke rows "predicted" hain ya "actual". Matrix hai [[85, 4], [11, 50]]. Aap kaise pata lagayenge ke convention kaunsa hai, aur fp aur fn kya hain?

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

The diagonal is convention-independent: 85 and 50 are the correct predictions (tn and tp) under either reading, since transposing a matrix leaves the diagonal unchanged. Likewise n = 150 and the error rate = 15/150 = 10% are unambiguous.

The off-diagonals cannot be resolved from the numbers alone. Using the lecture's layout C = [[tn, fn], [fp, tp]] gives fn = 4, fp = 11; the transpose gives fn = 11, fp = 4. You must ask, or state your assumption explicitly.

Practical resolution: ask for the class totals. If there were 96 actual negatives, then tn + fp = 96 ⇒ fp = 11, fixing the convention. The lecture itself warns that "some people use the transpose of C" — so always label your rows and columns.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Pehle wo cheezein jo har surat mein nikal aati hain: diagonal (85 aur 50) convention se azad hai — matrix ko palatne (transpose) se diagonal nahi badalta. Is liye:

n = 150 sahi = 85 + 50 = 135 error rate = 15/150 = 10% ← ye yaqeeni hai

Magar fp aur fn numbers se tay nahi ho sakte. Lecture ke layout (C = [[tn, fn],[fp, tp]]) se fn = 4, fp = 11. Palat dein to ulta ho jaayega.

Hal: class totals poochein. Agar asli negatives 96 thay, to 85 + fp = 96, yaani fp = 11 — aur convention tay ho gaya.

Lecture khud ye warning deti hai: "some people use the transpose of C". Is liye exam mein apne matrix par hamesha rows/columns ka label likhein (jaise "rows = predicted") — phir chahe examiner ka layout ulta ho, aap ka jawab saaf aur sahi rahega aur marks nahi katenge.

C2 — sawal 81–88

Embedding aur un-embedding

Sample paper mein nahi aaya, magar numerical sawal banane ke liye sab se aasaan hissa hai — ŷ de kar class poochna, ya embedding chunwana. H11–H18.

816 marks

A 4-class problem uses one-hot embedding. The predictor outputs ŷ = (0.1, 0.6, 0.2, 0.5). What class is predicted, and by what rule?

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Chaar classes hain, one-hot embedding istemaal hui hai. Model ne ŷ = (0.1, 0.6, 0.2, 0.5) diya. Kaunsi class predict hui, aur kis rule se?

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

Rule: nearest-neighbour un-embedding, which for one-hot embedding reduces to argmax:

v-hat = arg min ‖ŷ - ek‖₂ = arg max ŷk k k largest entry = 0.6 at position 2 → v-hat = class 2

Justification: ‖ŷ−ek‖2 = ‖ŷ‖2 + 1 − 2ŷk; the first two terms do not depend on k, so minimising the distance is the same as maximising ŷk. Note the entries need not sum to 1 — these are raw scores, not probabilities, and argmax does not require normalisation.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: class 2 (kyunki sab se badi entry 0.6 hai, position 2 par).

Rule: nearest-neighbour un-embedding — magar one-hot ke saath wo argmax ban jaata hai, yaani "sab se badi entry dekho".

Wajah (ek line): ‖ŷ−ek‖² khol kar dekhein to do terms k par depend nahi karte, aur sirf −2ŷk bachta hai. Usay chhota karna = ŷk ko bada karna. Is liye distance nikaalne ki zaroorat hi nahi.

Dhyan dein: entries ka total 1 nahi hai (0.1+0.6+0.2+0.5 = 1.4) — aur ye theek hai. Ye raw scores hain, probabilities nahi. Argmax ko normalization ki zaroorat nahi hoti.

826 marks

Prove that for one-hot embedding, nearest-neighbour un-embedding is equivalent to arg maxk ŷk.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Saabit karein ke one-hot embedding ke saath nearest-neighbour un-embedding bilkul arg maxk ŷk ke barabar hai.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

‖ŷ - ek‖₂² = ‖ŷ‖₂² - 2 ŷTek + ‖ek‖₂² = ‖ŷ‖₂² - 2 ŷk + 1 Now: ‖ŷ‖₂² does not depend on k ‖ek‖₂² = 1 for every k, so it too is constant ⇒ minimising ‖ŷ - ek‖₂² is minimising (-2ŷk) i.e. MAXIMISING ŷk ⇒ arg min ‖ŷ - ek‖₂ = arg max ŷk QED

The two facts that make it work: ‖ek‖2 = 1 for all k (a unit vector has a single 1), and ŷTek = ŷk (the dot product with ek picks out entry k). State both explicitly.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Saboot teen line ka hai: square ko khol dein, aur dekhein konsa term k par depend karta hai.

‖ŷ - ek‖² = ‖ŷ‖² - 2ŷk + 1 ╵╵╵╵ ╵╵╵╵ ╵ k se azad k par k se depend azad

Natija: pehla aur teesra term har k ke liye wohi hain — unhein nazarandaz kar dein. Bacha −2ŷk, aur usay chhota karne ka matlab ŷk ko bada karna. Yaani distance ka minimum = entry ka maximum.

Do baatein zaroor likhein, inhi ke marks hain:
(1) ‖ek‖² = 1 har k ke liye — kyunki unit vector mein ek hi 1 hota hai, aur 1² = 1.
(2) ŷTek = ŷk — kyunki ek se dot product karne par sirf k-waan entry bachti hai, baaqi 0 se multiply ho kar khatam ho jaati hain.

836 marks

A student embeds V = {APPLE, ORANGE, BANANA} as 1, 2, 3 and fits a linear predictor. Explain why this is a poor choice and what should be used instead.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Ek student ne {APPLE, ORANGE, BANANA} ko 1, 2, 3 mein embed kiya aur linear predictor fit kar diya. Batayein ke ye bura faisla kyun hai, aur us ki jagah kya karna chahiye.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

A single-number embedding imposes an order and a metric that the classes do not have. It asserts that ORANGE lies between APPLE and BANANA, and that APPLE is three times "less" than BANANA — both meaningless. It is therefore not a faithful embedding.

Concretely, nearest-neighbour un-embedding would decode ŷ = 2 as ORANGE, so a predictor hedging between APPLE and BANANA outputs ŷ ≈ 2 and confidently returns the wrong third class.

Use one-hot embedding instead: APPLE → (1,0,0), ORANGE → (0,1,0), BANANA → (0,0,1) — all three representatives are equidistant, so no false ordering is implied.

When a single number is correct: when the classes genuinely are ordered, e.g. YES → 1, MAYBE → 0, NO → −1.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Masla: ek number waali embedding classes par ek aisi tarteeb aur faasla thop deti hai jo asal mein mojood nahi. Ye kehti hai ke:

  • ORANGE, APPLE aur BANANA ke darmiyan hai — bemani baat
  • APPLE, BANANA se "teen guna kam" hai — bilkul bemani

Yaani ye faithful embedding nahi hai.

Amli nuqsan (ye zaroor likhein): socha jaaye model APPLE aur BANANA ke darmiyan shaq mein hai, to wo beech ka jawab ŷ ≈ 2 dega. Aur un-embedding us 2 ko ORANGE padh legi — yaani ek aisi teesri class jo model ne socha hi nahi tha! Poore yaqeen se ghalat jawab.

Hal: one-hot embedding — (1,0,0), (0,1,0), (0,0,1). Teeno representatives aapas mein barabar faasle par hain, to koi jhooti tarteeb nahi banti.

Ek number kab theek hai: jab classes mein waqai tarteeb ho — jaise YES → 1, MAYBE → 0, NO → −1. Wahan "beech waala" ka matlab banta hai.

846 marks

Compare one-hot and reduced one-hot embedding for K classes. State the dimension of each and one advantage of each.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

K classes ke liye one-hot aur reduced one-hot embedding ka muqabla karein. Dono ki dimension batayein aur har aik ka ek faida.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

ONE-HOT : yk = ek ∈ RK dimension m = K REDUCED ONE-HOT : default class → 0 ∈ RK-1 others → e₁,…,eK-1 dimension m = K-1

One-hot advantage: fully symmetric — all classes are treated alike and are equidistant, and un-embedding is the simple argmax.

Reduced one-hot advantage: one fewer dimension, hence fewer parameters. The dropped dimension is redundant anyway, since one-hot entries always sum to one.

Cost of reducing: the default class becomes asymmetric — its region is shaped differently and un-embedding needs an explicit piecewise rule (the ½ thresholds in the YES/MAYBE/NO example) instead of a plain argmax.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Dimensions: one-hot m = K, reduced one-hot m = K−1.

One-hot ka faida: poori tarah symmetric — saari classes barabar, sab aapas mein barabar faasle par, aur un-embedding bas argmax.

Reduced one-hot ka faida: ek dimension kam, yaani parameters kam.

Wo ek dimension kaise bachti hai: kyunki one-hot mein ek dimension bekaar (redundant) hai — entries ka total hamesha 1 hota hai, to pehli K−1 entries se aakhri khud pata chal jaati hai. Reduced one-hot bas usay hata deta hai. (Australian weather slide mein isi ka zikr tha: "16 one-hot values all sum to one".)

Reduced one-hot ki qeemat: default class asymmetric ho jaati hai — uska ilaaqa alag shakal ka hota hai aur un-embedding ke liye poora piecewise rule likhna padta hai (YES/MAYBE/NO misaal mein ½ waale thresholds), argmax se kaam nahi chalta.

856 marks

With the reduced one-hot embedding YES → (1,0), MAYBE → (0,0), NO → (0,1), un-embed the prediction ŷ = (0.3, 0.7). Show your reasoning.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Reduced one-hot embedding hai: YES → (1,0), MAYBE → (0,0), NO → (0,1). Prediction ŷ = (0.3, 0.7) ko un-embed karein, aur apna tareeqa dikhayein.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

By distances:

‖(0.3,0.7) - (1,0)‖² = 0.49 + 0.49 = 0.98 YES ‖(0.3,0.7) - (0,0)‖² = 0.09 + 0.49 = 0.58 MAYBE ‖(0.3,0.7) - (0,1)‖² = 0.09 + 0.09 = 0.18 NO ← smallest

By the lecture's rule: NO requires ŷ2 > ½ and ŷ1 < ŷ2. Here 0.7 > 0.5 ✓ and 0.3 < 0.7 ✓.

Answer: v-hat = NO, by both methods.

Note: the thresholds of ½ are not arbitrary — they are the perpendicular bisectors between the origin and each unit vector, i.e. exactly the nearest-neighbour boundaries.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: NO. Dono tareeqon se wohi jawab aata hai.

Tareeqa 1 — distances nikaalein (teen representatives, teen faasle):

YES se: 0.98 MAYBE se: 0.58 NO se: 0.18 ← sab se chhota, to jawab NO

Tareeqa 2 — lecture ka rule lagayein: NO ke liye do shartein hain — ŷ2 > ½ aur ŷ1 < ŷ2. Dekhein: 0.7 > 0.5 (theek) aur 0.3 < 0.7 (theek). Dono poori → NO.

Wo ½ kahan se aaya: ye andaaze se nahi rakha gaya — ye origin (0,0) aur unit vector ke theek darmiyan hai (perpendicular bisector). Yaani ½ wala rule aur distance wala tareeqa ek hi cheez hain, bas dikhne mein alag. (Is liye dono ka jawab hamesha milega.)

866 marks

Define a Voronoi diagram in the context of un-embedding. Why are the region boundaries straight?

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Un-embedding ke silsile mein Voronoi diagram ki tareef likhein. Aur batayein ke in regions ke kinaare seedhe kyun hote hain?

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

Nearest-neighbour un-embedding partitions Rm into regions

Rk = { ŷ : ⌜ŷ⌟ = k } , k = 1, …, K

i.e. Rk is the set of points closer to yk than to all other representatives. This partition is the Voronoi diagram; the regions are polyhedra, and the boundaries are the perpendicular bisectors between pairs of representatives.

Why straight: comparing ‖ŷ−yj‖2 with ‖ŷ−yk‖2, the ‖ŷ‖2 terms cancel, leaving

2(yk - yj)Tŷ - ‖yk‖² + ‖yj‖² = 0

which is affine (linear) in ŷ — a hyperplane. Had the quadratic term not cancelled, the boundary would be curved.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Tareef: nearest-neighbour un-embedding poore space ko K tukdon mein baant deti hai — Rk mein wo saare points hain jahan jawab "class k" aata hai. Is taqseem ka naam Voronoi diagram hai. Regions polyhedra hote hain aur kinaare representatives ke jodon ke perpendicular bisectors.

Kinaare seedhe kyun hain — asal nuktah: jab do faasle ka muqabla karte hain (‖ŷ−yj‖² vs ‖ŷ−yk‖²), to ‖ŷ‖² dono taraf se kat jaata hai. Aur jo bacha, wo ŷ mein linear hai — yaani hyperplane.

Ye sochne layaq baat hai: agar ‖ŷ‖² na kaatta, to boundary quadratic (tedhi) hoti. Yaani "sab se qareeb kaunsa hai" jaisa tedha sawal asal mein ek seedhi line ka sawal hai — aur isi liye classifier ka decision boundary aksar seedha hota hai. Jab seedhi boundary kaafi na ho, to nonlinear features ya hidden layer ki zaroorat padti hai.

876 marks

State the three-stage structure of a classifier built by embedding, and write the final classifier as a composition of functions.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Embedding ke zariye bane classifier ke teen hisse batayein, aur aakhri classifier ko functions ke composition ki shakal mein likhein.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

1. EMBED x = φ(u) raw input → feature vector (and y = ψ(v) for the output in training) 2. PREDICT ŷ = gθ(x) g : Rd → Rm, chosen by (R)ERM 3. UN-EMBED v-hat = ⌜ŷ⌟ nearest-neighbour rule, ⌜·⌟ : Rm → V Final classifier: v-hat = fθ(u) = ⌜ gθ( φ(u) ) ⌟

Why the detour is needed: the predictor g can only output numbers, but v is a category. Embedding turns the category into a vector so a numerical predictor can be fitted; un-embedding converts the numbers back. Read the composition inside out: φ first, ⌜·⌟ last.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Teen hisse:

  1. EMBED — x = φ(u): raw input ko numbers ki list banao. (Aur training mein output ko bhi: y = ψ(v).)
  2. PREDICT — ŷ = gθ(x): numbers se numbers ka andaza lagao. θ ko ERM/RERM se chunte hain.
  3. UN-EMBED — v-hat = ⌜ŷ⌟: numbers ko wapas class banao (nearest-neighbour rule se).

Poora classifier: v-hat = ⌜ gθ(φ(u)) ⌟

Padhne ka tareeqa: ANDAR se BAHAR. Sab se andar φ hai, to wo pehle chalta hai; ⌜·⌟ sab se bahar, to wo aakhir mein.

Ye ghoom kar kyun jaana padta hai: kyunki predictor g sirf numbers nikaal sakta hai, aur v ek qism hai. Model kabhi "SPAM" nahi likh sakta. To qism ko number banaya, number ka andaza lagaya, aur number ko wapas qism bana diya. Beech ka poora hissa model ki duniya hai — wahan sirf numbers hain.

886 marks

For Boolean classification with TRUE → +1, FALSE → −1, derive the un-embedding rule and explain why the decision boundary is ŷ = 0.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Boolean classification mein TRUE → +1, FALSE → −1. Un-embedding ka rule nikaalein, aur batayein ke decision boundary ŷ = 0 par kyun hoti hai.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

Nearest-neighbour un-embedding compares |ŷ − 1| with |ŷ + 1|. These are equal exactly at the midpoint of +1 and −1, which is 0. Hence

v-hat = TRUE, ŷ ≥ 0 FALSE, ŷ < 0 i.e. v-hat = sign(ŷ)

The boundary is ŷ = 0 because it is the perpendicular bisector of the two representatives — the set of points equidistant from +1 and −1.

Two consequences worth stating: (i) un-embedding uses only the sign of ŷ, not its magnitude, so −0.01 and −300 decode identically; (ii) the magnitude is used by the loss instead, which is why margin = v · ŷ is the natural quantity — its sign gives correctness and its size gives confidence.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Rule: ŷ ≥ 0 → TRUE, ŷ < 0 → FALSE. Yaani bas sign(ŷ).

Derivation (ek line): +1 aur −1 ke theek darmiyan ka nuqta 0 hai. ŷ is se oopar ho to +1 ke qareeb, neeche ho to −1 ke qareeb. Is liye boundary ŷ = 0 par hai — wo dono representatives se barabar faasle par hai (perpendicular bisector).

Do ahem nataij (ye likhein):
(1) Un-embedding sirf nishaan (sign) dekhti hai, size bilkul nahi — −0.01 aur −300 ka jawab bilkul ek hai (dono FALSE).
(2) To size ka istemaal kaun karta hai? Loss function (Lecture-8) — aur wahan −0.01 aur −300 mein zameen aasmaan ka farq hai. Isi liye margin = v · ŷ itni kaam ki cheez hai: uska nishaan batata hai theek ya ghalat, aur uski size batati hai kitne yaqeen se.

C3 — sawal 89–104

Classifier theory — har mumkin sawal

Paper mein is section se sirf 1 sawal aata hai, aur wo short theoretical hota hai. Magar wo kaunsa hoga, ye pata nahi — is liye yahan Lecture-7 ka poora mazmoon chhote chhote sawalon mein tora gaya hai. Har jawab 3–4 line ka hai, kyunki paper mein utni hi jagah milti hai.

Ye poora section 10 minute mein revise karein

CLASSIFIER = predictor jahan output categorical ho, V = {1,…,K} K = 2 → Boolean | K > 2 → multi-class CHAAR NATAIJ tp, tn = theek | fp = Type I fn = Type II CONFUSION MATRIX error rate = 1 - trace/n RECALL = tp/P (jo thay, kitne pakde) PRECISION = tp/(tp+fp) (jo kaha, kitne sach) NP METRIC = (κ·fn + fp)/n κ = 1 → error rate PIPELINE EMBED → PREDICT → UN-EMBED UN-EMBED Boolean: sign(ŷ) one-hot: argmax ŷk VORONOI regions polyhedra; boundaries = perpendicular bisectors

896 marks

Define a classifier. When is it called Boolean and when multi-class? Give one example of each.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Classifier ki tareef likhein. Usay Boolean kab kehte hain aur multi-class kab? Dono ki ek ek misaal dein.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

Predicting a categorical raw output v ∈ V from a raw input u ∈ U is called classification, and the predictor f : U → V is a classifier, where V = {1, …, K} is a finite label set.

K = 2 → BOOLEAN e.g. V = {SPAM, HAM} K > 2 → MULTI-CLASS e.g. V = {COVID19, FLU, COLD}

A classifier effectively partitions all of U into K regions, one per predicted label.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Tareef: jab output ek qism (category) ho, number nahi, to usay classification kehte hain, aur predictor f : U → V ko classifier. V ek mehdood set hota hai.

K = 2 → Boolean (spam / ham). K > 2 → multi-class (COVID / flu / cold).

Ek line aur likhein: classifier asal mein poore input space ko K tukdon mein baant deta hai — har tukde ka apna label.

906 marks

Why is the error rate alone a misleading performance metric? Illustrate with a short example.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Sirf error rate dekhna gumrah kun kyun hai? Ek chhoti misaal se samjhayein.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

Because it weights both kinds of error equally and is dominated by the majority class when the data is imbalanced.

1000 patients, 10 have a disease. Classifier predicts "no disease" for everyone: error rate = 10/1000 = 1% ("99% accurate") recall = 0/10 = 0% (catches NOTHING)

Fix: report recall alongside, and use the Neyman-Pearson metric (κ·fn + fp)/n with κ > 1 so that missed positives are properly penalised. Always compare against the majority-class baseline.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Kyun: error rate dono qism ki ghalti ko barabar ginta hai, aur imbalanced data mein majority class us par haavi ho jaati hai.

Misaal: 1000 mein se 10 ko bimari. "Kisi ko bimari nahi" kehne wala classifier → error 1% ("99% accurate"), magar recall 0% — ek bhi marz nahi pakda.

Ilaaj: saath recall report karein, aur NP metric (κ > 1) istemaal karein. Aur accuracy ko hamesha baseline se mila kar dekhein.

916 marks

Define precision and recall. Give one scenario where recall matters more and one where precision matters more.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Precision aur recall ki tareef likhein. Ek misaal jahan recall zyada ahem ho, aur ek jahan precision.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

RECALL = tp/P = tp/(tp+fn) of all ACTUAL positives, how many found? PRECISION = tp/(tp+fp) of all PREDICTED positives, how many right?

They differ only in the denominator: recall divides by what truly is positive, precision by what we called positive.

Recall matters more — cancer screening: a miss can be fatal, a false alarm costs only a follow-up test. Precision matters more — spam filtering: one spam in the inbox is trivial, but a real email lost to the spam folder is costly.

They generally trade off: predicting positive more freely raises recall and lowers precision.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Farq sirf neeche waale (denominator) ka hai: recall tp/P (jo asal mein thay), precision tp/(tp+fp) (jo hum ne kahe).

Machhli ki misaal: 100 machhliyan, aap ne 30 cheezein nikaleen — 24 machhli, 6 joote. Recall = 24/100 = 24%; precision = 24/30 = 80%.

Recall ahem — cancer test (chhoot gaya to jaan ka khatra). Precision ahem — spam filter (zaroori email gum ho gaya to nuqsan). Aur dono ek saath nahi barhte.

926 marksStanford HW6 Q2(a)

What is the difference between a Type I and a Type II error? How does the model account for their differing costs?

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Type I aur Type II error mein kya farq hai? Model un ki mukhtalif qeematon ka hisaab kaise rakhta hai?

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

TYPE I = FALSE POSITIVE : ŷ = +1 but v = -1 (false alarm) TYPE II = FALSE NEGATIVE : ŷ = -1 but v = +1 (a miss)

The asymmetry is encoded by κ in the Neyman-Pearson metric (κ·fn + fp)/n: set κ > 1 when misses are worse, κ < 1 when false alarms are worse, κ = 1 for the plain error rate.

In training the same κ enters by scaling the loss of positive examples: ℓ(ŷ,+1) = κ·p(−ŷ). Use the same κ in training and validation.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Type I = false positive (jhoota alarm) — hum ne "haan" kaha, tha nahi.
Type II = false negative (chook) — cheez thi, hum ne "nahi" kaha.

Qeemat ka hisaab κ se: κ > 1 jab chookna bura ho, κ < 1 jab jhoota alarm bura ho, κ = 1 par simple error rate.

Do jagah aata hai: validation mein NP metric ke tor par, aur training mein positive class ka loss κ se multiply kar ke. Dono jagah wohi κ rakhein.

936 marks

What is a confusion matrix? Explain what the diagonal, off-diagonal and column sums represent, and how to get the error rate from it.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Confusion matrix kya hai? Diagonal, off-diagonal aur column sums ka matlab batayein, aur us se error rate kaise nikaalte hain.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

Cjk = number of records with prediction j and true class k (lecture convention: rows = predicted, columns = actual).

DIAGONAL Ckk = correct predictions for class k OFF-DIAGONAL Cjk = how often k was mistaken for j (j ≠ k) COLUMN SUM = number of records truly in class k ALL ENTRIES SUM = n error rate = 1 - (trace of C)/n

Warning: some texts use the transpose (rows = actual). Always label your rows and columns — the diagonal and the error rate are unaffected, but precision and recall swap.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Cjk = kitne records mein prediction j thi aur asli class k.

Diagonal = sahi predictions. Off-diagonal = "k ko j samajh liya". Column sum = us class ke asli records. Sab ka total = n.

Error rate ka tez tareeqa: 1 − trace/n — off-diagonal ke saare numbers jama karne ki zaroorat nahi.

Warning: kuch kitabein transpose istemaal karti hain. Hamesha rows/columns par label likhein — diagonal aur error rate to wohi rehte hain, magar precision aur recall aapas mein badal jaate hain.

946 marksStanford HW6 Q2(a)

Define the Neyman-Pearson metric for Boolean and for multi-class classification. What does κ mean, and what happens when all κ equal 1?

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Neyman-Pearson metric ki tareef likhein — Boolean aur multi-class dono ke liye. κ ka matlab kya hai, aur jab saare κ 1 hon to kya hota hai?

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

BOOLEAN : NP = (κ · fn + fp)/n κ is a NUMBER MULTI-CLASS : NP = Σk κk rk/n κ is a VECTOR rk = times class k was mistaken for another class All κ = 1 → NP = the overall ERROR RATE

κ expresses how much we care about one kind of error relative to the other — in Boolean, false negatives relative to false positives; in multi-class, κk is how much we care about mistaking class k.

It exists because we have two objectives but model selection needs one number — a scalarization of the two error rates.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Boolean: NP = (κ·fn + fp)/n — κ ek number.
Multi-class: NP = Σk κk rk/n — κ ek vector.

Saare κ = 1 → NP bilkul error rate ban jaata hai. Ye line har NP waale sawal mein likhein.

Kyun banaya gaya: hamare paas do maqsad hain magar model chunne ke liye ek number chahiye. Is kaam ka naam scalarization hai.

956 marks

Why must a categorical output be embedded at all? Describe the three-stage structure of a classifier built this way.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Categorical output ko embed karne ki zaroorat hi kyun padti hai? Aur is tareeqe se bane classifier ke teen hisse batayein.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

Because predictors (linear models, neural networks) can only output numbers, but v is a category — a model cannot output the word "SPAM". So we convert the category to a vector, predict numerically, and convert back.

1. EMBED x = φ(u), y = ψ(v) category → vector 2. PREDICT ŷ = gθ(x) numbers → numbers 3. UN-EMBED v-hat = ⌜ŷ⌟ vector → category Classifier: v-hat = ⌜ gθ(φ(u)) ⌟

yk = ψ(k) is the representative of class k; the set of all representatives is the constellation. Read the composition inside out.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Kyun: model sirf numbers nikaal sakta hai, aur v ek qism hai — model "SPAM" likh hi nahi sakta. To qism ko number banaya, andaza lagaya, aur number ko wapas qism banaya.

Teen hisse: EMBED → PREDICT → UN-EMBED, yaani v-hat = ⌜gθ(φ(u))⌟.

Do naam yaad rakhein: yk = class k ka representative (uska "pata"); saare pataon ka majmua = constellation.

966 marks

Compare one-hot and reduced one-hot embedding: dimensions, one advantage of each, and the cost of reducing.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

One-hot aur reduced one-hot ka muqabla: dimension, har aik ka ek faida, aur kam karne ki qeemat kya hai.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

ONE-HOT yk = ek ∈ RK m = K REDUCED ONE-HOT default class → 0 ∈ RK-1 m = K-1

One-hot advantage: fully symmetric — all classes equidistant, and un-embedding is a simple argmax.
Reduced advantage: one fewer dimension (fewer parameters); the dropped dimension is redundant anyway, since one-hot entries always sum to one.

Cost of reducing: the default class becomes asymmetric — its region has a different shape and un-embedding needs an explicit piecewise rule (the ½ thresholds) instead of argmax.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Dimension: one-hot m = K, reduced m = K−1.

One-hot ka faida: poori tarah symmetric, aur un-embedding bas argmax.
Reduced ka faida: ek dimension kam — aur wo dimension waise bhi bekaar (redundant) thi, kyunki one-hot entries ka total hamesha 1 hota hai.

Qeemat: default class asymmetric ho jaati hai — uska ilaaqa alag shakal ka, aur un-embedding ke liye poora piecewise rule likhna padta hai.

976 marks

Why is it wrong to embed {APPLE, ORANGE, BANANA} as 1, 2, 3? When is a single-number embedding appropriate?

Sawal aam alfaz mein

{APPLE, ORANGE, BANANA} ko 1, 2, 3 mein embed karna ghalat kyun hai? Aur ek-number waali embedding kab theek hoti hai?

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

It imposes an order and a distance the classes do not have — it asserts ORANGE lies between APPLE and BANANA. It is not a faithful embedding.

Concretely: a model hedging between APPLE and BANANA outputs ŷ ≈ 2, and nearest-neighbour un-embedding decodes that as ORANGE — a confident, completely wrong third class.

Use one-hot instead: (1,0,0), (0,1,0), (0,0,1) — all three equidistant, no false ordering.

Single-number is fine when the classes really are ordered, e.g. YES → 1, MAYBE → 0, NO → −1, where "in between" is meaningful.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Kyun ghalat: ye classes par aisi tarteeb aur faasla thop deta hai jo mojood nahi — keh raha hai ORANGE, APPLE aur BANANA ke darmiyan hai.

Amli nuqsan (ye zaroor likhein): agar model APPLE aur BANANA ke beech shaq mein ho to wo ŷ ≈ 2 dega — aur un-embedding usay ORANGE padh legi. Yaani ek aisi teesri class jo model ne socha hi nahi tha.

Hal: one-hot — teeno barabar faasle par. Ek number kab theek: jab classes mein waqai tarteeb ho, jaise YES / MAYBE / NO.

986 marks

State the nearest-neighbour un-embedding rule, and what it simplifies to for (a) the Boolean ±1 embedding and (b) one-hot embedding.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Nearest-neighbour un-embedding ka rule likhein, aur batayein ke wo (a) Boolean ±1 embedding aur (b) one-hot embedding ke saath kis saadi shakal mein badal jaata hai.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

GENERAL : v-hat = arg mink ‖ŷ - yk‖₂ (choose the class whose representative is nearest) (a) BOOLEAN ±1 : v-hat = sign(ŷ) boundary at ŷ = 0 (b) ONE-HOT : v-hat = arg maxk ŷk

Why (b): ‖ŷ−ek‖² = ‖ŷ‖² + 1 − 2ŷk; the first two terms don't depend on k, so minimising distance = maximising ŷk.

Note: un-embedding uses only the sign (or the ranking), never the magnitude. Magnitude is used by the loss instead — which is why margin = v·ŷ is the natural quantity.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Aam rule: v-hat = arg mink ‖ŷ − yk‖ — "jo representative sab se qareeb ho, wohi class".

(a) Boolean ±1: bas sign(ŷ), boundary ŷ = 0 par (kyunki +1 aur −1 ka darmiyan 0 hai).
(b) One-hot: bas argmax — sab se badi entry.

(b) ki wajah ek line mein: square khol dein to do terms k par depend nahi karte; sirf −2ŷk bachta hai, aur usay chhota karna = ŷk bada karna.

996 marks

What is a Voronoi diagram in this context? Why are its region boundaries straight?

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Is context mein Voronoi diagram kya hai? Aur uske kinaare seedhe kyun hote hain?

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

Nearest-neighbour un-embedding partitions Rm into regions Rk = {ŷ : ⌜ŷ⌟ = k} — the points nearest to representative yk. This partition is the Voronoi diagram; the regions are polyhedra and the boundaries are the perpendicular bisectors between pairs of representatives.

Comparing ‖ŷ-yj‖² with ‖ŷ-yk‖², the ‖ŷ‖² terms CANCEL, leaving 2(yk-yj)Tŷ - ‖yk‖² + ‖yj‖² = 0 → AFFINE in ŷ

The cancellation of the quadratic term is exactly why the boundary is a hyperplane and not a curve.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Tareef: nearest-neighbour un-embedding poore space ko K tukdon mein baant deti hai — Rk mein wo points jo yk ke sab se qareeb hain. Is taqseem ka naam Voronoi diagram hai; regions polyhedra, aur kinaare perpendicular bisectors.

Seedhe kyun: do faasle compare karte waqt ‖ŷ‖² dono taraf se kat jaata hai, aur jo bacha wo ŷ mein linear hai — yaani hyperplane.

Agar ‖ŷ‖² na kaatta to boundary tedhi (quadratic) hoti. Yehi ek kaatna poori geometry tay kar deta hai.

1006 marks

Why is square loss a poor choice for classification, and what property should a good classification loss have?

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Classification ke liye square loss bura intikhab kyun hai, aur ache classification loss mein kaunsi khoobi honi chahiye?

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

Square loss is not monotonically non-increasing in the margin. It penalises deviation from the representative in both directions, so a confident correct prediction is punished:

ℓ(-3, -1) = (1-3)² = 4.0 (confidently CORRECT) ℓ(+1, -1) = (1+1)² = 4.0 (clearly WRONG) -- SAME loss!

During ERM this drags correct predictions back toward the boundary.

A good classification loss must be non-increasing in the margin v·ŷ — flat or zero once the point is correctly classified with enough margin. Hinge and logistic satisfy this.

In fairness: square loss is still used because ERM with it reduces to a least-squares problem solvable exactly — computationally easiest, but geometrically wrong.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Kyun bura: square loss ki shakal pyaali hai — wo dono taraf saza deta hai. To ŷ = −3 (yaqeen se sahi) aur ŷ = +1 (bilkul ghalat) — dono ka loss 4.0!

Amli nuqsan: training mein model apni sahi predictions ko boundary ki taraf wapas kheench leta hai.

Achi khoobi: loss margin barhne par kabhi barhna nahi chahiye — ek had ke baad chapta ya zero. Hinge aur logistic dono theek hain.

Insaaf ki line: square loss phir bhi istemaal hota hai kyunki ERM least-squares ban jaata hai aur formula se seedha hal ho jaata hai. Yaani hisaab mein behtareen, geometry mein ghalat.

1016 marksStanford HW6 Q2(b,c)

Name the loss function and regularizer used by the least squares classifier, logistic regression and the support vector machine.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Least squares classifier, logistic regression aur SVM — teeno ka loss function aur regularizer batayein.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

Least squares classifier : SQUARE loss + square regularizer Logistic regression : LOGISTIC loss + any regularizer Support vector machine : HINGE loss + square regularizer

Note: for logistic regression the regularizer is not part of the name ("logistic regression with ℓ1 regularizer"), whereas for the SVM square regularization is part of the definition. Also: despite its name, logistic regression is a classification method.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Teen jore — rata laga lein:

SQUARE + square reg = LEAST SQUARES CLASSIFIER LOGISTIC + koi bhi reg = LOGISTIC REGRESSION HINGE + square reg = SVM

Do baatein: (1) sawal dono taraf se aa sakta hai — "SVM kaunsa loss?" ya "hinge+square kis ka naam?". (2) Naam mein "regression" hone ke bawajood logistic regression classification ka tareeqa hai.

1026 marks

A model reports 2% error on the data it was trained on. Can we conclude it will achieve about 2% on new data? Justify.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Ek model ne usi data par 2% error diya jis se wo bana tha. Kya hum keh sakte hain ke naye data par bhi 2% hoga? Wajah batayein.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

No. Training error is a biased, optimistic estimate of true risk, because the parameters were chosen to minimise the error on exactly that data. The model may have fitted noise specific to those examples (overfitting), which will not recur.

Correct procedure: evaluate the error rate on held-out test data never seen during fitting.

Example: the Iris results (1.33% error) were "trained on all data", so that is a training error, not a guarantee. The Australian weather study instead used an 80/20 split and reported train and test results were very similar — which is what justifies trusting its 16%.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: NAHI.

Kyun: parameters usi data par ghalti kam karne ke liye chune gaye thay — yaani apna paper khud check kiya. Aisa andaza hamesha umeed se acha aata hai. Model ne us data ka shor bhi yaad kar liya ho sakta hai (overfitting).

Do misaalein muqable mein: Iris ka 1.33% "trained on all data" tha — yaani training error, zamanat nahi. Australian weather mein 80/20 split kiya gaya aur likha gaya ke train/test nataij barabar thay — isi liye wahan 16% par bharosa hai.

1036 marks

For K classes, how many entries does the confusion matrix have, how many are correct predictions, and how many distinct error types exist? Why does this motivate a weight vector κ?

Sawal aam alfaz mein

K classes ke liye: confusion matrix mein kitne khaane, kitne sahi, aur kitni alag qism ki ghaltiyan? Aur is se weight vector κ ki zaroorat kaise saabit hoti hai?

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

entries = K² correct = K (the diagonal) error types = K² - K = K(K-1) e.g. K = 5 : 25 entries, 5 correct, 20 distinct error types

Why κ must be a vector: different confusions carry very different costs. Mistaking COVID for a cold is far worse than mistaking a cold for COVID, yet a single error rate collapses all K(K−1) error types into one number and hides this.

Hence the full confusion matrix is reported, and the multi-class NP metric weights each class with its own κk.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: K² khaane, K sahi (diagonal), K(K−1) qism ki ghaltiyan. K = 5 par: 25, 5, 20.

κ vector kyun chahiye: har ghalti ki qeemat alag hai. COVID ko cold samajhna, aur cold ko COVID samajhna — dono barabar nahi. Magar ek error rate in saari K(K−1) qismon ko ek number mein daba deta hai aur ye farq chhupa leta hai.

Is liye: poori confusion matrix report ki jaati hai, aur multi-class NP metric mein har class ka apna κk hota hai.

1046 marks

Define specificity, false alarm rate and true positive rate in terms of the confusion matrix entries, and state which pairs are the same quantity under different names.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Specificity, false alarm rate aur true positive rate ko confusion matrix ki entries ki shakal mein likhein, aur batayein kaunse naam asal mein ek hi cheez hain.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

N = tn + fp (actual negatives) P = fn + tp (actual positives) true positive rate = tp/P also called SENSITIVITY and RECALL false alarm rate = fp/N same as the FALSE POSITIVE RATE specificity = tn/N also called TRUE NEGATIVE RATE precision = tp/(tp+fp)

Same quantity, different names: TPR = sensitivity = recall; false alarm rate = false positive rate; specificity = true negative rate.

Useful identity: specificity + false alarm rate = 1, since tn + fp = N. Likewise recall + false negative rate = 1.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Pehle N aur P nikaalein — warna denominator ghalat lagta hai.

tp/P = TPR = sensitivity = RECALL (teeno ek hi cheez) fp/N = false alarm rate = FPR (dono ek hi cheez) tn/N = specificity = TNR (dono ek hi cheez) tp/(tp+fp) = precision

Do kaam ki identities: specificity + false alarm rate = 1 aur recall + false negative rate = 1 — kyunki dono ek hi total (N ya P) par taqseem hote hain. Is se jawab check kiya ja sakta hai.

C4 — sawal 105–108

Stanford ke asli final exam se

Ye chaar sawal Stanford EE104 ke asli final exam (Spring 2022, S. Lall & S. Boyd) ke Problem 2 — "Titanic survivors" se liye gaye hain. Numbers asli exam ke official solution se hain, banaye hue nahi. Stanford mein ye sawal Julia code likh kar hal hota tha; yahan matrix pehle se de kar haath se hisaab karwaya gaya hai — jaisa aap ke paper mein aayega.

Setup — chaaron sawalon ke liye yehi

Titanic ke musafiron ka data. Target v: 1 = died, 2 = survived. Confusion matrix mein rows = predicted, columns = actual (lecture waala convention). Test set mein 178 musafir hain — 98 died, 80 survived. Hum "survived" ko positive class maan rahe hain.

1056 marksStanford Final 2022 Q2(a)

A simple predictor uses gender alone: G(u) = survived if the passenger is female, died otherwise. On the test set its confusion matrix is C = [[83, 23], [15, 57]]. Compute the error rate, and the recall and precision for the "survived" class.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Ek bohat saada predictor: agar aurat hai to "survived", warna "died" — bas itna, koi training nahi. Test set par uski confusion matrix di gayi hai. Error rate nikaalein, aur "survived" class ka recall aur precision.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

actual died survived pred died [ 83 23 ] row sum 106 pred surv [ 15 57 ] row sum 72 col sums 98 80 n = 178 tn = 83 fn = 23 fp = 15 tp = 57 N = 98 (died) P = 80 (survived) error rate = (fn+fp)/n = (23+15)/178 = 38/178 = 0.2135 = 21.35% recall = tp/P = 57/80 = 71.25% precision = tp/(tp+fp) = 57/72 = 79.17%

Worth adding: with no training at all, a one-line rule already reaches 78.65% accuracy and catches 71% of survivors — because "women and children first" really was applied. This is the baseline any trained model must beat.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: error rate 21.35%, recall 71.25%, precision 79.17%.

Pehla qadam — row aur column sums nikaalein: columns = asli ginti (98 died, 80 survived), aur total 178. Ye check zaroor karein, warna denominator ghalat lag jaata hai.

recall = 57/80 ← neeche: jo ASAL mein bach gaye precision = 57/72 ← neeche: jin ko HUM NE "bach gaya" kaha

Ghaur karein: is predictor ne koi training nahi ki — bas "aurat = bach gayi". Phir bhi 78.65% accuracy aur 71% survivors pakad liye! Wajah: Titanic par waqai "women and children first" ka usool laga tha. Yehi "baseline" hai — koi bhi trained model is se behtar hona chahiye, warna uska koi faida nahi.

1066 marksStanford Final 2022 Q2(b,c)

A second simple predictor uses passenger class alone: G(u) = survived if class < 3, died otherwise. Its test confusion matrix is C = [[72, 26], [26, 54]]. Compute its error rate and state which of the two simple predictors is better.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Doosra saada predictor: agar class 3 se kam hai to "survived", warna "died". Uski confusion matrix di hai. Error rate nikaalein aur batayein ke dono saade predictors mein se kaunsa behtar hai.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

class-based: tn=72 fn=26 fp=26 tp=54 n = 178 errors = 26 + 26 = 52 error rate = 52/178 = 0.2921 = 29.21% recall = 54/80 = 67.50% precision = 54/80 = 67.50% gender-based (from Q105): errors = 38, error rate = 21.35% 38 < 52 → the GENDER-based predictor is better

Note: for the class-based predictor precision and recall are equal (both 54/80) — a coincidence of fp = fn = 26, i.e. its two error types exactly balance. It is unbiased between the classes but simply less accurate: gender carries more information about survival than ticket class did.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: error rate 29.21%; gender waala predictor behtar hai (38 ghaltiyan vs 52).

Dilchasp baat: is predictor ki precision aur recall bilkul barabar hain (dono 54/80 = 67.5%). Wajah: fp = fn = 26 — yaani uski dono qism ki ghaltiyan barabar hain.

Matlab: class-based predictor kisi taraf jhuka hua nahi (unbiased), magar kam durust hai. Nateeja: gender mein bachne ke baare mein zyada maloomat thi, banisbat ticket class ke. Ye line likhne par interpretation ke marks milte hain.

1076 marksStanford Final 2022 Q2(d)

A classifier is now trained with logistic loss and an L1 regularizer (λ = 0.01). Its confusion matrices are Ctrain = [[392, 83], [59, 177]] and Ctest = [[86, 22], [12, 58]]. Compute both error rates and state whether the model is overfitting.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Ab ek asli trained model hai — logistic loss aur L1 regularizer ke saath. Training aur test, dono ki confusion matrices di hain. Dono ka error rate nikaalein aur batayein ke model overfit kar raha hai ya nahi.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

TRAIN: tn=392 fn=83 fp=59 tp=177 n = 711 errors = 83 + 59 = 142 error rate = 142/711 = 0.1997 = 19.97% TEST: tn=86 fn=22 fp=12 tp=58 n = 178 errors = 22 + 12 = 34 error rate = 34/178 = 0.1910 = 19.10% recall = 58/80 = 72.50% precision = 58/70 = 82.86%

Not overfitting. The test error (19.10%) is essentially the same as — in fact slightly lower than — the training error (19.97%). A model that had memorised the training data would show training error far below test error.

Why: the L1 regularizer is doing its job — it keeps θ small and sparse, so the model cannot chase noise in the training set. The small gap in the test set's favour is just sampling variation on 178 points, not a real effect.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: train 19.97%, test 19.10%. Overfitting NAHI ho rahi.

Kaise pehchana: overfitting ki nishani ye hoti hai ke training error bohat kam aur test error bohat zyada ho. Yahan dono taqreeban barabar hain — balki test thora behtar hai.

Wajah: L1 regularizer apna kaam kar raha hai — wo θ ko chhota aur sparse rakhta hai, to model training data ka shor yaad nahi kar pata.

Test ka train se behtar hona ajeeb kyun nahi: test set mein sirf 178 points hain, to thora sa utaar charhaav normal hai. Ye koi asli faida nahi, bas sampling variation hai. Ye baat likhne par poore marks milte hain — log aksar is par ghalat natija nikaal lete hain.

1086 marksStanford Final 2022 Q2 — tajziya

The trained logistic + L1 model achieves 19.10% test error, while the one-line gender rule achieves 21.35%. Was the machine learning worth it? Justify your answer.

Sawal aam alfaz mein

Trained model (logistic + L1) ka test error 19.10% hai, aur ek line waale gender rule ka 21.35%. To kya poori machine learning karne ka faida hua? Apne jawab ki wajah batayein.

Jawab

English — paper par likhein

gender rule (no training) : 21.35% error logistic + L1 (trained) : 19.10% error ------ improvement : 2.25 percentage points (38 errors → 34 errors, i.e. 4 passengers)

The honest answer: barely. A full pipeline — one-hot embedding, an engineered alone feature, standardization, logistic loss, L1 regularization, tuning λ — buys only 2.25 points, which on 178 test points is 4 passengers. That difference is within the sampling noise of a test set this size.

Two conclusions:

  • Always report the baseline. "81% accurate" sounds strong until you learn a one-line rule already gets 78.65%.
  • The gain may still be worth it where it is cheap and the recall matters (72.50% vs 71.25%), but the model should not be presented as a large improvement.

Same lesson as the Australian weather study: 16% model error against a 22% "always predict no rain" baseline — the real contribution was 6 points, not "84% accuracy".

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Imaandar jawab: bohat kam faida hua.

gender rule (bina training) : 21.35% trained model : 19.10% ------ faida : 2.25 percentage points (38 ghaltiyan se 34 — yaani sirf 4 musafir)

Poora pipeline — one-hot embedding, naya alone feature banana, standardization, logistic loss, L1 regularization, λ tune karna — aur natija sirf 4 musafir behtar. Aur 178 points par itna farq shor (noise) ke andar hi hai.

Do nataij:

  • Baseline hamesha report karein. "81% accurate" sun kar model zabardast lagta hai — jab tak pata na chale ke ek line ka rule pehle hi 78.65% de raha tha.
  • Faida phir bhi lia ja sakta hai (recall 71.25% se 72.50% hua), magar usay "bohat bada behtar model" keh kar pesh karna ghalat hoga.

Bilkul wohi sabaq jo Australian weather study mein tha (I12): model 16%, aur "hamesha baarish nahi" kehne wala baseline 22% — asli faida sirf 6 points. Accuracy ka number akela hamesha bemani hai.
